- **Checkpoint D review 3 (C3-03) — the invariant table assigned the terminal guarantee two incompatible tiers.** Applied as decided: **I-6's tier becomes `B for every call the kernel sequences, over a tier-C participant obligation (I-36)`** with the global "no callback fires afterwards" clause retained verbatim; **I-36 stays tier C** and gains the indirect-invocation clause; the provenance preamble, a tier-legend paragraph naming the combined-rating idiom and its three relations, 01 §Teardown's closing clause, F-47's citation, ledger L-12 and the Phase 18 deliverable all record the pairing. **No production source or test change follows from it** — all 24 source citations of I-6/I-36 were verified to assert no tier.
- **Checkpoint D review 3 (C3-04) — five factual overclaims in public and evidence prose.** The README, `requireFinite`'s comment and the options-suite header no longer say all four option domains validate at construction — a `landing({ duration })` thunk validates per landing and `landing({ run })` suppresses the domain outright, both already pinned by the suite. Contract 03's "M-3 recorded complete at +0.81 kB against a 9.90 kB minimal" corrected to **+0.76 against 9.33** (+0.77/9.34 after m3's own re-measure note); both figures were wrong, not one. C2-01's §7 cost table is **annotated as a prototype forecast** rather than rewritten, since it is a historical record — landed +30–90 B, `layoutAnimation` 10.51 kB. The ledger's negative-assertion claim is **narrowed to `sortable.js`**, the entry each of the four names was dropped from, _and_ `AnimationTiming` — the only one of the four with a plausible second home, having dissolved into option types that live on their own entries — gains assertions on `sortable/landing.js` and `sortable/layout-animation.js`. The full 4×9 cross-product was rejected as noise that asserts nothing anyone would do.
- **C3-01's cost, measured.** minimal 10.07 → **10.08 kB**, `minimal (xy)` 10.12 → **10.13 kB**, `+ layoutAnimation` 10.51 → **10.49 kB**, `+ landing` 10.36 → **10.39 kB**, complete 10.85 → **10.86 kB**, baseline A 10.60 → **10.58 kB**. Module counts unchanged; every composition inside its budget, headroom **0.17–0.18 kB**. The two negative deltas are brotli noise on a change that only adds returns and one branch per axis. The Phase 21 re-base note stands and no budget was raised.

**Review 4 — [`reviews/checkpoint-d-4.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-4.md), four findings, all resolved.** Implementation record: [`reviews/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-implementation.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-implementation.md). C2-01's mechanism, C3-01's return channel and C3-03's tier split are all untouched. The four findings needed no architect decision; the residue left open by C4-01 did, and was decided afterwards in [`reviews/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue.md) — the last bullet below.

- **C4-01 — I-36 still permitted consumer calls and state publication after `destroy()`, and the third narrowing in a row is the finding.** The barrier stood between the `visual()` resolver and the candidate's own `getBoundingClientRect()` — and _that_ read is a consumer call under the indirect-invocation clause C3-03 §3.2 added, because the candidate is the consumer's element and, with no `visual()` composed, is also its own visual. A destroy raised from it fell through to the cache write, to the next candidate's resolver, and on the **last** candidate to the trailing bookkeeping that marks a retired cache clean and measured. The same class was wide open in `layoutAnimation()`, whose two passes call `getBoundingClientRect()` and `animate()` on consumer-owned rows in loops the behavior cannot reach into; the reviewer reproduced it against the real composition (expected 0 post-close `animate()` calls, received 1). **F-47's table was wrong** where it said the `beforeMove`/`afterMove` hooks need no barrier because no first-party hook reaches consumer code.
- **The fix is a general mechanism, not a third site patch, and the argument for that is recorded because it is the transferable part.** The enumeration had been derived by listing consumer _callbacks_; under the indirect clause the real set is **every DOM method the library calls on a consumer-owned node**, which does not terminate as a list — three passes each closed the site the previous reviewer reproduced. So the rule became structural: a reading at the **head of every loop that touches consumer-owned nodes**, after each consumer-reachable call inside it, and before the next foreign call, DOM mutation or state publication — and every participant that touches consumer DOM is _handed a liveness reading_ rather than having its caller patched. `DisplacementView` gained `live`, the **fifth** additive widening of a consumer-declared view, which C2-01 §9.5 had already pre-recorded as a routine act. Nine readings landed across `rect-index.ts` (entry, post-resolver, post-geometry), `xy.ts` (its second placeholder call, which `y()` does not make), `layout-animation.ts` (both passes plus post-`animate()`), `spec.ts` (the `beforeMove` pipeline, `anchorTarget`, `release.effect`) and `placement.ts` (post-factory). F-47's table is re-derived per **site** rather than per callback name.
- **One residue is stated, and at review 5 it is _discharged_ rather than excused** (C5-03; review 4 classified it as a conforming residue, which C5-03 found incoherent). `landing()`'s `duration` thunk is consumer code, and the next statements inside `LandingStart` reach the consumer's own visual — `realm.window.matchMedia`, then `visual.animate()`, then the `finished` subscription. The module holds no liveness reading and **needs none**: it is **bracket-discharged under I-36 (1)**. Before publication F-30's `!settlementLive(attempt)` branch destroys the unpublished handle, whose `destroy()` is `generation += 1; animation.cancel()` — removing the effect including the forwards fill inside the same synchronous stretch, with no style flush, no rAF and no paint; after publication `retireSettlement` disposes the published handle the same way, which is what covers `retarget` too. **The falsifier**: any consumer-reaching stretch inside `start` or `retarget` whose effect the bracket does not undo — a publication, a retained reference, a mutation on a node the library did not adopt, or a continuation that outlives `start`'s return. On any of those `landing.ts` must hold a reading and `FeatureContext.live()` is the shape — behavior-owned, unexported, **no frozen SPI and no public surface**, measured at **+6 B to +53 B**. C4-01's contrary claim that it needed a widened `LandingContext` was wrong on the facts and review 4's correction of that stands. **The remedy is available and measured but not queued**: it installs a `rt.closed` reading blind to a `panic()` destroy beside a kernel bracket that sees one, and would spend half the tightest composition's headroom on defence in depth.
- **Checkpoint D review 4 (the landing residue) — I-36's indirect-invocation clause was a quantifier over call sites and is now a floor plus a register.** Decided by the architect in [`reviews/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue.md) and applied as specified; implementation record [`reviews/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue-implementation.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-4-resolution-landing-residue-implementation.md). Read literally, the clause governed every overridable member of every consumer-owned node — a set the consumer chooses, which cannot be enumerated and which contains _paired_ calls (`animate()` and the `cancel()` that undoes it) admitting no reading between them, so no implementation could be **shown** to discharge it and each of three passes closed only the one site the previous reviewer reproduced. It becomes three parts: **(1) provisioning**, quantified over _modules_ — every module that reaches consumer code holds a liveness reading, a question with a mechanical answer; **(2) a five-act floor**, universal and testable — after a closed reading, no publication that outlives the operation, no retention past `retire()`, no DOM mutation teardown will not undo, no declared consumer callback, no dereference of state `retire()` may have nulled; **(3) a register of named ceilings**, where a site absent from the register gets the floor and carrying no ceiling is **not a finding**. The floor is calibrated so C4-01's two defects still breach it and the `landing()` residue does not; the register is what keeps C3-01 a defect, its site being named at `contract/03` and `README.md`. **The narrowing licenses removing no landed reading** — all nine of C4-01's stay — and it costs **0 B**: markdown plus one conformance test. **The fifth review found the new formulation internally inconsistent, and review 5 (C5-03) completed it**: provisioning is **bimodal** — a reading _or_ a named kernel bracket that revalidates and undoes — the floor's antecedent is fixed to _once the controller is closed, whether or not the participant has taken a reading_, I-6 clause 3 is qualified **in its own invariant cell** to _afterwards no callback fires that leaves anything behind_, and the word **complete** is removed from provisioning, which is necessary and not sufficient: C5-01 and C5-02 were both at modules that already held readings.
- **Deviation recorded while applying the landing-residue decision: one sentence outside the sweep list had to move, and every §1 factual claim was re-verified against source rather than taken.** The decision's §8 sweep did not list Review 4's own introductory sentence _"No architect decision was needed… the one thing that would have needed one is left open and stated rather than decided"_, which the decision itself falsifies; it is corrected to say the four **findings** needed none and the residue was decided afterwards. Nothing else outside the seventeen anchors was touched. The four claims §1 rests on were each checked in the artifact and all four hold: `armSettlement`'s `start` call through F-30's `runner.destroy()` is one synchronous stretch with no await and no rAF, so there is no paint between `animate()` and `cancel()`; `play`'s `catch` bumps the generation and cancels before rethrowing, so a throwing `finished`/`then` leaves no orphan; `completeLanding` returns early when `settlement !== attempt || queue.closed`, so `done`/`fail` are inert on a retired attempt (I-4); and `realm.window.matchMedia?.(…)` sits between the thunk and `animate()`, making it a **third** consumer-reachable call under the clause reading, which is the decision's own demonstration that the clause enumerated the wrong thing. **`landing.ts` gains no guard and no landed liveness reading was removed anywhere.**
- **C4-01's cost, measured.** minimal 10,079 → **10,116 B** (+37), `minimal (xy)` 10,125 → **10,168 B** (+43), `+ layoutAnimation` 10,493 → **10,563 B** (+70), `+ landing` 10,391 → **10,405 B** (+14), complete 10,864 → **10,934 B** (+70), baseline A 10,581 → **10,668 B** (+87). Module counts unchanged; **every composition stayed inside the budget it already had and no budget was raised**. Headroom is now **0.11–0.16 kB**, tightest on `+ layoutAnimation` and `complete` at 106 B each. Composition cost is **266 B / 0.27 kB / 2.5%** and migration cost **3,227 B / 3.23 kB**, both re-derived from this pass's own run. Per-frame: one boolean-returning call per candidate per _rebuild_ — now in **every** composition, including the minimal one, where C2-01's shape paid nothing — plus one per affected row per _committed move_ in the displacement bracket, and one field read per committed move at each behavior-side reading. Rebuilds do not happen on a warm cache, which is the common frame, and each reading sits on the same line as a `getBoundingClientRect()` that forces layout.
- **Sixteen new tests, every one verified to fail against pre-fix source.** The convention earned its place again: three first drafts **passed** against unfixed source, because a destroy raised from an _earlier_ candidate's geometry was already caught by the next iteration's reading — only the **last** candidate discriminates. A fourth needed the instrumented rect returned _shifted_, because teardown puts the row back exactly where the pass measured it and `delta === 0` skips `animate()` for a reason unrelated to the barrier. Test count 736 → **752**.
- **C4-02 — the normative account denied the return channel that was implemented.** Contract 03 still said an aborted rebuild reaches `null` down the ordinary I-15 path "so no new return channel exists". It now describes the boolean channel and, per the finding, the **complete set of calls capable of causing the abort** — entry, resolver, candidate geometry — plus `xy()`'s second placeholder reading and why `y()` needs none.
- **C4-03 — Q-7 and Q-12 carried mutually exclusive statuses.** Q-7 is moved into contract 05's resolved table with its "blocking before implementation sign-off" label explicitly **discharged** rather than deleted, the C3 implementation record's carry-forward is annotated as wrong, and the Phase 11 gate note says it cleared. Q-12's contract 03 sentence now agrees with 05 and names the checked-in fixture. `q7.md` gains a dated correction: it was written when the axis was `vertical()` and rebuilt **lazily**, and its answer-2 argument from lazy timing is overtaken by the eager bracket — which strengthens the conclusion rather than weakening it, because the two features now read in the same bracket and still share nothing. **The sweep found two more**: contract 00's F-5 was still `Open` although the Q-1 answer that decides it landed in Phase 4, and contract 05's M-3 row published 2026-08-02 absolute bytes with no note that they are a baseline rather than the live figures.
- **C4-04 — size-derived prose re-derived from this pass's own `just size` run**, with exact byte counts quoted beside the rounded ones so the next reader can tell a stale figure from a current one: composition cost **266 B / 0.27 kB / 2.5%**, migration cost **3,227 B / 3.23 kB**, remeasurement labels advanced to 2026-08-08, `plan.md`'s headroom sentence corrected (it said `0.16–0.21 kB` where the harness showed `0.17–0.23 kB`) and re-measured. The ledger's "dropped from `sortable.js`" is now "omitted from the decided `sortable.js` surface", which is what the negative fixture proves and is true of `CancellationReason`, which was never a named export.

**Review 5 — [`reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-5.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-5.md) (originally misspelled `checkpoitn-d-5.md`; corrected on disk, and every reference now follows it — C6-08), four findings, all resolved.** C5-03 was decided by the architect in [`reviews/checkpoint-d-5-resolution-c5-03.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-5-resolution-c5-03.md); implementation record for the whole pass: [`reviews/checkpoint-d-5-resolution-implementation.md`](reviews/checkpoint-d/checkpoint-d-5-resolution-implementation.md). C2-01's mechanism, C3-01's return channel, C3-03's tier split, D2, D5 and L-11's Phase 23 deferral are all untouched, and nothing was added to any public entrypoint or to the frozen SPI.

- **C5-01 — `layoutAnimation()` could retain a new animation after retirement.** C4-01 checked `view.live()` after `element.animate()` but then performed a second acquisition sequence with no terminal check: read the overridable `animation.finished` accessor, call its `.then(…)`, publish into `running`. Both are consumer-reachable through an overridden `animate()`, and **the failing path is a synchronous destroy that returns normally**, so the `catch` that already treats them as fallible never sees it — `retire()` runs while `running` is still empty and cannot cancel, and the row keeps a live displacement forever. Floor acts 1, 2 and 3. **Subscription is now part of the all-or-nothing acquisition**: a reading between `finished.then(…)` and `running.set()`, cancelling the untracked animation on a closed reading. Two permanent regressions — accessor and thenable — both verified to fail against pre-fix source.
- **C5-02 — `createPlaceholder()` guarded the factory but not its mechanics.** On a live reading `applyMechanics()` ran an unchecked sequence over consumer-owned objects: two `setAttribute()` calls, `item.getAttribute()`, a slot mutation, three style writes and `visual.offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`. Those mutations stay on the consumer's element — teardown never adopted it — so this is floor act 3, six statements deep. **Restructured to every read before any write, then one reading per write.** The read run leaves nothing behind whichever read closes the controller, and no write is reached after a closed reading. Three regressions: a custom placeholder's own `setAttribute`, a visual offset getter with a custom placeholder, and the same getter on the **default** composition, all verified to fail pre-fix.
- **C5-03 — the landing-residue decision made I-6/I-36 internally inconsistent, and the architect's resolution is applied verbatim.** Provisioning is now **bimodal** — a participant's own liveness reading, _or_ a named kernel bracket that revalidates after the module's consumer-reaching stretch and undoes what it did — so `landing()` stops being a counterexample **without an exemption being written for it**. The floor's antecedent is fixed to _once the controller is closed, whether or not the participant has taken a reading_. I-6 clause 3 is qualified **in its own invariant cell** to _afterwards no callback fires that leaves anything behind_, which is mandatory rather than discretionary: the kernel itself deliberately calls `runner.destroy()` on a consumer-authored `LandingHandle` after teardown, and must, or it leaks a runner nothing owns (I-20). **C3-03's retention of the headline verbatim is superseded by this**, and that supersession is stated in I-6's own mechanism cell rather than by editing C3-03's resolution record, which is historical. **`FeatureContext.live()` is withdrawn as a queued obligation** and removed as a queued item from F-47, ledger L-12, this checkpoint's bullets and Phase 21. **The public conformance test keeps every assertion**; only its comment changed, and it gains a second citation and the label _the bracket-discharge witness_. One test is owed and landed: the kernel's admitted post-terminal relinquishment, pinned as _exactly one_ `destroy` on a consumer-authored handle with `retarget` never called. Both landing pins were mutation-checked against removing F-30's `runner.destroy()`, against which both fail. Cost: **0 B**.
- **C5-04 — supporting documents retained pre-resolution claims.** Contract 06's trace no longer says the two features measure the same list or that Q-7/M-4 is an open duplicate-read cost, and no longer calls Q-12 "the one open mechanism"; `tests/COVERAGE.md`'s Q-12 section states the landed answer instead of opening as unresolved and closing with a recommendation; and `ledger.md`'s parity explanation no longer says the three names other than `AnimationTiming` were names `sortable.js` alone ever exported — `CancellationReason` was never a named export, `ResolutionContext` was only structurally exposed, and shipped `DragSubject` was exported by both `sortable.js` and `draggable.js`. The negative assertions themselves were sound and are unchanged.
- **The sortable stretch table — Checkpoint D's remaining closure obligation, landed; retired as a proof domain by D-37 (Revision 2).** C5-03 §7 made the terminating sweep an obligation of this checkpoint rather than of Phase 18, on the grounds that a checkpoint cannot honestly close over an artifact nobody has swept. The table is in §[05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md) beside F-47 and **replaced F-47's per-foreign-call table as the normative enumeration**. Complete against `ls src/sortable/*.ts`: twenty modules, **62 stretches — 38 (a) reading-headed, 6 (b) bracket-discharged, 18 (c) conforming residue with a pin, and 0 (d)**. Six modules carry no consumer-reaching stretch and are listed with the reason.

  **It is no longer normative and it is not deleted.** D-37 narrows the liveness domain to declared slots, admissions and publications, so the table's 18 conforming-residue rows classify stretches that the domain no longer reaches, and the sweep's completeness argument — "complete against `ls src/sortable/*.ts`" — was never the hard part; the quantifier inside each module was. **The 38 reading-headed rows stay true and their readings stay in the source**, and the table survives as provenance and as a map of where the sortable touches consumer-owned DOM, which is useful for reasons that have nothing to do with liveness. **Read the sweep's own result as the argument for retiring it:** twenty modules, five passes, three narrowings and 62 rows produced zero (d) verdicts — the enumeration found nothing the floor had not already caught, which is what an obligation looks like when its ceiling is unreachable rather than merely unreached.

- **The sweep found nine defects across eleven stretches, of which seven were new**, and that count is the honest measure of whether four prior passes had converged. Two were the fifth review's (C5-01, C5-02). The seven new: `placeholder.ts`'s `classList.add` after the consumer's `create` factory — fixed by threading the reading on the **internal** slot signature, so the public `PlaceholderFactory` is unchanged — and six in `spec.ts`: the draft seed and the command destination after `getVisual`, the activation publications and `onStart` after the survival conjuncts (`isConnected`, `nextElementSibling`), `draft.insertion`/`draft.proposal` after a `beforeMove` hook _and_ after the axis's own placeholder read, `rt.pendingRequest` after the release render, `draft.domain` after the resolution's own accessor, and the destination re-anchor after its conjuncts. **They share one shape the module-level quantifier could not see**: six of the seven are publications into a frame teardown has already scrubbed and will never scrub again, at modules that were fully provisioned. Ten new regressions, every one verified to fail against pre-fix source; both new conformance pins mutation-checked.
- **Review 4 had recorded three of its readings as uncovered defence in depth. Two of the three now discriminate, and finding out how is what exposed C5-02 and four of the sweep's rows.** `createPlaceholder`'s was uncovered because the preparation is discarded whole — but the first consumer-reachable call _inside_ `applyMechanics` discriminates. `anchorTarget`'s and `release.effect`'s were uncovered because the placeholder is torn down along the same edge — but a direct drive with a consumer accessor that destroys discriminates, and both turned out to have a second, unguarded stretch behind the landed reading. _"No discriminating fixture was found"_ is a statement about the fixtures tried; at a floor-level barrier it is a reason to try a different level.
- **The budget re-based, and the rule that made it a non-decision.** Phase 21's re-base was pulled ahead of this pass on the architect's recommendation. Mid-pass the fixes took `complete` **1 B over** its 11,040 budget; the budget re-based rather than the fix shrinking, per the rule now written into Phase 21 — _a size budget is never a reason to defer a fix for a floor breach_. Brotli then returned part of it as the repeated `rt.closed` guards began sharing a dictionary, so the landed cost is minimal 10,116 → **10,199 B** (+83), `minimal (xy)` 10,168 → **10,245 B** (+77), `+ layoutAnimation` 10,563 → **10,653 B** (+90), `+ landing` 10,405 → **10,487 B** (+82), complete 10,934 → **11,025 B** (+91), baseline A 10,668 → **10,765 B** (+97); baseline B unchanged at 6,889 B. Module counts unchanged. Every budget is now its measurement plus ~150 B. **Per-frame cost is zero**: not one reading is on the pointer-sample path. `applyMechanics`'s six are once per _operation_; `seedDraft`'s and activation's are once per _admission_; the release, settlement and re-anchor readings are once per _operation_; `layoutAnimation()`'s new one is once per animated row per _committed move_, on a line that already forces layout.
- **C4-01's release-render finding is extended rather than reopened.** C4-01 placed a reading between `movePlaceholder` and `rt.lift.write` in `release.effect`; the sweep found that `write` composes a transform onto `visual.style`, which is itself an accessor a custom element may define, so `rt.pendingRequest`'s publication two lines later needed a reading of its own. The landed reading stays; a second one was added after the render.

---

## Revision 2 — the API redesign

**Contract revision complete — D-36…D-60, dated 2026-08-13, reconciled as D-61…D-65 the same day.** Documents 00–06, both READMEs and this plan revised in place, in one pass, exactly as Phase 14 was. Implementation is **not** part of this section; it is Phase R below.

**Revision 2.1 — the owner found five owner-decision drifts.** A supported middle authoring tier, one `onEnd` terminal, the removal of the public custom landing runner, coarse consumer error codes with internal stages, and `placeholder` as the callback slot. **Four of the five live only in the earlier API review rounds**, not in the final summary that Revision 2 reconciled against — which is the whole lesson: §12 said "reconcile against the decisions above", and "above" was one document out of five. A reconciliation is performed against the decision **history**; the digest is a checklist for what is recent, not a substitute for the record.

**Revision 2.1 opened Q-14 and the owner closed it as D-66.** Owner final §4 requires one `onEnd` per started, still-subscribed operation and declines the `aborted` arm review 3 §11 proposed for precisely the case 02 §The join used to skip the terminal in. The resolution: **`onEnd` is exactly-once domain disposition — the frame's existing result where it holds one, `canceled` where it does not.** 02 §The join's skip is retracted and D-23 loses the clause _"no terminal callback"_.

**The mechanism was already written down in this file, two documents away from where it was needed.** §Phase 5 above states it exactly: _"the mapping table requires `settlement.prepare(SETTLED_FAILED)` to build the terminal state"_ — the failure checkpoint drives the settlement seam stamped `REPORTING`, and `SettlementInput` has carried `{ stage, error }` since D-24. D-66's first draft named `reportFailure` instead, which is admission-only and cannot carry an in-operation failure at all. **A reviewer caught it; the plan had the answer the whole time.** That is the same failure mode as Revision 2.1 itself, one level down: the record was consulted selectively, and the part that was skipped is the part that mattered.

**D-67** replaces the zero-argument landing thunk with `duration({ distance, from, to })`, closing the L-6 conflict D-63 opened.

**What is different from Phase 14, and it is the thing to understand first.** Phase 14 revised the **SPI** — four places the construction model could not express something, three of which were the sortable's shape written into the kernel. Revision 2 revises the **consumer surface that sits on top of it**, and changes who the library is for. Nothing here reopens Part I. **Two things do ask the SPI to express something new** — D-53's added `KernelHost` member and D-59's widened `admit` return — and the claim that nothing did was asserted three times and corrected three times, which is recorded below as a pattern rather than as an erratum. **Revision 2.1 adds no third crossing**: D-63 moves the landing runner between tiers without changing a seam. That is also why the freeze rule discharges differently, and §Ground rules 5 and the Phase 13 gate now say so explicitly rather than leaving the reader to infer it.

### Probe → decision

| Probe | Failing executable case | Decisions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A** — transaction bracket | 21 browser failures against the floor-preserving variant; **27 of 33** statement-level liveness readings retire; a manufactured `panic()` producer shows teardown leaving the throwing stack, observed as teardown order flipping `['retire','report']` → `['report','retire']` | D-36, D-37, D-38, D-39 |
| **C1** — the commit window | 3 of 5 authored commits land at the viewport origin while reporting `onFinish` once and `onError` **zero** times; the placeholder runs 30 px too tall for a whole drag | D-42, D-43 |
| **E** — input policy | 8 of 10 cases release-blocking, **six with no drag ever activating**; `ArrowRight` in a nested text input produces a complete accepted reorder from one keystroke | D-46 |
| **api-1** — layout | measured: no single-window rule reproduces the removed footprint in both nested cases | D-43 |
| — | **none, and not claimed** | D-40, D-41, D-44, D-45, D-47, D-48, and the consequential rows D-49…D-58 that follow from them |

**Two SPI crossings, each with its own evidence, and the rule is working rather than being waived.** This section asserted **one** until late in the reconciliation, and the correction is recorded below as a pattern rather than as an erratum, because it is the third time the claim has had to be fixed.

| Crossing | What it changes | Discharged by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **D-53** | **Adds a member.** One readonly `KernelHost` reader answering _is this controller logically closed_ — the latch itself, not a proxy for it. | **Probe A.** D-38 forbids physical-teardown observations, and after that prohibition **there is no reading left**: the latch is kernel-private and `KernelHost` exposed none. Probe A's three `presentation.signal.aborted` sites exist _precisely because_ no logical reading was available, and they are the only failures the bracket caused on its own. Requiring every behavior to hand-mirror the latch is worse after D-47 than before it, because the kernel is published and a third-party author would have to know to do it. |
| **D-59** | **Widens a seam signature.** `admit` returns the element to lift **optionally paired with a box element**, and every behavior implements `admit`. | **api-1, through a chain rather than a single case** — and the chain is written out because _"it repairs D-52"_ is not on its own a freeze-rule justification: api-1 measured that **no single-window rule** reproduces the removed footprint in both nested cases → D-43 needs two windows → the second window needs the box element **before** `acquireLift` → the only two carriers are the behavior's draft and admission's return → the draft form contradicts **H-2** and **D-15** → admission's return is what remains. The failing executable case is api-1's; D-59 is simply where it lands. |

**Recorded as a pattern, because it recurred after being corrected twice.** "Revision 2 asks nothing new of the SPI" was asserted three times — in the brief, in 00 §Status, and in the implementation handoff — and corrected three times, each time by a reviewer rather than by the author. The claim is attractive because it makes the revision sound cheaper and better-behaved than it is, and it survived two corrections precisely because nobody re-derived it; it was carried forward as settled. **The instrument that caught it each time was a document being made consistent with another document**, not a review pass looking for it: the liveness reader surfaced from reconciling D-38, and the `admit` widening from reconciling the trace. **Two sanctioned crossings with named evidence is a healthier record than one, and far healthier than a false claim of zero** — a freeze rule that is never invoked is not being honoured, it is being avoided.

**The last row is the honest one and is kept as a row rather than a footnote.** Six of the thirteen decisions carry no executable case. They are owner decisions about the shape of a public API, taken after three review rounds, and the freeze rule does not govern them — it governs the SPI frozen for the implementation probe. D-41 is the closest to the line because it retracts D-33, which _was_ a Phase-14 SPI revision; it is admitted as a **deletion**, and a deletion needs no case the SPI cannot express.

### Declined as Revision 2 scope

Each was on the table, each is refused with a reason, and each is recorded here so a later phase does not re-propose it as an oversight.

| Declined | Instead | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **An `aborted` terminal** beside `canceled` (D-40) | One `canceled` terminal; `onError` stays the orthogonal diagnostic channel | A second name would encode _provenance_, not a different consumer obligation — in both cases the consumer must not assume its own started work was undone, because the library never had a way to undo it. Splitting the name obliges every consumer to handle two results that call for the same code. |
| **Recovery machinery for destructive renderers** (D-42) | A stated integration contract plus an **O(1)** precondition check that reports through `onError` and lands from the unrepaired position | C1 shows `ReorderRequest.before`/`after` survive in four of five strategies, so recovery is _buildable_ — and buying it would mean relaxing D-27's cross-container refusal for a case now outside the contract. |
| **Detection of unsupported layouts** (D-43) | Three positively stated scope limits: visual order follows DOM order, rule-placed layouts unsupported, `box === visual` required in grid | Nothing in `sortable/` reads a computed placement, and the shipped package fails these layouts too — silently, by deriving the gap from DOM order after a geometric search. A detector would be new machinery guarding a case that has never worked. |
| **A generic dependency resolver for config fragments** (D-45) | Atomic capability slots: several runtime parts that must be acquired and retired together are **one slot**, not several independently mergeable fields | Preserving accidental coupling between fields that happened to come from the same helper is exactly the provenance tracking D-45 deletes. If parts belong together, say so in the schema. |
| **Elaborate selection-intent detection** (D-46) | Decline on interactive and editable descendants; a modifier (Alt) requests native text selection inside a draggable **non-interactive** region | The owner's direction verbatim: do not build it unless evidence requires it. Probe E's ten cases are answered by the decline path, which is already total. |
| **An exported `defineBehavior` brander** (D-48) | `draggable()`, `BehaviorFactory` and the behavior-construction types published at `@ydinjs/drag/kernel` | The brander preserves the two-phase handshake equally well, and leaves D-45 §8 and D-47 §11 wrong as written while making the consumer write two calls where the shipped package wrote one. Moving the public boundary answers both; branding around it answers neither. |

### Review rounds

| Round | Outcome |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | Independent reviews (`api-review-1-*`). The consumer surface examined as a surface for the first time — Part I and Phase 14 had both reviewed it as a consequence of the construction model. |
| 2 | `api-review-2-*`. Produced the probe slate: the questions that could not be settled by argument were named and turned into probes rather than decided. |
| 3 | `api-review-3-*`, plus `api-review-3-probe-plan.md` and the C5 resolution. Ran A, C1, E and api-1, and every one of them changed a decision — which is the property that distinguishes this round from a consistency pass. |
| — | [`api-review-final-summary.md`](reviews/api-reviews/api-review-final-summary.md) — the owner's twelve sections, which D-36…D-60 record. |

**The shape of the record, as Phase 14 read its own:** every probe here falsified a claim that had survived a full review round on argument alone. C-03 relocated the admission `preventDefault()` and Checkpoint C called it "the same observable result for the same events" — probe E then found the call itself release-blocking in eight of ten cases. I-36 was narrowed three times and swept across 62 stretches — probe A then retired 27 of 33 readings by changing the domain. D-16 was corrected once — probe C1 then showed the provisional target is stale in **all five** commit strategies, which D-41 dissolves by removing the provisional interval. **Phase 14's lesson was that a correction is not finished when its sentence is true. Revision 2's is narrower and worse: a sentence can be true, reviewed five times, and about the wrong thing.** Only running it found that out.

**What Revision 2 deliberately did not touch, checked rather than assumed.** The eight-phase vocabulary; the five resource lifetimes; the kernel's seven-field frame slice; the seam set; the tri-phase transition; `SeamOutcome` and the per-seam continuation policies; D-15's kernel-first frame composition; every measured number in M-1, M-2 and M-4. `KernelHost` **shrinks** by one member — `presentationCommitted`, D-33's and now deleted — and gains none.

---

## Phase R — Revision 2 implementation

**Lettered rather than numbered on purpose.** It lands after Checkpoint D and before Phase 18, and renumbering 18–25 would invalidate every cross-reference in the contract set, the reviews and the ledger for no gain.

**Scope.** Bring `src/`, `tests/` and both READMEs to the reconciled contracts. This is the first phase to run **any** of D-36…D-65 against a runtime, so it carries the same standing obligation Phase 15 carried for Phase 14: the existing suite green against the revised surface, with every recorded deviation re-checked rather than assumed.

**Deliverables.**

- **D-36/D-38** — the transaction bracket. Logical closure on `destroy()`'s closing statement; physical teardown at the outermost transaction boundary; `destroy(): Promise<void>` settling once; `panic()` on the same model with the order _close → report → teardown_. **Every liveness reading audited against D-38**: no `signal.aborted`, disposed session or detached node may answer a liveness question, and the three probe-A failures were exactly those sites.
- **D-37** — retire the ceiling register and the stretch table as normative artifacts, keep every landed reading, and restate the invariant over the finite domain. Nothing is removed from `src/`.
- **D-39** — `activation.rollback` becomes required and non-vacuous for the sortable: it undoes library-authored attributes, styles and state written onto a consumer-owned placeholder during a `prepare` that was never adopted.
- **D-41** — delete the readiness protocol: `controller.ready`, `ResolutionOptions`, `KernelHost.presentationCommitted`, `rt.pendingRequest`, `authoredReady`, the deadline, `readinessTimeout` and the readiness-specific retarget lifecycle. Settlement holds one gate. `SortableRuntime` returns to six mutable fields.
- **D-42/D-43** — the O(1) placeholder precondition check at the authoritative landing measurement, reporting through `onError`; `box()` beside `visual()` with `box(item) = visual(item)` by default; the two measurement windows, both offset-box reads, with the footprint as `boxPre − boxPost`.
- **D-44** — `items` as the pull source, `controller.invalidate()` as the payload-free signal, array identity as the structural test, the shallow copy on the structural branch only. Delete `updateItems`.
- **D-45/D-47/D-48/D-55/D-56/D-57** — plain declarative partial config, schema-aware merge by slot, installers materialized post-merge in **schema order** with plugins in array order and retirement the reverse of installation (D-57, which closes the ordering D-45 left undefined once provenance was erased). Withdraw `FEATURE_BRAND`, `SortableFeature` as a brand-only type, `unbrandFeature`, `brandFeature` — **and `Behavior<Controller>`, `brandBehavior` and `unbrandBehavior`'s install-path use (D-55)**, which D-48 leaves without a producer. `sortable(root, config, …fragments)` and `freeDrag(root, …fragments)` return their controllers directly. **Entry topology (D-48, corrected): `drag.js` keeps its job as the shared vocabulary** — errors, `Point`, `DOMRealm` — **and a new `kernel.js` carries `draggable()` and the authoring types**; `sortable.js` is unchanged in role. **D-56 deletes `callbacks()`, `handle()`, `visual()` and `placeholder()` as factories** and the three subpaths carrying them (`sortable/callbacks.js`, `sortable/handle.js`, `sortable/placeholder.js` — `handle()` and `visual()` share one), moving their construction-time validation into the merge where it fires for a config supplied any way at all. Surviving factories: `y()`, `xy()`, `landing()`, `layoutAnimation()`. **Net subpath count 9 → 7.**
- **D-49/D-51/D-52/D-53/D-54/D-58** — the consequential rows, each of which legislates code another decision also touches, and none of which is optional. **D-49/D-60**: a landing that cannot be measured reports through `onError`, **skips the animation and joins immediately**, superseding D-42's "lands from the unrepaired position" — the unrepaired position is the viewport origin, and a confident animation to `(0,0)` is the defect C1 shows. **D-60 makes the channels orthogonal**: one operation may produce `onError` **and** `onFinish`, and `FAILURE_LANDING_TARGET` is the first stage that is classified, non-consequential and has **no recovery** — the settlement is not failed and the domain result stands. The contract carried an unstated biconditional, `onError ⇒ consequential`, that nothing had needed to test until D-49 falsified it. **Suite consequence, stated because C1 phrases the defect the other way round:** the defect is _`onFinish` once, `onError` zero_ and the fix is _`onFinish` once, `onError` once_, so **every assertion of mutual exclusivity between the two channels is now false** and must be found rather than waited for. **D-51**: D-37 (a)'s one named relinquishing exception, `LandingHandle.destroy()`, without which the finite domain is false where I-20 and F-30 need it true. **D-52/D-59**: the two `box` windows have distinct owners — kernel reads `boxPre` before `acquireLift`, behavior reads `boxPost` at the top of `activation.prepare` — and **`originRect` is derived from neither**. **D-59 repairs D-52's delivery**: `admit` returns the element to lift **optionally paired with a box element** and the kernel stores `box` in **its own frame slice**; it does **not** read a behavior-authored draft field, which would contradict H-2 and D-15 and is exactly the "kernel learns one sortable-shaped thing" defect Checkpoint C found four times. **Sequence this one first, and not with the geometry work its subject matter suggests** — see §Ordering below. **D-53**: one readonly `KernelHost` member for logical liveness — **one of the two SPI crossings**, not the only one. **D-54**: `preventDefault()` moves to activation, selection begun pre-threshold is cleared, one trailing `click` is suppressed in the capture phase, and scroll suppression is `touch-action`'s job, not this call's. **D-58**: insertion candidates measured as `box`, not `visual`, superseding parity D2's choice of node while keeping its reasoning.
- **D-46/D-50** — withdraw the unconditional admission `preventDefault()`; decline on interactive and editable descendants on both ingresses; `isComposing` never admits; the Alt convention for text selection in non-interactive draggable regions. **D-50 gives explicit consumer scoping precedence over the default decline** — where a `handle` resolves the subject, the resolved subject governs and the decline does not re-veto it, tested by walking the path between the event target and the resolved subject — which means `handle` stops being the _sole_ descendant-scoping mechanism 03 asserts and becomes the **override** for a policy that now exists by default. **Document the focusable-grip obligation** that a handle carries, which the shipped package also had and never stated (ledger §4). **Record probe E's evidence limit**: Chromium and mouse only, so touch long-press and tap-highlight behavior is an owed measurement, not a settled case (D-54).
- **D-61 / D-64** — the middle tier and the coarse error surface, landed with the entry-topology work above or the manifest is edited twice. `sortable/feature.js` publishes `SortableInstaller`, `FeatureContext`, `SortableContribution` and the seam types they name; `FailureStage` and the 14 `FAILURE_*` constants move to `kernel.js`; `DraggableError` with a coarse `code` appears on `drag.js`. **Write the total stage → code mapping first** — every `onError` call site depends on it, and a `default:` arm reproduces exactly the gap D-60 exists to forbid.
- **D-62 / D-63 / D-65** — the surface renames, deliberately last: one `onEnd(result)` taking the four-arm `ReorderTransactionResult` with `onFinish`/`onCancel` deleted; `landing({ run })` withdrawn and the three landing seam types re-homed on `sortable/feature.js`; `placeholder` as the single callback slot with `createPlaceholder` and `placeholderClassName` deleted. **D-62 cannot be finished until Q-14 is answered** — implement the four domain arms, leave the consequentially-failed path exactly as 02 §The join specifies, and mark the site.
- ~~**Check D-56 (see Phase 21 §Check D-56).** Run `bench/size` immediately before and after the three subpath deletions land. The prediction is **zero byte movement** on every affected composition; a non-zero delta falsifies D-45's premise for those modules and reopens D-56 rather than being absorbed. Record both numbers.~~ **Withdrawn unsatisfied 2026-08-22 by the owner, and recorded as O-8 in [`obligations.md`](obligations.md).** Phase R landed the deletion and nobody took the numbers, so the window this check names is permanently gone. **It is not satisfied and must not be described as satisfied**; no later measurement substitutes, and D-56 itself remains accepted on its argument.
- **The parity ledger re-verified**, not re-derived. Four rows changed class and six were added at Revision 2; each names an executable destination and Phase R is where those destinations exist.

**Ordering — one deliverable is mis-sorted by its subject matter, and it is the one that touches every behavior.** The list above reads roughly in dependency order, and **D-59 is the exception: it appears among the geometry rows because `box` is a geometry concept, and it is a change to `admit`, a seam every behavior implements.** Its blast radius is the seam set, not the sortable's measurement code — the sortable spec, the free-drag typed probe, the compiled fixtures and every hand-written slot literal in the test corpus all declare an `admit`, and each one's return type moves. **Land D-59 first**, with the seam signature and its call sites, before any of the geometry work that motivated it; landing it late means re-touching every file the earlier steps already changed. **The same caution applies to D-53** for a weaker reason: it adds rather than widens, so existing implementations keep compiling, but a `KernelHost` member that arrives after the liveness audit (D-38) means auditing twice. **Nothing else in Phase R may assume the SPI is untouched**, which is the assumption the phase would otherwise inherit from a revision described — for most of its reconciliation — as consumer-surface-only.

**Acceptance tests.** The owner's instruction is explicit and is recorded here so it is not softened into a suggestion: **remaining probe scenarios may become implementation acceptance tests** unless reconciling the contracts exposed a new architectural contradiction. Probe C1's five commit strategies, probe E's ten input cases and probe A's teardown-order and abandonment scenarios are the highest-value candidates, because each is a case the shipped package passes silently and wrongly.

**Costs already measured — carried here so they are not rediscovered.**

- **`destroy(): Promise<void>` puts 46 first-party call sites in `no-floating-promises` violation.** Every one is a plain `controller.destroy();` that never wanted a completion signal, **including a React `useEffect` cleanup in this package's own demo**, where returning the promise would change the cleanup's contract. The remedy is `void controller.destroy();` at each site that does not care and `await controller.destroy();` at the few that do — mechanical, and 46 sites of it.
- **The bracket spike measured +500/−491 lines across 13 source files.** It is a **complexity and invariant win, not a size win**, and it should not be presented as one at Phase 21 or in any README. What it buys is that 27 of 33 statement-level liveness readings retire and the remaining domain is finite.

**Done when.** The suite is green against the revised surface; the ledger's Revision 2 rows each have a landed destination; no document in the contract set, the READMEs, this plan or the ledger still describes `updateItems`, `controller.ready`, `ResolutionOptions`, the feature brand, `Behavior<Controller>` as public, `callbacks()`/`handle()`/`visual()`/`placeholder()` as factories, the readiness gate or a synchronous `destroy()` as current.

**Gate.** Phase 18 does not start until Phase R lands. Free drag inherits the controller shape, the config model, the teardown model and the liveness domain, and designing its public contract against the pre-Revision-2 versions of any of those is the mistake this ordering exists to prevent. **And it inherits a changed SPI**: D-59 widens `admit`, which free drag implements, and D-53 adds the `KernelHost` liveness reader it will need instead of a hand-mirrored latch — so 13c's typed probe is stale against both, and Phase 18 must re-read it rather than trusting it.

---

## Phases 18–20 — free drag

Treated as its own track, not a phase, because it is a complete second behavior with its own public contract, controller semantics, coordinate model, lift modes, constraints, settlement, stories, tests and measurements. Split so that the parity boundary, the API decisions, the implementation and the architectural evidence can each be reasoned about — and reviewed — separately.

### Phase 18 — Free drag: public contract and decomposition

**Complete, 2026-08-14. Delivered as [`contract/07-free-drag-contract.md`](contract/07-free-drag-contract.md)** — a normative contract document carrying **D-69…D-76** and **F-62…F-66**, with the decision rows in [`contract/00-index.md`](contract/00-index.md) §Phase 18, the four new entries in [03](contract/03-feature-composition.md) §The export topology this requires, and the lifecycle criteria in [05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md) §Test matrix, _Free drag_. Every deliverable below is discharged there; the list is kept because it is what the phase was asked for.

**What the phase found that it was not looking for, and what each one cost.**

- ~~**Two Phase 14 decisions are normative and unimplemented**~~ — **both landed 2026-08-15** as D-76's discrete step. (F-63): `BehaviorSpec.activation` is still pinned to `HTMLElement` and `LandingContext.from` is still `pointerX - originX`. `plan.md` §Phase 14 books both for _Phases 19–20_, so this is scheduling rather than drift — but free drag is **incorrect** without either, and no instrument reports a decision booked to a later phase. **D-76 makes them a discrete kernel step before Phase 19**, landed against the sortable alone with M-1/M-3 re-measured, so a landing regression has one candidate cause rather than two.
- ~~**The kernel classifies two behavior-generic seams with the sortable's vocabulary**~~ — **renamed 2026-08-15 (D-74)**. As found (F-62), and not as an overridable default: the settlement seam ran under `FAILURE_REORDER_RESOLUTION` and the action seam under `FAILURE_INSERTION`/`FAILURE_PLACEHOLDER_MOVE` for **every** behavior. They are now `FAILURE_RESOLUTION`, `FAILURE_ACTION_PREPARE` and `FAILURE_ACTION_EFFECT`, with no numeric value moved; the window in which that was free closes when the kernel tier gets its first consumer, and it had not yet.
- **The kernel measures a placeholder footprint for a behavior that has none** (F-65). D-52's window 1 is unconditional. Not a defect, not free, and not fixable by renaming — Phase 21 measures it, Checkpoint E decides.
- **Three composition types are duplicated across the two middle tiers** (F-64) and are **deliberately not unified**: inventing a cross-behavior composition vocabulary here would consume the evidence Checkpoint E exists to read. What lands is one declaration with two publications, which costs nothing and keeps the identity.

**Design phase; no runtime.** Builds on 13c's probe, which by now has been through the contract revision — **and which was re-read rather than trusted**, per the handoff's instruction; F-63 is what re-reading produced.

**Deliverables.**

- The public surface, per the ledger's free-drag rows: entry subpath(s), option domains and their validation (drag2 validates every option at construction and throws a `TypeError` — free drag's numeric and enum options join that rule **wherever the value exists at construction** — see the two stated sortable exceptions, a `landing({ duration })` thunk and a `landing({ run })` replacement runner), result and resolution types.
- **The feature decomposition.** Which of `axis`, `bounds`, `coordinateSpace`, lift mode, home target and the movement callbacks are behavior core and which are composable features. This is where the composition model earns or fails to earn its 0.26 kB: a consumer wanting an unconstrained drag should carry no bounds code.
- **The lift-mode surface decision**, made deliberately after Phase 11's finding that lift mode is behavioral. Whether the three internal modes become public, and under what names, is decided here rather than inherited.
- **`controller.update()`**: the live-policy seam and the controlled `position`, designed against whatever 14 settled for externally driven motion.
- The export topology extension, decided in full before modules exist — the same measurement precondition Phase 0 observed, for the same reason.
- **The terminal-barrier enumeration (I-36) — rewritten by D-37 (Revision 2); read this paragraph, then the original for context.** The deliverable is now **finite and small**: enumerate every **declared consumer slot** free drag's behavior invokes — admission resolvers, `onStart`, the per-sample movement callbacks, a bounds or constraint resolver, `controller.update()`'s policy, a home-target resolver, `onDrop`, `onFinish`/`onCancel`/`onError` — and state, for each, where the terminal barrier is. Add the other two categories D-37 names: where free drag **admits** an operation, and where it **publishes** a lifecycle or domain event. That list is produced by free drag's own option surface and it terminates. **Do not reproduce the stretch decomposition, the provisioning/floor/register apparatus or the "does anything the stretch does survive the stretch" sweep**; they were built to discharge a quantifier D-37 withdrew, and copying them forward would import an undischargeable obligation into the second behavior. The **floor survives verbatim** and is what the enumeration is checked against: once the controller is closed, whether or not the participant has taken a reading, no publication that outlives the operation, no retention past `retire()`, no DOM mutation teardown will not undo, no declared consumer callback, no dereference of state `retire()` may have nulled. The shape still transfers for free — one field, one line in `destroy()` — and **the stated falsifier is unchanged**: if free drag needs a **third** copy of the latch, kernel-supplied controller-lifetime liveness becomes a Checkpoint E question with evidence behind it. Original deliverable follows, retained because its reasoning about _why_ the rule does not transfer by itself is still the reason this is a deliverable and not an implementation detail of Phase 19. ~~**Read I-6 and I-36 as one pair before starting. I-6's tier states which half the kernel gives you free (`B for every call the kernel sequences`) and which half is yours (`over a tier-C participant obligation`); the enumeration below is the free-drag instance of the second half.** Free drag will have its own consumer callbacks — admission resolvers, `onStart`, the per-sample movement callbacks, a bounds or constraint resolver, `controller.update()`'s policy, a home-target resolver. **Enumerate every one the behavior invokes and state where its terminal barrier is**, in the same form as F-47's table: who invokes it, whether the kernel already revalidates around it, and — where the behavior drives the sequence — which liveness reading the site has. The _shape_ transfers for free: each behavior builds its own controller over its own private runtime, so a latch is one field and one line in `destroy()`. The _rule_ does not transfer by itself, which is why this is a deliverable and not an implementation detail of Phase 19. **If free drag needs a third copy of the latch**, that is the stated falsifier for keeping it behavior-owned, and a kernel-supplied controller-lifetime liveness becomes a Checkpoint E question with evidence behind it. **The enumeration is per module, and then per _consumer-reaching stretch_ within it — I-36 (1), and the sortable stretch table in §[05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md) is the worked example to copy.** For each module that reaches consumer code, state how it discharges: it **holds a liveness reading**, or its whole consumer-reaching stretch sits inside a **named kernel bracket that revalidates after the stretch and undoes what the stretch did** — the bracket cited and its undo stated. Then decompose each module into stretches: a **stretch** is a maximal run of the module's own statements from a consumer-reachable call to the next liveness reading, kernel bracket, or return, where a consumer-reachable call includes an overridable accessor on a consumer-owned node, not only a declared slot. **One question per stretch: does anything the stretch does survive the stretch?** — a publication that outlives the operation, a reference retained past `retire()`, a DOM mutation on a node the library did not adopt or will not restore, a declared consumer callback invoked, or a dereference of state `retire()` may have nulled. Three verdicts and no fourth: **(a)** reading-headed, **(b)** bracket-discharged with the bracket named and its undo stated, or **(c)** a conforming residue whose answer is "none" and which carries an **executable pin**. A stretch with a surviving consequence and no reading at its head is a defect, not a residue. **Holding a reading is not discharging** — C5-01 and C5-02 were both at provisioned modules, and seven more of the same species were found in `spec.ts` and `placeholder.ts` by the sweep that the module-level form could not see. Do not enumerate DOM method names: that set is chosen by the consumer and does not terminate.~~ **That last sentence is the one D-37 acted on**: an obligation whose subject does not terminate cannot be discharged by an implementation or checked by a review, and three narrowings did not change that.

- **Assumptions this phase inherited that Revision 2 breaks, checked rather than carried.** Phase 18 was written against a pre-Revision-2 surface and four of its premises no longer hold. **`draggable(item, freeDrag(...features))` is not the ordinary form** — D-48 makes it `freeDrag(item, ...fragments)`, returning a `FreeDragController` directly, and `draggable()` moves to the kernel tier; the "entry subpath(s)" deliverable decides one fewer thing and inherits a decided shape. **A feature is not a branded value, and after D-56 most of them are not values at all** — D-45 makes a fragment a plain declarative partial config merged by slot and D-56 deletes every factory that installs nothing, so free drag must decide which of `axis`, `bounds`, lift mode and the movement callbacks carry runtime machinery (a factory and a subpath) and which are plain config keys (neither). **D-55 also removes the packaged behavior value**, so free drag is installed by `freeDrag(root, …)` returning a controller, not by branding a behavior. D-45 makes it a plain declarative partial config merged by slot, so "the feature decomposition" deliverable is now a decomposition into _config slots_ with stated merge semantics (last-wins for scalar and capability slots, append for `plugins`), and the atomicity rule matters: `axis`, `bounds` and lift mode must each be one slot if their parts are acquired and retired together, because there is no dependency resolver and none is coming. **`controller.update()` is designed against a different neighbour set** — the controller has three members, not four, `updateItems` is gone, and D-44's pull-plus-invalidate shape is the precedent `update()` should be weighed against rather than `updateItems`'s push shape. **`destroy()` returns a promise** — free drag's controller inherits `Promise<void>` and the deferred-teardown model, so any free-drag deviation that reasoned from "physical release completes before it returns" is void, and D-38 forbids answering a liveness question from `signal.aborted` on a bounds resource or a presentation signal. **There is no readiness gate to design around**: settlement holds one gate, landing.

### Phase 19 — Free drag: implementation

**Gate, added at Phase 18 (D-76) — SATISFIED 2026-08-15.** Every clause below is discharged, against the sortable alone and with no free-drag module in the tree; the per-criterion evidence is [`contract/07-free-drag-contract.md`](contract/07-free-drag-contract.md) §Kernel preconditions. Does not start until the kernel step lands: D-34's parameterized activation staged type threaded through the construction generics, D-35's recorded lift delta and the `BehaviorLiftSession` projection, 05's **Landing origin — new (D-35)** group executing, the three stage renames with their numeric values asserted (D-74), and M-1/M-3 re-measured for the two scalar writes — all against the **sortable alone**, with no free-drag module in the tree. The reason is attribution: D-35 changes the hot path and re-verifies the landing for the behavior that already ships.

**Second gate, added by the validation reconciliation (F-67) — decided 2026-08-15 as D-77, and SATISFIED 2026-08-15: the step landed against the sortable alone, with no free-drag module in the tree.** What follows is the gate as it was written. The rule is re-derived across both behaviors: required configuration is a **required first argument** with later fragments `Partial`, and a construction-time throw survives only for an invariant over what installers _contribute_. The step is shaped like D-76's, for D-76's reason — it changes a public signature and deletes five explicit checks in the behavior that already ships, so it lands **against the sortable alone, with no free-drag module in the tree**: `sortable(root, config, ...fragments)`, `y()`/`xy()` returning the installer, the five deletions with [05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md)'s unwind-ordering obligation on the last of them, `claim` untouched, and the type fixture's negative assertions. Phase 19 then writes `freeDrag()` in a shape that is settled **and** already exercised on a live suite. **Independent of the D-76 gate**, which landed without it on 2026-08-15.

**Deliverables.** The behavior, its frame part, its features, its controller. Geometry through `@ydinjs/box-quad` — there is still no coordinate module in this package, and `coordinateSpace` must not become one.

**Complete, 2026-08-16.** Twelve entries where there were eight: `free-drag.js`, `free-drag/feature.js`, `free-drag/bounds.js`, `free-drag/landing.js`. D-69…D-73 and D-75 are implemented and their rows are retired from [`contract/00-index.md`](contract/00-index.md) §Decisions not yet implemented, which is now **empty** — every decision this contract states in the present tense is in the code, and `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` holds both directions over the empty table. D-76's kernel step and D-77/D-80's construction rules were consumed as landed rather than re-derived: `activation.prepare` returns `true`, `LandingContext.from` is the recorded delta, the required first argument is the signature, and there is no pull to validate ahead of the installers because there is no collection.

**Two findings, raised rather than designed around** — F-71 (the bounds source surfaces at the rendering seam rather than at `action.prepare`; same coarse code on the hot paths) and F-72 (the behavior re-measures the box space the kernel already measured, because `ActivationScope` does not carry the inherited linear part — F-65's mirror). Both are Checkpoint E evidence and neither blocks Phase 20.

**Geometry did go through box-quad, and `coordinateSpace` did not come back.** `localDelta` is the inverse of the inherited linear part, captured once at activation; every point on the surface is viewport, which is the seam D-72 followed.

**Not in this phase, by the phase boundary rather than by omission:** the adversarial matrix rows, the three restored stories and the per-lift-mode geometry fixtures under transform and zoom. `tests/free-drag/free-drag.browser.test.ts` is the implementation's own verification — 35 rows through the public entry covering activation, the hot path, both policy members, the round-trip and the terminal — and is not a substitute for Phase 20's deliverables.

**Watch item, carried from M-1.** The frame-copy cost jumps ~10× between 12 and 16 behavior-part fields on Chromium, and the sortable part sits 4 fields below that cliff. A free-drag part is a _different_ shape. If it lands above the cliff that is a measured cost, not a surprise — but it has to be measured, in Phase 21, and not assumed to inherit the sortable's number.

### Phase 20 — Free drag: validation and demos

**Deliverables.** Its own adversarial matrix rows; the three shipped stories restored (`Interactive`, `AsyncDropConfirmation`, `TransformedStage`); geometry tests for bounds clamping, coordinate mapping and each lift mode under transform and zoom.

**Explicit requirement, from Phase 11's lesson.** Phase 11 found a lift-mode regression that all 644 tests passed through, because no test compared the lifted visual's on-screen box to the placeholder's — it was caught by driving a demo. Free drag has three lift modes and a transformed-stage story. **The lift-geometry assertions are a deliverable of this phase**, and closing that open gap for the sortable belongs here too.

**Complete, 2026-08-16.** Four new suites and one restored story file: `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts` (B-4, all five clauses), `tests/free-drag/lifecycle.browser.test.ts` (L-1…L-4), `tests/free-drag/geometry.browser.test.ts` and `tests/sortable/lift-geometry.browser.test.ts` (L-5, both behaviors), `tests/free-drag/feature.declaration.test.ts` (B-6's type half), plus two packed consumer fixtures — an ordinary-tier free-drag consumer (B-5) and an out-of-line `constrain` installer (B-6) — and `src/free-drag.stories.tsx` restoring `Interactive`, `AsyncDropConfirmation` and `TransformedStage`. The fixture harness moved to `tests/support/free-drag.ts` rather than being copied four times. **D-81's source half landed**: `src/free-drag/bounds.ts` names all four seams and states that `FAILURE_ACTION_PREPARE` is unreachable from the row, so §Decisions not yet implemented is empty again.

**What the matrix found, and both are claim-side.** **F-75** — B-4 (b)'s _exactly one terminal_ cannot hold for the classified rows that surface before `onStart`, because D-66 refuses to publish an end for a beginning the consumer has no record of; the suite asserts the absence for those rows and exactly one for the rest, so the split is executable. **F-76** — 07 §Validation attributes a throwing `visual` resolver to activation, and **both** behaviors resolve it during admission, so the code is `consumer` rather than `interaction`. Neither changes any code: in each case the shipped behavior is the intended one and the sentence is what disagrees.

**L-3 and L-4 needed a middle-tier installer to be falsifiable at all**, which is D-81/F-74 discharged rather than restated: the `bounds()`-based fixture is kept beside the discriminating one and asserts its own _non_-discrimination, so a later reader cannot mistake it for coverage.

**Size stayed telemetry.** No production module was added; the only source edit is a comment in `src/free-drag/bounds.ts`, and the stories file is not in the package's `files` allowlist. **F-65 and F-72 did not move** — the SPI is untouched and the duplicate activation traversal is still Checkpoint E's, measured in Phase 21.
