## Checkpoint E — cross-behavior architectural evidence

The checkpoint this roadmap exists to reach, and the one Part I could not have run.

**The claim under review: the kernel is behavior-agnostic.** Until now that has been a property of type signatures. With two real behaviors it becomes checkable:

- Does anything in `kernel/` know a collection, a placeholder or an insertion? Asserted, not read for.
- Do the two behaviors use the **same** seam set, or did one of them quietly get a seam the other ignores?
- Does the frame-part model hold at two part shapes — including where each sits against M-1's cliff?
- Is `draggable()` still genuinely behavior-agnostic at the type level with two inhabitants of `Behavior<Controller>`?
- Does the export topology hold, and can a consumer of one behavior still not reach the other's modules?
- Which Part I deviations were sortable-specific accommodations that a second behavior now shows to be general — or shows to be wrong?

**Form.** Owner review plus an independent audit, plus a closure assessment.

**Exit.** The behavior-agnosticism claim is either evidenced or corrected. **No refinement or cutover work starts before this exit** — refining an architecture whose central claim is unverified optimizes the wrong thing.

### Remediation — complete, 2026-08-16

The [independent audit](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-1-codex.md) raised six blockers and two evidence corrections. **Three needed a decision — D-85, D-86, D-87 — and three were implementation fixes under contracts that already said what to do**, which is itself the disposition rather than a deferral. All eight are closed in one pass.

- **E-01 / D-85 — one activation snapshot, not two.** `ActivationScope` carries `inheritedSpace`, derived from the measurement `acquireLift` already took before it mutated anything; `acquireLift` returns the session and that projection together. Free drag's `captureLocalSpace` and its four private box indices are deleted, and no module under `src/free-drag/` or `src/sortable/` names box-quad or a box index any more. **Filed by F-72 as overhead and closed as a correctness fix**: the deleted traversal ran after acquisition had changed positioning, dimensions, top-layer state and transforms, and the falsification is the artifact — the pre-fix tree reports `localDelta { x: 10, y: 7.5 }` against a stage that was `scale(2)` when the drag began.
- **E-02 — the final activation barrier, in both behaviors.** 07's terminal table claimed the reading and neither implementation had it: free drag read the latch after the `axis` source and then ran a third-party `constrain.apply`, the lift write and the progress advance; the sortable's `invalidateInSeam` reported whether its third-party call **threw**, never whether it had destroyed the controller. Both now read after the last contributed call. **Deliberately not extracted** — the two fixes having the same shape is not evidence of a common mechanism, and no liveness SPI was added.
- **E-03 — `reportFailure` after `destroy()`.** Guarded at the two kernel-owned call sites, `runAdmission`'s catch and `reportQuality`, because the rule is controller lifetime rather than domain settlement and both behaviors reach them identically.
- **E-04 / D-86 — per-tag action legality.** `TAG_POSITION` and `TAG_POLICY` are legal in `ACTIVATING` and `ACTIVE` and are deterministic no-ops after, through `prepare`'s existing `null` discard. The `onEnd` row is the discriminating one, and the sortable's late collection `invalidate()` **still works** — the row that would have failed had this been solved in the kernel's tag router.
- **E-05 — the `home` result is read, finiteness-checked and copied inside `anchorTarget`**, so the fault lands on the `FAILURE_LANDING_TARGET` quality track its attribution already named instead of panicking outside the seam.
- **E-06 / D-87 — two lines, one per direction.** `FreeDragContribution.insertion?: never` and `SortableContribution.constrain?: never`. Falsified by removing them, which turns three `@ts-expect-error` rows into unused directives. An installer returning `{}` stays assignable to both, asserted positively. **Evidence and prose closure, same pass.** Four gaps the remediation left, closed without touching production behavior: the E-03 **quality** route gained the permanent regression it was missing — a `home` resolver that destroys and then throws, asserted as no `onError` and no terminal, and falsified by removing `reportQuality`'s guard; E-04's landing-runner row now **holds `done()`** across a frame and a microtask drain, so it reads the rendered position while the runner still owns the transform instead of after the join has cleaned up behind a `moveTo()` that took full effect; E-07's two remaining accepted-drop landing-failure claims are corrected in 05 and 07 §The settlement's own failure, along with the obsolete `undefined` duration wording, since **an accepted free drag never arms a landing at all** and the executable preservation case is the committed rejected verdict; and E-08's isolated documentation gate is now the **exit code** under `--treatWarningsAsErrors` plus the emitted artifact's module list, rather than two greps over captured stdout that would have weakened silently on a reworded log line. The three older TypeDoc runs are deliberately left as they are — rewriting green instruments was not what the checkpoint asked about. The retired `FAILURE_REORDER_RESOLUTION` name is gone from `src/kernel/errors.ts` and the attribution claim there is narrowed to what the call sites support; **the 13-stage vocabulary is unchanged**, and a behavior-selected stage stays refused.

- **E-07 / E-08 — the evidence corrections.** The Infinity row is restated as preservation of a committed **rejected** verdict, in the test title, the ledger and B-4 (d); free drag gets the isolated documentation run B-3 asks for and did not have; and the failure-stage comment in `src/kernel/errors.ts` is narrowed to what the call sites support.

**F-65 did not move**, and is Phase 21's to measure — including beside D-85, whose unconditional kernel arithmetic is F-65's own complaint pointed the other way. No kernel SPI was generalized: the two activation barriers, the two contribution exclusions and the two report guards are each two instances of a rule rather than one mechanism, and F-64's judgement at two behaviors is unchanged.

**Gates.** typecheck clean, lint clean, `just test` 48 files / 986 passed / 25 skipped, up from 44 / 950; build clean at 131 files.

**Size, as telemetry.** minimal 10.70 → **10.74 kB** (+40 B), `minimal (xy)` 10.76 → **10.79** (+30), `+ layoutAnimation` 11.14 → **11.16** (+20), `+ landing` 10.98 → **11.02** (+40), complete 11.42 → **11.45** (+30), baseline A 11.13 → **11.16** (+30); baseline B unchanged at 6.89 kB. Module counts unchanged, and **`captureLocalSpace`'s deletion did not pay for the guards** — the sortable-only compositions grew too, which is the sortable's own activation barrier plus the two kernel report guards. The pre-existing budget overruns K-6 records as muted telemetry are unchanged in kind; the rule Phase 21 already carries applies unaltered — **a size budget is never a reason to defer a fix for a floor breach**, and five of these six fixes are floor breaches. The re-base is Phase 21's, on the artifact that will ship.

### Second remediation — complete, 2026-08-16

A [second independent audit](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-1-claude.md), run **after** the D-85…D-87 pass, found eleven items. Four needed a decision — **D-88…D-91** — five were mechanical, and two are recorded as evidence.

- **CE1-01 / D-88 — the exclusion rule was wrong, and this is the row to read.** D-87 recorded _one exclusion per direction is the minimum that works_ as sufficient. It was not: it closed the two _defining_ capabilities and left `placeholder` and both displacement hooks open, and what appeared to close **those** was TypeScript's weak-type detection, which `retire` — the one member both records genuinely share — defeats. A hoisted **unannotated** installer carrying `{ placeholder, retire }` compiled into free drag's public `plugins` and was silently discarded: the exact defect E-06 was raised about, surviving the decision that claimed to close it. The rule is now **key-set totality** — both records declare the same seven keys, a key a behavior does not implement it declares `?: never` — checked by a `keyof` equality assertion rather than promised as future work. **The suite was as much at fault as the type**: every row started from an annotated alias, the one form the old exclusions already caught, so the escaped form was untested exactly where the boundary escaped.
- **CE1-02 / D-89 — the accepted anchor no longer re-enters the constraint.** It re-derived the same numbers from the same committed frame _in order to_ call `constrain.apply`, while the seam's own comment claimed no consumer call, I-36's Category-1 table omitted the slot, and D-81's re-derived four-seam list missed it — with `host.closed` read before `home` and not before the derivation. Deleting the derivation makes all three true at once and takes the barrier defect with it, which beats declaring a fifth seam and owing it an attribution and a table row.
- **CE1-03 / D-90 — contribution members are invoked detached**, stated on `MotionConstraint` and made consistent at free drag's three sites. The tree was split three ways, so a `this`-reading constraint worked from `controller.invalidate()` and threw on the first scroll and at every retirement.
- **CE1-04 / D-91 — `moveTo(point)` requires two finite coordinates.** A non-finite one **discards** the action before anything is written and surfaces on the platform reporter; a malformed point is deliberately unchecked and still classifies. The asymmetry with E-05 had been decided by silence rather than judgement, and the difference that survives scrutiny is the blast radius, not the principle: `home` can only be refused by failing its seam, this one can be refused before it is written, and classifying it would end a live drag over a consumer's arithmetic.
- **CE1-05, CE1-06, CE1-09** are prose corrections — the `bounds` feature holds no liveness reading of its own, 05 still carried the `Partial`-fragment technique P18A-20 had struck in 07, and the `onEnd` barrier is kernel-side rather than behavior-side. **CE1-07** adds the sortable `visual` → `FAILURE_ADMISSION` regression D-84 stated for both behaviors and only free drag pinned. **CE1-08** deletes `FreeDragRuntime.item` / `.visual`, two fields nothing read, never cleared at retirement, carrying a comment naming consumers that use other sources.
- **CE1-10 and CE1-11 change nothing, deliberately.** The composition vocabulary measures thin — `realm` has one installer reader, `report` none, `root` no reader anywhere and a **different meaning per tier** — which is evidence _against_ widening F-64. And three of thirteen failure stages having a single behavior producer at a sample size of two behaviors is not a behavior-shaped vocabulary. **Neither is generalized.**

**Two contradictions raised rather than designed around.** The accepted anchor's value has **no public observable** — free drag never arms a landing for an accepted result, and the kernel's pin is followed immediately by presentation disposal — so 05's requested value control cannot be written for that arm; the `home` arms carry it instead, and the file says so. And the obvious _destroy from the resolver_ row for D-89 is **absent deliberately**: the kernel's own `settlementLive` check skips the arm, so `anchorTarget` is never called and the row passes with the defect restored.

**Falsified.** Removing the three exclusions turns the escaped-form rows into unused `@ts-expect-error` directives; removing the finiteness check fails both `moveTo` rows; restoring the derivation fails the anchor row — after its fixture was rebuilt around a **pending** resolver, because with a synchronous one the whole settlement runs inside `release()` and the first version could not discriminate at all.

**Gates.** typecheck clean, lint clean, `just test` 49 files / 1,004 passed / 25 skipped, up from 48 / 987.

### Closure — complete, 2026-08-19

Eight further passes followed the second remediation — [2-claude](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-2-claude.md), [2-codex](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-2-codex.md), [3-claude](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-3-claude.md), [3-codex](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-3-codex.md), [4](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-4-claude.md), [5](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-5-claude.md), [6](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-6-codex.md), [7](reviews/checkpoint-e/review-checkpoint-e-7-codex.md) — and from review 2 onward they converged on one seam: the **calling convention** shared by `MotionConstraint` and `InsertionGeometry`. They produced **D-92, D-93 and D-94** and closed CE4-01/CE4-02, CE5-01…CE5-04 and CE6-01/CE6-02.

**D-90 is the entry worth reading first, because it was recorded as implemented while neither deliverable was in the tree.** Reviews 2 and 3 found the contract section landed, the sentence **not** on the published type, and the mapped test built from a constraint whose members ignored `this` — so reverting all three sites left the suite green. That is what re-populated the deferred-decision table with a decision the ledger already called done, and what set the standard the four decisions below were held to: a witness that stops holding, and a falsifier run before the marker moves. **No runtime behavior changed in any of them** — every finding was a published guarantee that did not match the tree, the instruments, or the other tier.

- **D-92 — the convention is stated in both tiers.** D-90 chose detached over bound to avoid _two conventions in one package_, citing a sortable precedent that stated a **technique** and derived no author obligation; `InsertionGeometry` said nothing at all. One statement beside one silence is worse than two silences, because the reader has positive evidence of a distinction the package does not make. The sortable gained the sentence and a discriminating instrument across its five sites.
- **D-93 — the published guarantee is the receiver negative alone.** The two tiers had been stating it **differently**: the sortable published the obligation, free drag published the mechanism (_calls them as bare functions_), and only the first is true at every site — the construction unwind's `retireHooks[i]!()` is an indexed call and hands the hook the assembler's internal array. Free drag's `=== undefined` assertions narrowed to `receiver !== own()`, and both enumerations moved from four sites to **five**.
- **D-94 — the guarantee named the wrong object.** _Never your contribution object_ was **satisfied by the implementation it forbids**: an installer returns `{ constrain }` / `{ insertion }`, so a fully bound `contribution.constrain.apply(…)` hands the member the **nested** record and never the contribution object. Both declarations now state it self-referentially — _never invoked with this record as their receiver_ — and flattening, lift location and concrete receiver values survive only where labelled measured code or evidence, which is what makes Phase 21's permitted transformations legible.

**The instruments were right the whole time, and that is the finding worth carrying forward.** Both fixtures always typed `own()` as the nested capability record and compared against it; only the prose drifted, in the places nothing executes. Each decision was retired against a run rather than an inspection — the per-lift falsifiers measured **1 / 2 / 2** for free drag's `apply` / `invalidate` / `retire`, re-binding the sortable's four lifts failed six rows, and the referent correction was checked in both directions: binding to the nested record fails the rows, binding to the outer contribution leaves the suite green.

**Exit condition satisfied.** The behavior-agnosticism claim is evidenced, and where it was over-stated it was corrected rather than defended: the kernel knows no collection, placeholder or insertion; the two behaviors share one seam set; F-64's duplicated composition types stayed **deliberately un-unified**, because CE1-10 measured the shared vocabulary as thin — `report` has no installer reader, `root` has none anywhere and denotes a different element per tier — which is evidence against widening, not for it. No kernel SPI was generalized across the whole checkpoint: the two activation barriers, the two contribution exclusions and the two report guards remain two instances of a rule rather than one mechanism.

**Gates at closure** (`4f4fb679`): typecheck clean, `just test` **50 files / 1,016 passed / 25 skipped**, no type errors. Review 7 verified the closure textually and ran the decision instrument at 12; its browser gates were not independently re-run — local-browser permission review timed out — so the executable half of that pass rests on the owner-run suite above, which covers the same instruments unchanged.

**Phase 21 is unblocked.** F-65 and F-72 carry into it as measurement subjects exactly as recorded, and nothing in this closure changes their disposition.

## Phase 21 — Measurements over the complete package

**Scope.** Every M-1…M-4 number in Part I is a single-behavior number, measured on a workload that no longer represents the package. Re-run under the same reproducibility standard (05 §Measurements owed): workload and harness, named engines and versions, warm-up and GC policy, counts under test, sampling and statistics.

| # | What changes |
| --- | --- |
| M-1 | The move path at **two** part shapes, both placed against the 12→16-field cliff; free drag's clamp-before-write on the hot path; `onMove` as a per-sample consumer callback. |
| M-2 | Heap and call behaviour at realistic **mixed** controller counts, not one kind. |
| M-3 | Every composition again, plus the free-drag compositions and whatever the 2-D axis shipped as. Any new subpath changes what "minimal" means — 05 names this as a reopening trigger, and the whole graph-assertion half is re-declared. |
| M-4 | Re-check the shared layout read with two axis features rather than one. |

**The table above states each row's subject; [`measurements/phase-21.md`](measurements/phase-21.md) is the contract and states each row's question.** It withdraws four obligations that cannot change a decision — free drag's part against M-1's cliff (settled by counting: five part fields against eight), M-2's closure-vs-static-spec model comparison (two stand-ins that do not ship), M-4's _two axis features_ (not a constructible composition) and F-72 (closed by D-85, not deferred) — and adds **M-5**, the workload F-65 has been owed since Phase 18. D-95 argues the narrowing. **The M-4 replacement is the one to read**: q7's _no shared read phase_ was measured against a **lazy** axis rebuild, and Phase 11's own answer 1 then made the rebuild eager and interior to the committed-move bracket, which C4-03 recorded without a re-run.

**P-06 joins that backlog from Phase 21 itself** (D-98): the eager full-candidate rebuild is the dominant term of a committed move — ~97% of the bare `y()` bracket at 800 rows — and M-4′'s conclusion that its read set is entailed by the rule's domain does not hold. The index already serves cached geometry within a slot dwell, so correctness rests on invalidation completeness rather than freshness; and at a committed move the library already knows which rows moved, because it computes the span one statement earlier and hands it to `layoutAnimation()`. The candidate is a **smaller eager rebuild in the same window** — never a lazier one — bounded to `y()`, to compositions with no `box` resolver, to invalidations caused by a committed move, and owing a re-synchronisation policy for the drift the full scan currently absorbs.

**Plus the open optimization backlog** the Checkpoint B closure assessment assigned to a later measurement phase (`reviews/drag2-review-B-codex-2.md`): the per-sample visual render (P-01), the six-scalar vertical cache (P-02), placeholder geometry per resolve (P-03), overlapping activation geometry reads (P-04) and collection-update allocation (P-05). **Their state after Phase 21 (D-99): P-01 opens** on M-6; **P-02 splits** — its time half closes on M-4′ (~8% of the rebuild) and its **retention** half opens on M-2′ (6.29 MB at a 100 000-item high water); **P-03 closes** as an accepted ≈0.04 ms per frame; **P-04 and P-05 were never given a Phase 21 workload and are neither closed nor evidenced** — they stay backlog, and M-5's activation figures are context for P-04's low prior rather than a closure of it. Each was deferred on the reasonable grounds that a cost is not worth paying down before the workload is final. It is final here.

**These are the only Checkpoint B findings still open.** Two others that assessment listed as open were closed during Part I and are **not** backlog: **B-05** (landing coordinate semantics) was resolved in Phase 9, which made the delta space normative in 02 §Runner obligation and pinned it with exact values, and **M-06** (`PresentationView.insertion` lifetime) was closed by clearing the field on both bracket exits. They may be _revalidated_ under the expanded behavior set — a second behavior and a second axis both touch them — but they are revalidation, not unfinished work, and must not be re-presented as open findings.

**Re-base the size budgets rather than absorbing another change into them.** The M-3 budgets were set with roughly 0.3 kB of headroom — "about one module" — and it is now **0.11–0.16 kB**, tightest on `+ layoutAnimation` and `complete`, after Checkpoint D's 40 B, C2-01's 30–90 B and C4-01's 14–87 B. (This sentence read `0.16–0.21 kB` where the harness showed `0.17–0.23 kB`; corrected at review 4, C4-04, and re-measured with C4-01 in place.) Nothing is over budget and no single change was unreasonable; what has happened is that four small correct fixes have each been absorbed silently, and the next one has visibly less room than the one before it. Phase 21 re-bases against the measured artifact and says what the headroom is _for_, so that "inside budget" keeps meaning something. A budget that only ever ratchets down until something breaches it is a number, not a constraint. **The `FeatureContext.live` remedy is withdrawn from the queue** at Checkpoint D review 5 (C5-03): `landing()` is bracket-discharged, and the remedy would install a panic-blind reading beside a panic-aware tier-B bracket. That returns its **53 B** of forecast pressure to the floor fixes that needed it. **The re-base moved ahead of the C5 closure pass** rather than sitting after Checkpoint D, on the architect's recommendation and for the reason below, and is landed in `bench/size/measure.ts` with the measured figures.

**Revision 2 adds to this phase's subject rather than changing its method.** M-3 must be re-run over a composition surface D-45 reshapes — plain config fragments merged by slot, no feature brand, no `unbrandFeature`, and after D-55 no brand helpers at all — and **the subpath graph changes by four entries in both directions**: D-56 deletes `sortable/callbacks.js`, `sortable/handle.js` and `sortable/placeholder.js`, D-48 adds `kernel.js`, and the count goes 9 → 7. That is not an adjustment to the graph-assertion half of M-3; it re-declares it, because every composition name means something different. M-1's move path is untouched by Revision 2; M-2's per-controller heap gains a `Promise` allocation per `destroy()` under D-36, which is once per controller lifetime and not a hot-path number. **And probe A's spike is the size datum to carry in**: **+500/−491 lines across 13 source files** for the transaction bracket, so the bracket is a complexity and invariant win and **not** a size win, and nobody should arrive at M-3 expecting it to pay for itself.

**Check D-56 — a falsifiable prediction attached to a decision, and it was owed at Phase R, not here. ~~It is owed.~~ Withdrawn unsatisfied 2026-08-22 by the owner; the live record is O-8 in [`obligations.md`](obligations.md), and the paragraph below stands as the statement of what was predicted and why it can no longer be run.** D-56's stated reason for deleting `sortable/callbacks.js`, `sortable/handle.js` and `sortable/placeholder.js` is that under D-45 their factories were identity wrappers returning a public config slot — **a subpath carrying no runtime machinery**. That reason makes a measurable prediction:

> After D-56, the **minimal composition's byte count must not move.** The deletions must show up as three fewer entries in the graph and as **zero bytes**, in every composition that used them.

**If bytes move, the premise was wrong** — something lived in those modules that the D-45 argument said was not there — and the deletion needs re-justifying against whatever that is, rather than being ratified by a graph that got shorter. **This is a check on D-56, not a measurement note.** It runs at **Phase R**, where the deletion lands and the before/after are one commit apart, and it costs one `bench/size` run; deferring it to Phase 21 makes it archaeology across a second behavior and a re-based budget. A later phase may not record it as satisfied without the two numbers.

**The rule, recorded so it does not have to be re-argued: a size budget is never a reason to defer a fix for a floor breach. If a floor breach is found and its fix does not fit, the budget re-bases and the fix lands; what a budget may defer is defence in depth.** Review 4's third reason for deferring `FeatureContext.live` was a brotli delta inside a terminal-safety argument, and the fifth review then cited that deferral as part of what left I-36 undischarged. The first two reasons — bracket coverage and class closure — were sufficient on their own, and that is where the argument should have rested. **This rule was exercised immediately.** The C5 closure pass landed nine I-36 floor fixes; mid-pass they took `complete` 1 B over its 11,040 budget, and the budget re-based rather than the fix shrinking. Brotli then returned some of it as the repeated `rt.closed` guards started sharing a dictionary, so the landed cost is **+91 B** on `complete` (10,934 → 11,025). The re-base stays even though that figure would have fitted inside the old 11,040 budget: 15 B is not a margin the next correctness fix should be planning against.

**M-3′ ran 2026-08-19** — the first of the five, and the record is [`measurements/m3-prime.md`](measurements/m3-prime.md). The composition surface is re-declared at two behaviors: four free-drag rows, a combined both-behaviors row, and the cross-behavior absence claim asserted as a subtree prefix. **The topology question is answered and did not reopen** — the combined graph is exactly the union of the two single-behavior graphs, 46 modules against 46, with 16 shared and none duplicated, so D-48's `kernel.js` split holds at two behaviors. **The budget re-base this section promised is landed**: five sortable rows and baseline A had gone 247–407 B over, and every budget is now its measurement plus 150 B under one rule, with the headroom's purpose stated — it is sized to notice a module entering a graph and is deliberately too small to absorb a feature. M-4′, M-5 and M-2′ are unrun, and §Check D-56 stays Phase R's.

**M-1′ ran the same day** — the record is [`measurements/m1-prime.md`](measurements/m1-prime.md). **Three of its four decisions close and the fourth is raised rather than answered.** The shared publication site needs no shape-stable design for two behaviors: against an alternation-matched control the ratio is 0.87, and correcting the residual field-count bias with the monomorphic figures puts the shape-count effect at 1.02. M-1's 6× is corrected in the same session by re-running its own 3/8/20 scenario — it was measuring its own largest frame across the cliff, and arm A finds that cliff unmoved. The lazy resolve holds its contract: one resolve per `apply` at every `k`, now asserted on every suite run and falsified against an eagerly-resolving `invalidate()`, so **frame coalescing is withdrawn rather than deferred**. I-26's claim survives at the second behavior — no retention across a collected pair, a tail inside the control's spread — with its tier unmoved, because nothing in the arm can move it. The constraint call and clamp are a one-tick null. **No Phase 22 optimization opens.** **P-01 does not close**: the deciding quantity is samples committed per frame under real high-rate input, which this harness's batched dispatch cannot produce, and the candidate closure on D-96's own arithmetic is a contract decision rather than this run's. **D-97 took it and refused the closure**: a write gate is not a correctness change the way the bounds gate was — only the last transform before a paint is presented — so P-01 cannot inherit that withdrawal, and D-96's _about one sample per frame_ turns out to be Chromium dispatch policy rather than structure, which suspends the within-frame half of its own gate withdrawal. **P-01 is owed M-6**, a census of `lift.write` and `constrain.apply` per rAF tick under input injected through the browser's input pipeline, with a coalescing control so a too-slow injector cannot read as a bound. The cost of one write is not measured unless the census opens the question.

**M-6 ran 2026-08-20 and P-01 opens** — the record is [`measurements/m6.md`](measurements/m6.md). The validity control earned its place by failing first: Playwright's sequential mouse path emits ~59 movements per second, below the display rate, never coalesces, and returns one write per tick — recorded as **inconclusive**, with none of its counts used. Issued without waiting for each acknowledgement the same `page.mouse` path reaches ~129/s and ~309/s, where a quarter to a half of dispatches carry more than one coalesced movement, and there the census reads **writes p95 = 3 and 8** with maxima of 7 and 10. So Chromium coalesces a fast stream **and still delivers several `pointermove` dispatches inside one rendering opportunity**, and `constrain.apply` exceeds one per tick on the same arms — which **closes D-96's suspended within-frame half** as well. The across-frames withdrawal stands on its own ground: a held rect moves the visual. A consumer's own `moveTo()` traffic is kept separate and classified rather than gated — one call per rendering opportunity yields exactly one write, bursts of 2/8/32 yield exactly 2/8/32, and that surplus is a documented consequence of a rate the consumer chose. **Phase 22 owns the gate, including the flush obligation on every terminal path; no gate is implemented.**

**M-4′ ran the same day** — the record is [`measurements/m4-prime.md`](measurements/m4-prime.md). ~~**All three of its decisions close and none of them opens a Phase 22 optimization.**~~ **Superseded by D-98**: questions (a) and (b) close as written, and question (c) opens **P-06**. The rest of this paragraph's figures are unaffected. The eager rebuild is the dominant term of one committed move — 80–97% of it in a bare composition at every size, 36–69% with `layoutAnimation()` composed and rising with the list — and `xy()` pays exactly what `y()` pays. The bracket's read placement is now exact and asserted on every suite run: the resolve reads one element, `beforeMove` reads the two-row span, the placeholder write reads **nothing**, the rebuild reads **n − 1**, and `afterMove` reads the same two; **it is recorded as a read count and never as a forced-layout count**, and the falsifier that injects reads into each hook caught a defect in the instrument before any number was quoted. **P-02 does not open**: measured against a stride-1 alternative composition whose committed slot sequence is asserted identical first, the narrower cache saves ~8% of the rebuild at 800 rows and nothing resolvable below it — because q7's finding holds on the real rebuild, the reads dominate and the stores do not. **P-03 does not open either, and the arm that settled it corrected this run's own first reading**: on a committing frame the clean-resolve placeholder read looked like a 0.3 ms per-frame tax, but a drag spends most of its frames committing nothing, and on those it costs **≈0.04 ms flat in list size and flat in composition** — 0.2–0.3% of a frame. Both close as accepted, named costs. **The eager position was not re-decided and no evidence here bears on it**; P-02's retention half is a heap claim and stays with M-2′. M-5 and M-2′ are unrun, and §Check D-56 stays Phase R's.

**M-5 ran 2026-08-20 and closes F-65** — the record is [`measurements/m5.md`](measurements/m5.md). It is the finding two documents and a checkpoint booked to a measurement phase without ever owing it a workload, and it now has one. At a 32-level ancestry with a distinct non-translation transform on every level, **D-52's window 1 has no resolvable cost in situ**: the median of 21 paired differences straddles zero across five runs, bounded at **≤ 0.0125 ms** and **≤ 2.7%** of measured activation, and a direct measurement of the same sequence supplies the magnitude the null cannot — **0.8–1.6 µs**. The mechanism is in the source rather than in the number: `acquireActivation` reads the origin rect on the statement **before** window 1, so its two offset reads are served from a layout that has just been flushed, which is why the cost stays flat while measured activation grows six-fold from shallow to deep. Both legs of the pre-declared conjunction are satisfied by two orders of magnitude, so **F-65 closes as an accepted, named cost and D-82's deferred `ActivationScope` question does not open.** **Arm B confirms the ground D-85 was accepted on and could not have done otherwise**: the `inheritedSpace` derivation costs **≤ 6 ns** per activation and performs **zero** layout-facing reads, with its harness copy held to an in-situ equivalence check against the coefficients the kernel's own `compose` encodes before any figure is quoted from it. **No SPI moves, nothing is implemented, and no Phase 22 candidate comes out of this run.** M-2′ is the last unrun measurement, and §Check D-56 stays Phase R's.

**M-2′ ran the same day and is the last of the five** — the record is [`measurements/m2-prime.md`](measurements/m2-prime.md). **Per-controller cost is predominantly common rather than behavior-specific**, which is what Phase 22 needed before it reads the two surfaces as one: at 1000 controllers a sortable retains **5618 B** and a free drag **4505 B**, so the sortable's behavior adds **1112 B — 20%** — and the other **80%** is carried by both. The common figure is a **bound** on the kernel's share and is written as one, because free drag's own behavior cost sits inside it; the exact quantity is the difference, since the kernel and the DOM cancel out of it. **The additivity check holds to the byte** — a mixed population is predicted at 5062 B and measured at 5062 B in all three runs — so nothing is shared between controllers and population heap is linear in the count. **Retention over the declared workload does not grow with the drag count**: the smallest workload gives the largest reading in every run, which a per-drag leak cannot do, and the arm reports its own measured sensitivity (a deliberately injected 781 kB reads back as 109 kB) rather than asserting one. **P-02's retention half opens** — 6.29 MB retained at a 100 000-item high water, exactly the figure P-02 claimed, of which **5.12 MB is scalars no axis rule reads** — and it is a different quantity from the time half M-4′ closed: space rather than time, retained rather than transient, scaling with the high water rather than with the current collection. **The frame-task policy is not re-opened and no figure here bears on it** (D-96). **Phase 21 closes with three Phase 22 candidates — P-01, P-06 and P-02's retention half — and no optimization work started.** §Check D-56 stays Phase R's.

**Done when.** Every number carried in a contract document, a README or a size budget is either re-measured or explicitly re-affirmed with its workload named.

---

## Phase 22 — The refinement pass

**Scope.** Performance, bundle structure, public API and maintainability — the pass Part I never had, because there was never a complete artifact to refine.

**Deliverables.**

- **Performance**: act on Phase 21. A measurement that changes nothing was either already optimal or was not worth taking; say which.

### The performance entry, decided from Phase 21's evidence (D-99)

**Three candidates, and no fourth.** Phase 21 produced exactly three optimization candidates with measurements behind them. **The ≈80% of per-controller retained heap that is common to both behaviors is prioritisation context, not a candidate** — it says a kernel-level saving pays twice and a behavior-level one pays once, and no measured problem attaches to it. P-04 and P-05 remain un-evidenced backlog. **Nothing here may start without the step named in its _next step_ column completing first.**

|  | **P-06** — smaller eager rebuild | **P-02 retention** — spatial-buffer shape | **P-01** — visual-write coalescing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Payoff, as measured** | The rebuild is **80–97%** of a bare committed move and **36–69%** with `layoutAnimation()`, rising with `n`; ~3.5 ms per move at 800 rows. The candidate replaces an `O(n)` rescan with `O(distance)` arithmetic, so most of that is removable | **Exact and structural**: 6.29 MB retained at a 100 000-item high water, **5.12 MB** of it scalars no axis rule reads. Scales with the high water, not the live collection, and is held for the controller's lifetime | **Real and unquantified.** Arm 1's platform surplus is **p95 3–8** writes per rendering opportunity, so the saving is `(writes − 1) × one write` — with **one write never measured** |
| **Invariant / contract cost** | **The highest of the three, and D-98 fixes it**: reason-aware invalidation (widening `invalidateInsertion`, today one flag documented as _any single reason forces the same full rebuild_), `box === visual` only, `y()` only, and an explicit **re-synchronisation policy** for the drift the full scan currently absorbs. The **eager window is preserved** — a smaller rebuild in the same place, never a lazier one | **The smallest of the three, and it splits.** A **shrink policy** is local to a private module — `retire()` already empties `items` and keeps `values`; nobody else can name the buffer (H-4) — and touches no SPI. **Narrowing the stride is not local**: `RectIndex` is deliberately dimension-neutral so `y()` and `xy()` are two _rules_ over one cache, and a narrower stride re-couples them. **Keep the two apart** | **High, and it is a lifecycle cost rather than a type cost.** A deferred write owes a **flush on every terminal path** — release, cancel, destroy, landing hand-off — or the visual is left behind the operation. That obligation must survive into any design that is ever attempted |
| **Remaining evidence gap** | **None that is measurable.** The gap is architectural: the four boundary conditions have to become a contract. The one-span-row scalar delta stays an **existence proof that the boundary is reachable**, not an accepted design | **Deployment shape.** 6.29 MB needs a 100 000-item collection; a 1000-item list retains 48 kB, of which 40 kB is unread. Whether that is worth a policy is a question about the collections consumers actually have, and no Phase 21 arm asked it | **The cost of one `lift.write`, and the gate's own overhead.** M-6 was a count experiment by construction and deliberately measured no duration |
| **Next step** | **Architecture** | **Architecture** (choose the sub-candidate and the deployment premise), then implementation for the shrink half | **Measurement**, and it is likely dispositive — see below |
| **Stop condition** | Any boundary that cannot be stated as a contract — most likely the re-sync policy. If drift correction cannot be given a rule better than _rescan sometimes_, decline: an occasional full rescan with unpredictable timing is worse than a predictable one | If the largest collection a supported consumer plausibly holds keeps the unread retention **under ~100 kB per controller**, decline both halves — the figure is real but is not a problem, and `retire()` keeping the buffer is a deliberate reuse | **Decline if the surplus is worth under ~1% of a 16 ms frame.** At p95 3–8 the surplus is 2–7 writes; 1% of a frame is 0.16 ms, so declining follows unless one write costs **≳ 23–80 µs**, which a single `style.transform` assignment does not plausibly reach |

**On P-01, the arithmetic already predicts the answer and that is the point of taking the measurement.** `lift.write` is one `visual.style.transform` assignment; repeated assignments inside a frame overwrite a property that is read once at paint, so the marginal cost of the second through eighth write is a CSSOM set and little else. **Measure it to confirm a decline, not to discover a saving** — and take that measurement _before_ any gate is designed, because designing first is how the flush obligation gets paid for a benefit that was never there. **Arm 2's `moveTo()` surplus contributes nothing to this verdict**: it tracks the consumer's call rate 1:1, it is created by a rate the consumer chose, and it is documented as a consequence of the `moveTo()` contract rather than gated.

**P-02's shrink half is designed and is D-104** — [`p02-retention-shrink.md`](p02-retention-shrink.md). It is one gated branch beside the existing growth branch in `RectIndex.refresh`, and the table's 6.29 MB is **not** its payoff: at a stable collection the gate does not fire and that buffer is retained correctly. It recovers historical excess only, its real payoff is the unmeasured half of D-99's deployment-shape gap, and the stride sub-candidate stays undesigned. **Measured, earned and landed 2026-08-21**: the gate behaves exactly as derived, a supported workload reaches a payoff above D-99's threshold, and the branch shipped — see the closing paragraphs of this section. **P-06's architecture is done and is D-100** — the design is [`p06-verified-refresh.md`](p06-verified-refresh.md), and it survives as a **verified** incremental refresh: the span is a hypothesis four witness reads check, not state the feature trusts. Reason-aware invalidation turned out to cost no SPI at all, and the contract cost is one additive view field. **Order: P-06, then P-02's shrink half, then P-01.** Not by raw number — P-06 also happens to be the largest, but it leads because its uncertainty is **architectural rather than empirical**: it needs a decision, not another run, and Phase 21 has already supplied every number it will get. P-02's shrink half follows because it is the cheapest thing in the package to get right — one private module, no SPI, an exact figure — and its only open question is a premise about consumers rather than a property of the code. P-01 is last and is expected to be declined; it is kept on the list so the decline is recorded with a number rather than assumed.

**What Phase 22 may not do with this table.** Treat P-06's measured payoff as licence to relax its boundary; quote P-02's 6.29 MB without the high-water condition that produces it; or start a P-01 gate before the write cost is known.

**P-06 landed on 2026-08-21**, as D-100 designed it: the eager window, all eight gates, the full rebuild as fallback, `k = 8`, `y()` and default-box only, `xy()` untouched, and the permanent equivalence assertion beside it rather than after it. The record — the tree, the mutation table that shows the assertion discriminates, the paired before/after in both regimes, and the two places the tree differs from the design — is [`p06-verified-refresh.md`](p06-verified-refresh.md) §What landed.

**Three things the run settled that this table could not.** The **`count` contingency did not fire**: at 50 rows the verified path is 1.62× (bare) and 1.22× (animated) faster against a 0.0006 ms resolution, so no count gate exists and **no threshold was invented**. The **realised saving is ~2.6× at 800 rows, and `k` is not what caps it** — a verified move costs ≈1.0 ms of the general path's 3.5 ms while doing four reads instead of 799, because the rebuild's dominant term in the deployed regime is the **forced layout after the placeholder write** (≈1 ms at 800 rows) rather than the per-row reads (≈3 µs each). And the fast path **cost `xy()` bundle it can never execute** — 288 B on the minimal `xy()` composition, because the code lived in the shared `createRectIndex` closure. **D-101 and D-102 settled both divergences the same day and the implementation was finished against them**: the bare `__DEV__` read is ratified and now gated (one binding per tier, read once, `sortable/verified-refresh.ts` and `kernel/dev.ts` the only two), and the fast path moved into a module only `y()` reaches. **`minimal (xy)` is back to 10 787 B, byte-identical to before P-06, and the residue left in `RectIndex` is zero** — that file is byte-for-byte its pre-P-06 self. The budgets are re-based only on that condition being met; `xy()` and free drag are unchanged.

**The `k` invitation is withdrawn.** The ceiling is `full / verified` ≈ 3.5×, not `k×`; `k = 8` reaches 2.67×, which is 76% of what is achievable, against ~12% more for `k = 16` and ~24% less for `k = 4`. Raising it spends drift tolerance for very little, and `k = 8` stays.

**That last observation is a candidate this table does not contain and Phase 22 may not silently adopt.** The forced flush is now the dominant term of the committed-move bracket, and it is neither P-06's, nor P-02's, nor P-01's. It is un-evidenced beyond the two columns above, and it belongs in the same state P-04 and P-05 are in: named, unmeasured, and not started.

**P-02's shrink half ran second, per D-99's order, and is earned** — record [`measurements/p02-shrink.md`](measurements/p02-shrink.md). **The policy is exactly as sound as D-104 derives**: at a stable 100 000 items over 1000 drags it never reallocates, a real shrink reallocates exactly once, and a shrink frees a thousand times what it allocates.

**A first pass declined it and was overturned in review, on the interval it had skipped.** That decline bounded the drivable collection at ≈3 800 rows using M-4′'s **general** rebuild curve — measured before P-06, on the path P-06 replaced — and jumped from 2 000 items to 20 000, never testing the **2 049** bucket boundary where the buffer is already 192 KiB. Measured on the current tree, one committed move at 2 100 rows costs **5.70 ms bare and 5.74 ms with `layoutAnimation()`** — about a third of a frame — and 6.21/8.35 ms at 3 000, over 60 real frames each. **Extrapolating a post-optimization bound from a pre-optimization curve is the error, and it is worth naming as its own class.**

**Every firing from the 4096 bucket clears D-99's ~100 kB.** The gate needs `4096 > 4 × n`, so it fires only below 1 024 items and `capacityFor(1023)` is 1 024: the smallest reclaim available is **144 KiB** and the largest **186 KiB**, while nothing from the 2048 bucket clears the threshold at any destination. **The smallest earning workload**, driven end to end on one live controller: a collection reaching **≥ 2 049 items**, a drag at that size, republication below **1 024** through `items()` + `controller.invalidate()`, and a **subsequent drag at the smaller size** — the only moment the policy can fire, because `refresh` runs only inside an operation and an idle shrink reads zero geometry. A filtered or searchable reorderable list of a few thousand rows is that shape.

**And it landed the same day**, as the slice and nothing else: `STRIDE` stays 6, `retire()` is unchanged, `xy()` and `verified-refresh.ts` are untouched, and there is no timer, idle hook or retire-time prediction. **One divergence from the designed slice**, and it is D-104's own sentence applied — growth and shrink shipped as **one** branch with two triggers rather than as an `else if` beside the growth one, because they _are_ one decision about one resource, and because that shape gives the **`n = 0` correction** its natural home: the gate reads `capacity > 0` on an empty collection, which the one-slot buffer a previous empty refresh just produced also satisfies, so without an inner settle guard it would reallocate 48 B forever. **Cost: +34 B on the `y()` compositions and +14 B on `minimal (xy)`** — inside the ~150 B headroom on every row, so no budget moves. `xy()` pays its 14 B correctly: unlike P-06 this is a property of the dimension-neutral cache both axes share.

**The falsifiers stay load-bearing and now drive the shipped cache.** Five mutations of the landed branch are each caught, the loudest being a shrink that reuses the old buffer instead of rescanning — 126 tests across the sortable suite. **P-02's stride half is not closed by this** — 5.12 MB of unread scalars is a different quantity with a different candidate behind it.

**P-01 ran third and last, per D-99's order, and is declined — D-105, record [`measurements/p01-write-cost.md`](measurements/p01-write-cost.md), reviewed at [`reviews/phase-22/p01-review-claude.md`](reviews/phase-22/p01-review-claude.md).** The point of taking it was to record the decline with a number rather than an assumption, and the number turned out to disagree with the assumption while agreeing with the verdict.

**D-99 predicted the decline from the marginal cost of a repeated same-frame assignment — _a CSSOM set and little else_ — and that is measured at 0.485–0.489 µs.** It is the most reproducible figure in the file and it prices a regime a free drag never enters. **The deployed path issues one visual write per input dispatch**, and the browser cleans the style tree to hit-test the next one, so every in-situ write is a clean-tree write and costs an order more. **That structural fact is now held by a permanent row** — at most one write per dispatch, which fails if a behavior ever writes twice per sample — rather than living only in an opt-in report.

**The decline is argued from the typical frame at M-6's primary pace, which reproduces at 17.9–25.3% of the 160 µs bar across thirteen runs** — roughly a fourfold margin. **The wave-4 figures corroborate the direction and are not the ground**: arm E's null control reaches 8.46 µs against a 15 µs signal there, against ≲1.4 µs against 22–29 µs at the primary pace. **The gate's runtime cost is not what declines it** — 0.023–0.042 µs per request, break-even at 1.2–1.3 writes. **What declines it is the terminal-flush obligation**, which three structural rows prove exists and cannot be made free; how many paths a real gate would owe is a question for a design that does not exist. **No production gate was written**, which is what D-99 required.

**The review's correction is worth carrying past this candidate.** The verdict held, and every figure quoted around it had been quoted to a precision the instrument did not have — a three-run range printed as a bound, twice, in a record whose own stated subject is arithmetic that reads as fact.

**The error class is P-02's, met a second time in the same phase.** P-02 bounded a collection from a rebuild curve measured on the path its own optimization replaced; P-01 priced a write from a regime its own deployment never enters. Both read as arithmetic and are assumption, and Phase 22 has now produced one of each.

**Phase 22's performance entry is closed: P-06 landed, P-02's shrink half landed, P-01 declined.** What remains open is what was open before and is untouched by these three — P-02's **stride** sub-candidate, the committed-move **forced flush** the P-06 run surfaced, and P-04/P-05. None has a workload, and none may be started on the strength of a figure taken for another.

- **Bundle structure**: composition costs across the full topology, and whether the subpath set is still the right shape with two behaviors and two axes.
- **API**: the whole public surface read as one, by someone using it — naming consistency across the two behaviors, option-domain consistency, error vocabulary, and the migration table's honesty.
- **Maintainability**: the standing findings — duplicated SPI types and global control-flow shape (M-02) — plus whatever Checkpoint E surfaced.

### The bundle-structure entry, closed 2026-08-22 (D-106)

**The record is [`bundle-structure.md`](bundle-structure.md), re-measured from a fresh build rather than reconstructed from published deltas.** P-06 and P-02's shrink each recorded a per-row delta at landing, and a delta recorded at landing is a claim about the tree at landing; M-3′ is the baseline, but no figure here is M-3′ plus arithmetic.

**All twelve declared rows are under budget, the union identity holds at 47 = 47, nothing is emitted into two chunks, and every absence assertion passes.** That includes the `xy()`/P-06 exclusivity claim — the one assertion in this harness that has actually failed, held red by D-102 and then satisfied — which is the only evidence available that the instrument would catch the next topology mistake.

**The subpath set is affirmed unchanged.** Ten runtime entries, each carrying machinery a composition either imports or does not; two type-only entries that measure nothing and say so, which D-56's rule admits by name. Nothing here argues for adding a subpath and nothing argues for removing one.

**The three-root topology's own standing doubt closes, and it closes on a measurement rather than an argument.** 03 §The export topology says of it: _the topology has never been checked against a consumer who did not already believe it._ `drag.js` bundles to **121 B and one module** — a consumer who wants `err instanceof DraggableError` pays that and not the 6.51 kB kernel — so D-48's shared-vocabulary root is now a measured property. **Both roots were unmeasured until this pass**, and the assertion gap that left was **F-77** rather than a byte problem. **F-77 closed 2026-08-22**: both roots are declared compositions, the graph half needed a new `only` capability, the byte half needed a budget _narrowed_ to 29 B because the packed `errors.js` inlines the `FAILURE_*` constants, and each of the two predicted regressions was injected and caught by exactly one of the two halves. No production code changed.

**Where the fixed cost lives is the substantive result, and it is not a defect.** The cross-behavior shared set is **16 modules**, and across the two complete compositions ~7.6 kB of content is counted twice — ~57% of a two-behavior page. **A free-drag-only bundle is bounded rather than split**: it strictly contains the twelve-module, **6,514 B `draggable` floor, so ~75% of it is kernel** and its eleven-module free-drag layer is **≤ 2,203 B**. **This corroborates M-2′ from an independent direction**: ~80% of per-controller retained _heap_ is common, ~75% of shipped _bytes_ is common. Different quantities, same structure — the kernel is the artifact and the behaviors are thin. **It stays what D-99 called it: prioritisation context, not a candidate.**

**A first version of that paragraph reported ~87% and _~1.1 kB of free drag_, and it was wrong** — corrected 2026-08-22 on challenge, [`bundle-structure.md`](bundle-structure.md) §The free-drag layer. It subtracted a sixteen-module shared estimate from `free drag minimal`, which holds fifteen of them, treated a non-additive figure as an exact term, and produced a residual describing the **minimal** layer while reading as though it described all free-drag code. **The layer's honest figures are marginals against a stated base**: the complete thirteen-module layer costs **1,546 B** added to a sortable page and the minimal eleven-module layer **1,396 B**, while the same two optional modules cost 445 B standalone and **150 B** on a page already carrying `shared/landing-runner.js`. **That 445-versus-150 gap is why no context-free _the free-drag layer costs X_ exists**, and the error class is the phase's third of the same kind — P-02 bounded a collection from a superseded curve, P-01 priced a write from a regime its deployment never enters, and this divided a bundle that does not divide.

**No size candidate is earned, and the reason is uniform.** What is material is not locally removable; what is locally removable is immaterial or refused by a standing decision. The kernel floor is the library, and `draggable`-alone is not a deployment — the effective floor is 14 modules, not the 11 that row reports. Keyboard ingress and `input-policy` are an accessibility position and a safety position, and **a bundle pass may not reopen either on byte grounds**. `box-quad` is not ours and its placement is already asserted. `sortable/spec.js` splits into a ~60 B module boundary that buys nothing tree-shakeable. **No production code changed, and the deliverable was explicit that none should.**

**The budgets do not re-base.** Slack is 114–154 B against the ~150 B convention, and the drift is one landed change — P-02's +34 B on the `y()` rows, which added no module and was therefore absorbed under M-3′'s own rule rather than in spite of it. The instrument's sensitivity where it matters has not moved: the two modules that have ever appeared cost ~361–388 B and ~60 B, and at both 150 B and 117 B of slack the first trips the budget and the second does not. Re-basing on a schedule would invert D-102's ratified instinct that **an absorbed number is a number nobody reads again**. **The re-base conditions are stated rather than left to be re-derived**: a row goes negative, erosion stops being attributable to a named landed change, or **L-11 lands** — 05 §Measurements owed makes the frozen export map part of M-3, so L-11 re-measures M-3 whether or not it moves a byte. That is Phase 23's, not this phase's.

**Two contract questions close with it.** 03 §What isolation cannot shake asked for the composition plumbing to be weighed and had never had an answer: it costs **283 B, 2.4% of `complete`**, and across three readings and two feature additions it has gone 266 → 289 → 283 B. **The stability is the answer rather than the size** — an overhead that grew with the feature count would be an argument against the composition model. And 03 §Tree-shaking was still publishing its 2026-08-08 table, five sortable rows and a headroom sentence two re-bases stale, while 05 pointed at it as the live location; it now carries the full topology.

**The optimization question was asked separately, and it closes with no candidate (D-107).** D-106 closed this entry on accounting and structural verification — budgets, graph identities, attribution — and **none of that is a search for something to remove**, so the search was run afterwards on its own terms and **by ablation**: neutralise the candidate in the emitted code, re-compress, take the delta. Counting source characters would have priced every finding several times too high.

**The one material non-structural cost is error message text — 741 B on `complete`, 6.3%**, larger than any optional feature this package ships and larger than P-06's whole module. **It splits three ways and all three are declined.** The **author-facing** half is ~446–471 B on every composition and is two sites, not one: `validateFramePart` (~276–300 B) has explicit cover — contract 04 names it a **production** check because _its failure mode is silent state corruption_, and review 4 §28 recorded the trade — while `arm()`'s spec validation (~152–161 B) has **none on record**, sits four lines above a `DEV`-guarded assert, and was the only live-looking candidate in the sweep. **Both fall to one argument that came out of trying to remove them.** `kernel/dev.ts` justifies stripping author-facing assertions on the premise that _behavior authoring is not on the public surface_, and names its own revisit condition — _the day it is, this needs revisiting_. **That day passed at Revision 2.1**: D-61, D-70 and D-68 published the middle and kernel tiers precisely so a third party can author features and behaviors. Gating these blocks would strip validation from a **published** authoring API in the exact build a third-party author ships. **The real finding is F-78 — `dev.ts`'s revisit condition fired and nothing noticed** — and it points away from a size candidate: its own named remedies, a dual build under an `exports` condition or a per-site `process.env.NODE_ENV` comparison, cost the production bundle nothing and belong to the **API** deliverable. The **runtime and consumer-callback** half is 424 B and is declined on judgment: D-64 narrowed the consumer to a coarse `code` with detail on the `cause`, so stripping that message leaves `presentation` and nothing else in the one situation the design exists for. **What the sweep bought is a price for positions that had never been costed, and one expired premise.**

**Four sweeps found nothing and are recorded so nobody re-runs them**: `__DEV__` folds completely; `box-quad` shakes correctly, with the three exports drag2 never imports verifiably absent; the largest single literal in the bundle is a documented correctness requirement that **the minifier has already optimally encoded**; and cross-behavior duplication is real but immaterial — the second copy of every duplicated block totals **~187 B of 11,656, 1.6%**, and **0 B for any consumer bundling one behavior**, which the compression ratios corroborate (free drag ships alone at 2.91:1 and adds to a sortable page at **6.33:1**). **Two real costs are recorded as out of scope**: 54.1% of the published JavaScript is comments — 114,914 B of 212,229 B — which costs the bundle **exactly zero** and is a tarball question, and `box-quad` declares no `"sideEffects": false`, which costs nothing under Rolldown or Vite and matters only to a webpack consumer. **The large costs are intrinsic**: three modules are 47% of own code, and they are the kernel state machine and the two behavior implementations.

**The sweep corrected the pass that preceded it, and this is the fourth instance of one error class.** D-106 ranked modules by Rolldown's `renderedLength`, which counts JSDoc; comment density here varies by an order of magnitude, so the proxy **overstated every declined cost 3–8× and misordered the floor**. Keyboard is 221 B Brotli rather than ~1,534 B rendered; `input-policy` 234 B rather than ~4,030 B. **The declines stand — each was made on a decision, not a size — but a reader would have concluded large savings were being left on the table.** P-02's superseded curve, P-01's unreachable regime, the free-drag division and this are the same mistake four times: **a proxy quoted where the quantity was meant.**

**What Phase 22 may not do with this entry.** Read the 16-module shared set as a candidate — D-99 already refused it as one, and a second dimension of the same finding is still the same finding. Treat 114 B of slack as a reason to re-base, or as a reason to decline a correctness fix. Or close F-77 by widening a budget instead of adding the assertion, which would answer a graph question with a byte. **(F-77 closed 2026-08-22, and this warning held: the assertion was added and the budget was narrowed to 150 B. What the pass also found is that the graph question alone could not be answered by a graph — the packed `errors.js` inlines the `FAILURE_*` constants, so one of the two predicted regressions moves no module at all and only the tightened budget sees it.)**

**One correction and one dangling reference came out of the reading.** M-3′'s shared-set breakdown attributed an external package and `kernel.js` to the `kernel/` subtree — total right, breakdown wrong, corrected in place. And **`05 §What would reopen this` does not exist**: it is cited as the authority for _adding a subpath reopens what "minimal" means_ in §Phase 17 above and in `bench/size/measure.ts`, and the doctrine is real — 05 §Measurements owed carries it — but the heading was never written. **Not fixed here**, because one citation is in a source file and re-pointing it is not this deliverable's to land; the next pass over 05 either writes the section or re-points both. **Closed 2026-08-22 by D-112**, which found that _the next pass over 05_ was never a destination at all: both citations now read `05 §Measurements — landed 2026-08-02`, and `tests/references.node.test.ts` fails on the next reference that resolves to nothing.

### The API entry, decided 2026-08-22 (D-108 … D-111)

**The record is [`api-surface.md`](api-surface.md)**, read off a fresh `clean-build` and off `package.json` rather than off the ledger. The question is not what the surface costs — that closed at `d949cfeb` — but what it promises, and whether the promise is the one the record states.

**The generated half of the package contract is sound and the hand-maintained half has drifted.** `exports` is regenerated from `files.json` byte-for-byte by a repo-wide script, so subpath style, condition order and the two lists' set-equality **cannot** drift, and all 24 targets resolve on disk. `package.json` `files` is the one hand-maintained list, and it is where every packaging defect in this pass landed — which is what one would predict, and worth saying because it means the fix is not more vigilance.

**The four recorded items split two and two.** **F-78 is real** and neither remedy `kernel/dev.ts` names survives contact with the built tree (D-108). **`drag.js.map` is a stale line rather than a build defect** — a pure re-export chunk has no mappings, so rolldown emits neither map nor comment — but asking why it was missing found **F-79**, which is not cosmetic. **The `kernel.d.ts` 35-against-33 is a stale record and the rule held**: values match 33 = 33 exactly, and both extra types were ratified separately, by 07 §K-1 and D-85, with neither updating the total. **Revision 2.1's boundaries verified and hold** — three tiers over three roots, the type-only entries correctly carrying `types` and no `default`, no `"."` entry and the bare specifier correctly failing.

**F-78's remedy is neither of the two `dev.ts` names, and the reason is structural.** A `development` **`exports` condition is invisible to this package's own graph**: every internal edge is a relative specifier, and `sortable.js` reaches the kernel as `import { draggable } from './kernel.js'`, which never consults the map. A kernel-tier author composing `sortable()` would load **two kernel graphs** and therefore **two `DraggableError` class identities** — and `drag.js` exists precisely so `err instanceof DraggableError` works at both tiers. Making it safe needs a complete parallel tree on all ten runtime subpaths. The per-site `process.env.NODE_ENV` form is a **`ReferenceError` on the production path** for a `platform: 'neutral'` consumer with no `process`, and `dev.ts` has already ruled out the `typeof` guard that would prevent it. **D-108 un-gates instead**, on the fact neither remedy considered: **the whole set is four sites and every one is cold** — once per `arm()`, twice per drag at retirement, once per transaction open, and one failure path. The per-frame dev work is the sortable's own flag in its own tier under D-101, and it stays. **Contract 04's own discriminator already classifies these as production checks**, `validateFramePart` is the precedent at the same site, and `report()` is the platform channel rather than the consumer's — so an ordinary consumer of first-party behaviors can never see one fire.

**Two ratified rules were not carried forward, and both close permanently at publication** (D-109, D-110). D-75 states that different structures under one name is _the **only** condition that qualifies a name_ and that symmetry **cannot be had later** — and `OnStart`, `OnEnd` and `OnDragError` meet it three times from both ordinary roots, in the same file where D-75 renamed `DragErrorContext`. D-78 states that `SortableConfig` naming a slot is why an installer publishes from `sortable.js` — and `SortableInstaller` is named at two slots and published from none, while the mirror publishes `FreeDragInstaller` for three. **Neither is taste. Both are a rule the package wrote down, applied once, and did not run over the next case.**

**The class this pass actually found is F-81, and it is not the one the brief anticipated.** Four times, a decision landed **completely** and the prose that justified its predecessor was left standing — `dev.ts`'s premise, the 33-type count, `failures.ts`'s consumer claim after D-64, and `kernel.ts:474`'s _`DEV` is true in an ordinary browser_. Because nothing was deferred, **the deferred-decision instrument cannot see any of them, and neither can a green suite.** F-78 is the proof a later reader acts on them: D-107 nearly gated two more validators on `dev.ts`'s four days ago. Recorded rather than decided, because the remedy is an instrument and this ledger already has the model for one.

**One decision moves bytes upward, and it is not a reopening.** D-108 adds weight four days after a pass that declined to remove it, and the two are one argument: D-107 declined to gate A1/A2 **because** gating would deepen F-78. **No number is guessed** — slack is 114–154 B, this may exceed it, and M-3′ licenses that re-base by name. The measurement is owed at landing, and no budget is widened to avoid a correctness fix. **(Measured at landing: 282–305 B, roughly twice the slack. Twelve budgets re-based; nothing was widened to avoid the fix, and the fix did not shrink.)**

### The API refinement slice, landed 2026-08-22 (D-108 … D-111)

**All four decisions implemented in one pass, and the deferred table is empty a seventh time.** Rebuilt from a clean tree, re-measured rather than estimated.

**D-108 cost 282–305 B and no module appeared, which is the case the headroom rule was written for read in the unfamiliar direction.** ~150 B is sized to notice a module arriving; this is twice that and arrives entirely as text — two assert messages, two report messages, `sameKeys`, `validateFrameDescriptors` and two loops, all previously folded away. Every affected budget re-based visibly under M-3′'s standing rule, with the reason written into `bench/size/measure.ts` beside the numbers. **Two rows did not move**: `baseline B` is the shipped predecessor, and the `drag.js` vocabulary root is byte-identical at **121 B** — F-77's week-old assertion confirming the error vocabulary still does not reach the kernel, on a change that touched the kernel.

**The four checks are now read out of the packed tarball**, and that placement is the substance rather than the bookkeeping. The gate was invisible to this suite for its whole life because the repository builds `__DEV__` as `true`: every in-repo fixture ran the assertions the published build had folded to `assertFrameShapesMatch(a, b) {}`. An assertion over `src/` would have been green before D-108 as well as after. The one artifact that discriminates is the tarball a third-party author installs.

**D-109 landed as the behavior prefix, applied mechanically.** `SortableOnStart`/`FreeDragOnStart` and the two siblings, following `SortableErrorContext`/`FreeDragErrorContext`. `FreeDragOnDragError` stutters and is kept: a rule whose output has to be argued name by name is not a rule, and every prettier spelling renamed the base as well as qualifying it, which is more than the decision authorized. `OnMove`, `OnDrop` and `OnReorder` meet no condition; `ResolveHandle` and `ResolveElement` collide _and_ match, which is the counter-case that proves the rule discriminates. The instrument is the consumer fixture, which imports both ordinary roots into one file and compiles against the packed declarations — the one place an unqualified pair actually collides.

**D-111's remedies each landed with an instrument, because all four were invisible to a green suite.** The dangling `.d.ts.map` comment goes rather than the maps arriving, so the monorepo-wide alternative stays open; `drag.js.map` leaves `files`; `clean.extras` gains `kernel`, `shared` and the two `landing-runner` orphans; `type-fest` moves to `devDependencies`. **The sourcemap row is the one worth naming**: it existed, it was correct, and it scanned `.js` only — so 31 dangling references shipped past an instrument whose name claims to check every module. Widening it to `.d.ts` is the whole fix, which makes this an F-63/F-70 instance rather than a new class. The clean-coverage row asserts the **pathspec derivation** covers every path `files` ships, so it fails on a tree that was never built rather than reporting on whatever the working tree happens to hold.

**F-81 is preserved and its instrument was not invented here**, as the brief required. Two of its four instances stopped being live as a side effect — `dev.ts` is deleted and `kernel.ts:474`'s _`DEV` is true in an ordinary browser_ is now true again — but the finding is about the class and stands on the other two.

**A false finding was caught before it was written**, and it is the phase's fifth instance of one error class. An early grep for the dev-assertion strings in the built tree found all of them and appeared to contradict D-107's _`__DEV__` folds completely_. It did not: the hits were in `frames.js.map`'s `sourcesContent` and in preserved JSDoc, and the emitted functions really are empty bodies. **P-02's superseded curve, P-01's unreachable regime, the free-drag division, D-106's `renderedLength` ranking and this are the same mistake — a proxy read where the quantity was meant.** The first four reached the record; this one did not, because the built tree was checked against the minified artifact before anything was written down.

### The maintainability entry, decided 2026-08-22 (D-112 … D-115), clarified 2026-08-22 (D-116)

**The record is [`maintainability.md`](maintainability.md)**, and it is Phase 22's fourth and last entry. **No production change is proposed and none was made**; all four decisions land as `Unimplemented (Remediation)` with live witnesses.

**The diagnosis is one sentence.** The package has exactly one instrument for record continuity — `tests/decisions.node.test.ts` — and it can see exactly one shape: a **decision**, in **one file**, deferred to a **numbered phase**, witnessed by a **source-level fact**. Every maintainability defect this pass found lies outside that shape, and each escapes a different one of its four bounds. **So F-81 is not one problem**; it is the visible part of a record whose continuity machinery covers commitments and covers nothing else.

**The tense rule is what makes any of it affordable**, and it is recorded at D-112. A decision-ledger row is a **dated act** and may describe the tree as it stood — D-101 still names `kernel/dev.ts` and needs no repair. A source comment, a findings row, a contract document and a published `.d.ts` block are **present-tense descriptions** and staleness in them is a defect. Reviews and measurement records are history. That single distinction retires most of the naive reading of F-81 for nothing, and it is a convention the record already applies informally — strike-through for a retired name, a dated note for a superseded figure.

**One instrument, and it goes to references rather than to claims — D-112.** The census over all 57 source files returned a strong negative: every mechanically-checkable count is correct, every deleted symbol is struck with its decision id, every export claim matches the emitted declarations, and every D-109 rename is clean. **The defects concentrate entirely in references to things that live somewhere else** — twelve confirmed, nine of them in shipped source. `src/sortable/feature.ts:3` cites `03 §A feature is a function factory`, the heading **D-45 itself renamed**. Three modules cite `03 §Validation`, a heading in **07**; two citations in `src/sortable/spec.ts` point at **06** and **05**. Two sibling modules disagree about where their shared dependencies live while the free-drag mirror states both correctly. **The largest instance was unrecorded**: `05 §Measurements owed` is not a heading, and it is cited eleven times including inside **D-106's live re-base condition** for L-11. **Human judgment has already declined this class twice** — `05 §What would reopen this` was found, written down in two documents, and left both times because _re-pointing a citation is not this document's to land_. A defect with no owner is what an instrument is for.

**A published declaration ships a sentence denying three decisions — D-113.** `sortable/feature.d.ts` carries two consecutive doc blocks on `SortableInstaller`: the first says it is _internal and unstable, and unexported from the package_, the second says _D-61 publishes the type_. Both are in the artifact a third-party installer author installs. **An orphaned doc block is prose with no subject**, so no compiler, no TypeDoc run and no reviewer diffing declarations can ever tell it that it is wrong — F-81's mechanism in its purest form, invisible by construction. Six ship across 32 declarations; five are correct history and one is this. **It brushes the API entry deliberately and reopens nothing**: that entry read the export lists, not the doc blocks attached to them.

**API-03 is one of five, and the shape is not _somebody forgot_ — D-114.** Every one of the five was booked to a named destination, and the destination then closed without taking it: Phase R over **Check D-56**, the API deliverable over **D-107's Class B**, Checkpoint E over **D-81's** general question, no destination at all for **D-46/D-54's** touch measurement, and _the next pass over 05_ was never a destination. **Check D-56's window is permanently gone** — the check requires before/after numbers taken at the commit where the deletion landed and forbids a later phase from recording it satisfied — so it needs an owner's decision rather than a repair. The remedy is a rule and not a test because **no instrument can observe a destination closing**; what can is the pass that performs it. Three further normative questions in 05 have had their answering event occur and were never re-read.

**Three gates can fail open — D-115.** `packaging.node.test.ts`'s module-graph parser recognises one import form and is correct only by coincidence, with three assertions resting on it, including D-111's four-day-old clean-coverage row; three of the four `docs.node.test.ts` runs grep a stream for the word _warning_, which the file itself calls _the worst way for a gate to break_ before declining to fix them; and the citation convention is unparseable, which is why D-112's census is indicative rather than exact. **A gate that cannot be trusted is worse than an absent one**, because an absent gate is visible in a coverage reading and a fail-open gate reports success — and this is the last pass before a human owner reads a green suite.

**The phase's own error class was met twice more and caught both times.** A grep of the built tree appeared to contradict D-107's `__DEV__` folding — the hits were `.js.map` `sourcesContent` and preserved JSDoc — and two automated citation censuses disagreed, both wrong in both directions, with only a hand audit settling which instances are real. **A proxy read where the quantity was meant**, now six instances across Phase 22; the first four reached the record and these two did not.

**The plan's standing items are discharged.** M-02's duplicated-SPI half is **closed** — `ActionTransition`, `SeamRejection` and `Transition` are each declared once, in `kernel/seams.ts`.
