Its control-flow half is **not**, and is booked to the human owner rather than acted on: `kernel/kernel.ts` is 2 468 lines, the decisions M-02 said must precede a split have all since been taken, and splitting is now a live judgment that this pass declines to make on the last day of agent ownership. M-06 is closed, M-04 closed in place, M-05 is D-115 (a), and Checkpoint E's E-08 residue is D-115 (b).

**Two record corrections were made rather than booked.** **F-2** and **F-11** described their mitigation as a `__DEV__` assertion in both 00 and 05; D-108 made both unconditional four days ago, so each row understated its own mitigation — F-81's class, inside the findings register, created by the decision that closed F-81's first instance. **F-82** carries the eight live obligations so they are addressable before D-114's register exists.

### The maintainability slice, landed 2026-08-22 (D-112 … D-115)

**Four decisions, one instrument, and no production behavior change.** The register is [`obligations.md`](obligations.md); the decision rows carry what each landed.

**D-112 — the reference resolver.** `tests/references.node.test.ts` classifies **every** `§` in `src/`, `tests/`, `bench/`, `.scripts/` and `.plan/contract/`, and resolves every anchored repository path in their prose. **Clause 4 did what it was written to do**: forcing every occurrence to be classified made the citation form converge without anyone legislating a syntax, and the rule it converged on is that **a citation is checkable exactly as far as it is delimited**. Sixty-seven references were repaired — the twelve confirmed danglers, the two `src/` paths the sibling modules disagreed about, and the rest re-punctuated so a one-word heading is not followed by prose the resolver has to guess at. The class also had one instance nobody had looked for: the monorepo's own `.scripts/vite-config.ts` still explained drag2's `__DEV__` define by pointing at `src/kernel/dev.ts`, which D-108 deleted four days ago.

**Three kinds resolve and a fourth is named.** A **linked** citation is followed rather than demoted — `bundle-structure.md` §Headroom and `q7.md` §Answer 1 name real headings outside `contract/`, and the markdown link beside them says where to look, so the resolver reads it. A **specimen** is a citation quoted inside a code span: quoting a dangling reference is not making one, which is what lets D-112's own ledger row and the instrument's own doc block name the defects they exist to describe. **A deliberately retired reference is struck through**, and that marker now covers paths and sections as well as symbols.

**D-113 — no shipped doc block with no subject.** The false one is deleted. **The two legitimate kinds are recognized rather than allowlisted**: a module header is the first block of its `//#region`, and a deletion note carries strike-through. The third case turned out to be neither — `kernel/spec.d.ts`'s orphan was a **section header** that had no business being JSDoc, and it is now `//` comments in source, which is the form `frames.ts` already used.

**D-114 — obligations name destinations that can receive them.** The register is new, and it exists because assembling this list took a full census across nine documents. **Check D-56 is withdrawn unsatisfied**: its before/after measurement belonged to the deletion's landing window, that window was missed, and no later measurement substitutes — D-56 stands on its argument, and what is withdrawn is the falsifier. **API-03 is closed** by rewriting D-108's clause as the standing condition it always was, saying out loud that the call graph already discharges it. **Q-4's narrow half closed on the event it named**; **Q-6 was re-read and deliberately not closed**, because a re-read is not a licence to settle a semantic question locally.

**D-115 — a gate asserts its own premise.** All four `docs.node.test.ts` runs now turn the property under test into an exit status and read back the artifact, so none of them can report success from a stream it failed to read. The packaging parser's one-import-form premise is asserted directly rather than left to coincidence. **Every new instrument was falsified before landing** — a dangling citation, a dangling path, a side-effect import, a broken `{@link}` and a reintroduced orphan each turned its gate red.

**What this slice did not decide, and why that is the point.** Seven obligations stay live in the register, and every one of them needs an owner rather than an implementer: D-107's Class B and D-81's general question need destinations, the touch measurement needs a destination that can hold a measurement, a second engine needs paying for, Q-6 needs an answer, `kernel/kernel.ts`'s split needs a judgment, and F-80's four divergences need four calls. **The register is the artifact this handoff was missing**, and it is deliberately a list of open things rather than a shorter list of closed ones.

**The one point at which review reached the design — MNT-03 and D-116, 2026-08-22.** The tense rule classifies by **register** and the resolver skips by **container**, and those coincide everywhere except one place: a decision-ledger row holds dated reasoning _and_, in three rows out of one hundred and sixteen, a condition a later pass must act on. The row is skipped whole, so the live clause is the one thing in the tree no instrument can see — including the design's own headline instance, `05 §Measurements owed` cited inside D-106's unfired re-base condition.

**The fix is to the specification, not the resolver.** Un-skipping surfaces 26 citations inside D-rows of which most are correct past tense, and would turn the ledger into present-tense documentation. Instead a standing condition is **lifted into the register** D-114 opened, with its own `SC-n` id and its citations re-derived at the moment of lifting; the row keeps its reasoning and cites the id. The skip is then sound because **nothing live is inside a row**. It costs one scope root — `obligations.md` joins the resolver's tree — and no new mechanism. The residue, whether a _new_ decision embeds a condition rather than registering it, gets a backstop over the three bold lead-ins the record actually uses and an explicit statement that the vocabulary is open (D-115). **D-106, D-107 and D-108 are untouched**; only the location and the citations of their live clauses move. Recorded at [`maintainability.md`](maintainability.md) §6 and [`obligations.md`](obligations.md) §Standing conditions.

**Constraint.** This phase changes code against evidence. Anything without a measurement, a review finding or a ledger row behind it is not refinement.

## Phase 23 — Finalization review

### The size-and-ownership audit's clean set, landed 2026-08-22

**What landed is (a) only, and nothing here decides a (b).** The audit is [`reviews/phase-23/size-ownership-audit-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/size-ownership-audit-claude.md), and it classified every finding as a clear violation, an owner decision, or a justified cost. This pass took the first class and the one (c) whose condition could be met, and left B-1 through B-14 — the kernel landing gate, the lift-mode and displacement leakage, the diagnostic text (SC-2/O-1), the merge walkers, the seam latch — where the audit put them.

**`validateFramePart` keeps three checks and loses four.** The kernel-key, `__proto__` and symbol checks defend library-owned invariants: they stop the kernel's own frame slice being overwritten, the frame's prototype being mutated, and a symbol-held DOM node surviving every scrub. The prototype, accessor and non-enumerable checks each cost the **author** their own field and cost the library nothing, and the non-writable check described a failure that cannot happen at all — `Object.assign` uses `[[Set]]` on a fresh extensible object, so the copy is writable. Each removed row is now an **asserted outcome** in `tests/kernel/frames.node.test.ts` rather than a deleted assumption, and contract 04's table is struck and annotated accordingly. This is the largest single item at ~150 B.

**`arm()` stops re-checking its own type and stops refusing what the platform ignores.** `typeof type !== 'string'` re-stated `readonly string[]`; a duplicate entry was refused where `addEventListener` dedups on (type, callback, capture) — all three identical for every entry here — so the second binding was already a no-op, and `indexOf` inside the loop made refusing it quadratic. **The `pointerdown` collision check stays**: two _different_ callbacks on one type is a different claim and is the one that protects a library invariant. `types.length === 0` and the empty string also stay; neither was named in the brief and both refuse a value the type admits.

**The dead surface.** `destroyRequested` was a second name for `queue.closed`, set on the following statement and never cleared. `READINESS_SETTLED` outlived the protocol D-41 deleted in full, with no reference anywhere. `SortableFramePart.outcome` had four writes and no reader — contract 04 justified it by a reader D-62/D-66 deleted, and that row is corrected rather than left to describe code that no longer exists. Three `host.closed` guards asked the question `dispatch` opens with. `Lifetime.finalized` and `FrameTask.flush` are dead members of live objects, which is the one kind a bundler cannot shake; the two tests that read them now read what they stood for instead — dispose idempotence, and a real animation frame.

**`STAGE_TO_CODE` is positional, and the condition for taking it was met rather than assumed.** The brief allowed it only if the source stayed at least as clear and the measurement matched; `as const satisfies Record<FailureStage, DraggableErrorCode>` keeps D-64's mechanism literally true — adding a stage without naming a code still does not compile, now failing twice — and each line names its stage. **No published value moved**: a dense zero-based table would have renumbered a wire value, which is the one change `failures.ts` forbids, so slots 0 and 13 are padded instead. Padding with an existing code measures 7 B better after Brotli and 8 B worse minified, which is why both figures are read.

**Measured on the composition harness, not carried forward from the ablations.**

| Composition               | before | after  | Δ        | slack |
| ------------------------- | ------ | ------ | -------- | ----- |
| minimal                   | 11 435 | 11 162 | **−273** | 423   |
| minimal (xy)              | 11 085 | 10 807 | −278     | 428   |
| minimal + layoutAnimation | 11 874 | 11 589 | −285     | 435   |
| minimal + landing         | 11 728 | 11 449 | −279     | 429   |
| complete                  | 12 139 | 11 859 | **−280** | 430   |
| free drag minimal         | 9 007  | 8 768  | −239     | 392   |
| free drag + bounds        | 9 159  | 8 922  | −237     | 388   |
| free drag + landing       | 9 307  | 9 066  | −241     | 394   |
| free drag complete        | 9 459  | 9 217  | −242     | 393   |
| both behaviors            | 13 699 | 13 418 | −281     | 432   |
| `drag.js`                 | 121    | 121    | **0**    | 29    |
| `kernel.js`               | 6 797  | 6 598  | −199     | 352   |
| baseline A                | 11 848 | 11 583 | −265     | 417   |

**`drag.js` is unmoved and that is the expected result**, not a disappointment: the vocabulary root reaches two modules and none of this was in them. Every row is inside its budget and **no budget was re-based** — slack is now 352–435 B against the ~150 B convention, which is above it rather than under it, and re-basing is D-106's and **SC-1**'s, whose trigger is a row going negative. It has not fired. A re-base is the natural next act for an owner and it is deliberately not taken here.

### The diagnostics policy, decided 2026-08-23 (D-117)

**The ownership measurements closed the `FAILURE_*` question and opened this one.** Publishing the thirteen stage constants costs a bundling consumer **0 B** — record [`reviews/phase-23/failure-vocabulary-cost-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/failure-vocabulary-cost-claude.md), measured three ways including against a purpose-built authoring fixture — so D-64 has no size case against it and the size column for that decision is written `0` rather than left blank. What survives as the package's largest owned cost is diagnostic text and the machinery around it, at 851–949 B on the pre-clean-set tree.

**The decision is D-117 and the specification is [contract 05 §Diagnostics, by provenance and audience](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md).** It answers the question the audit could not: not how to shorten forty messages, but which of them the contract requires. **It requires none.** What it requires is every check that changes the lifecycle path and every classification that check produces; what a message is required by is field usability, which asks for an identity and not a narrative.

**The unit changed, which is why this supersedes rather than overturns.** D-107 declined Class B as one 424 B block on one argument — stripping the cause's message leaves `presentation` and nothing else — and that argument was correct about the design it was made against, where a library-raised sentence is the only thing separating five sortable release faults that all classify `interaction`. D-117 replaces the sentence with an identity instead of removing it, so the premise no longer holds and the block no longer exists: its sites classify one at a time, by **what must be true for them to fire**. D-107's A1 and A2 stand untouched, D-108 stands and gains the stopping rule it never had, and **[SC-2](obligations.md) is retired** with its live residue narrowed to SC-5.

**Then measurement, and the order is not cosmetic.**

1. **Re-baseline.** Every figure in the audit predates the clean set that landed on 2026-08-22 and the positional `STAGE_TO_CODE` that landed with it. The 851–949 B is a rank, not a budget.
2. **Rank the classes with four separate ablations against that baseline** — P1 identities, P2 gating, P3 narration, machinery. **Ranks only.** Brotli deltas do not decompose, and the audit's own §4 (ii) is the worked example: D-107's recorded total and its recorded halves do not reconcile because two ablations priced separately were summed.
3. **Measure the landed policy once, jointly.** That single figure is the one that enters the budgets.
4. **Measure machinery on the runtime axis, not only in bytes.** `assertFrameScrubbed`'s per-retirement allocation and the seam latch's per-sample branch are `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §0 questions, and §0 is senior to §16. A byte figure answers neither.
5. **`kernel root — kernel.js` is a live instrument for this question**, unlike for the `FAILURE_*` question where it was structurally blind: the message ablation moved it −586 B. Read it.
6. **Expect a re-base, not headroom.** Slack is already 352–435 B against the ~150 B convention after the clean set, and this is larger. Re-base under M-3′'s rule — the fix lands, the budget follows — and note that **SC-1 does not fire**: its trigger is a row going negative.
7. **Two instruments already exist and are run the other way.** `tests/consumer.node.test.ts` reads the packed tarball, so every gated site gains the inverse assertion — that it is _absent_ from the artifact a third-party author installs, which is the check D-108 built to prove the opposite. And `__DEV__` is `true` in this repository, so a gated site stays fully exercised by the suites; the thirteen assertions riding on the seam latch's message survive gating and would only need re-pointing to its token.
8. **~35 message-fragment assertions in the browser suites re-point to identities.** Mechanical and bounded, and it is the suite conceding the point: it already treats the prose as a site identity, matching `/re-entered/u` and `/same element twice/u` rather than any sentence.

**One site is not the implementer's to classify.** F-85 records that the seam re-entry latch has two incompatible reachability claims on the record — its own module doc and the audit's B-6 — and D-117 (b)'s default answers the interim question rather than the substantive one: unsettled means P1, so it ships until someone proves it cannot fire.

### The D-117 measurement, and the closure that makes it implementable, 2026-08-23

**Measured** — [`reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/d117-measurement-claude.md). F-85 is resolved from the call graph and then attacked from twelve seams by thirteen vectors; the baseline is re-taken at `92cbae58`; four ablations rank the classes; the policy is measured jointly at **−350…−547 B Brotli**; and both machinery sites are timed. **The parts understate the whole by 2–9 %, always in the same direction** — forty identities sharing a prefix compress against each other in a way three separate ablations cannot see, which is the clearest demonstration this record has that a ranked-and-summed pass books the wrong number.

**Four points the measurement surfaced are settled in D-117's row rather than left to the implementer.** All four resolve toward _touch less_, and that is not caution — each is a case where the evidence turned out to argue for the narrower act.

- **The four sortable release faults are P1**, with everything else the measurement left provisional. Two of them say _a broken invariant_ in their own comments, and F-85 is this record's own proof of what a comment is worth as a reachability claim. **The settlement costs nothing**: all eight P2 checks are load-bearing — remove one of the four and a release reports a drop that never happened — so under either class the check ships and the message becomes an identity. Proving them would govern only a later gating decision, and gating is unavailable to them anyway, so it is settled rather than booked.
- **`drag.js` stays at 121 B and its 29 B budget is untouched.** The +2 B was `DraggableError`'s constructor being slugged, and that site detects nothing — it _implements_ the identity floor, and `drag: ${code} failure` is already an identity. It is out of scope, which makes the vocabulary root the slice's **control row**: a policy that touches only what it should leaves it byte-identical. Absorbing 2 B into a deliberately 29 B budget is the one thing that row exists to prevent.
- **The remediation is message text and nothing else** — no check, branch, outcome, lifecycle path or gate. The runtime evidence **splits the machinery pair rather than justifying an edit**: the seam latch is on the per-sample path and costs 1.0 ns, 0.04 % of a sample; `assertFrameScrubbed` is cold at ~0.4 of one sample per operation and allocates 32 transient objects per retirement, scaling with part width. Ablation D prices that machinery by deleting I-27's and I-28's only mechanisms, which is `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §13 and not a size pass. It is booked as **O-12** so closing this slice does not orphan it.
- **No tier acquires a `__DEV__` binding.** Exactly one kernel gate candidate exists, and D-101 answers it in its own words: the boundary _must not be argued on bytes_, and bytes are the whole case. The P2 rule gains the clause it was missing — a P2 diagnostic gates only in a tier that already binds the flag — so the kernel's two sites ship as D-108 left the four, `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts`'s declared set is unchanged, and the decline carries **SC-6**. Contract 04 is corrected where its pin reads as a prohibition rather than as the declaration rule D-101 built.

**What the implementer lands.** Forty message strings become identities under **one prefix**, `drag: ` with a space, with the area in the slug — measured rather than tidy, since a split prefix vocabulary gives away the joint compression above. **A token names the condition, never the verdict**: `sortable/release-no-proposal`, not `sortable/release-invariant`, because most sites hold their class by default rather than by proof and forty strings should not carry an unproved claim. `errors.ts`'s fallback and the one `__DEV__`-gated sentence are untouched.

**Two instruments come off the list.** With nothing gating, `tests/consumer.node.test.ts` needs no inverse assertion and keeps D-108's meaning exactly, and `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts` needs nothing at all. Of the three the measurement flagged, only `tests/decisions.node.test.ts`'s witness moves, which is what a witness is for. The remaining 25 assertions are message-fragment matches, and **the thirteen re-entry assertions need no work** — they match `/re-entered/u`, which the identity still contains.

**The budgets re-base against the landed build, not against −502.** The joint figure was measured with the gating this closure declines and with one implementer's tokens, so it is an upper bound. No row goes negative, so **SC-1 does not fire**, and the re-base is M-3′'s ordinary one.

### The D-117 remediation, landed 2026-08-23

**Thirty-nine classified sites, message text only.** Every shipped narrative diagnostic is now `drag: <area>/<condition>` under the single prefix, with the offending value interpolated where the site has one. `errors.ts`'s formatting fallback and `verified-refresh.ts`'s one gated sentence were out of scope and are byte-identical. **No check, branch, lifecycle path, outcome, failure classification, gate or declared tier moved** — the closure's boundary held, and the diff is strings, the comments that now hold what the strings said, and the tests and contracts that quoted them.

**Tokens name the condition, never the verdict.** `sortable/release-no-proposal` rather than `sortable/release-invariant`, exactly as 05 §The message is an identity requires — which matters most at the four sortable release faults, whose provenance the closure settled by default rather than by proof.

**Where the derivation rule needed a second reading.** Two placeholder losses at different instants — one at insertion, one at the reorder commit — produce the same subject and different branches, and are `sortable/insertion-placeholder-lost` and `sortable/landing-placeholder-lost`. D-117 (c) claims a token is derivable from the site without editorial judgment; that holds, but the site has to be read as _which branch_ rather than _which subject_, and this pair is where a second implementer would most easily diverge.

**Measured jointly, once, against the pre-slice tree at `76176da8`** — never summed from the per-class ranks, which the measurement shows understate the whole by 2–9 %.

| Row                         | pre-slice | landed | Δ brotli | Δ minified |

| minimal (xy)                | 10,807    | 10,364 | **−443** | −1,238     |

**The landed figure sits inside the closure's −350…−547 B rather than at it, and that is the expected place for it.** The joint ablation gated the P2 kernel sites, which this slice declines, and used one implementer's token lengths; both were named as upper-bound terms before anything landed. Nothing here is compared against the audit's retired 851–949 B.

**`drag.js` did not move, and it is the row that was built to notice if it had.** The classification never reaches `DraggableError`'s constructor, so the control row is byte-identical at 121 B and keeps its deliberately tight 29 B budget. That is the boundary's own falsifier passing, not an absence of evidence.

**The budgets re-base down.** Landed slack was 618–932 B — four to six times the ~150 B convention, which is sized to notice one module entering a graph and stops doing that at six times the width. Every measured row returns to landed + ~150 B; `drag.js` keeps 29 B and baseline B keeps its 151 B, since neither moved. Rationale in [`bench/size/measure.ts`](../bench/size/measure.ts) §`budget`, with the pre-slice column recorded beside the landed one as API-01 requires.

### The two surviving `command.types` checks, decided and deleted, 2026-08-24

**Decided** — D-118, on [`reviews/phase-23/command-types-checks-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/command-types-checks-claude.md). The 2026-08-22 clean set took four checks out of `arm()`'s `command.types` loop down to two, and stopped there because that brief did not authorize deciding semantics. This decides them: **the empty array and the empty-string entry are deleted; the `pointerdown` collision stays.**

**The rule that separated them is `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 — _whose invariant, not whose mistake_ — and the answer was built rather than argued.** An **empty array** binds no discrete listener, and the assertion that matters is not that it stops throwing: its call sequence is _identical_ to a behavior that declares no `command` member at all, which contract 02 already blesses. The check was refusing a configuration the library supports under a different spelling. An **empty-string entry** binds an ordinary, distinct listener for a type nothing dispatches — `addEventListener('')` is neither an error nor a wildcard — so the author's discrete ingress is inert while the kernel's is intact. Both cost the author their own feature and the library nothing.

**The third check is the library's, and the contrast is the evidence.** Two listeners for one `pointerdown` run two admission members for one event, and the second finds the first's operation already committed — silently, and only sometimes. All three sat in one loop over one array, and only one survives the test: **proximity is not provenance**, which is why the loop read as though all three belonged.

**The counter-argument for the empty string is on the record, not buried.** Without it, `[config.event ?? '']` yields a silent dead listener where it used to yield a throw, and a failure that succeeds and does nothing is the worst shape a failure takes. But that is a nannying benefit and §1.1 declines those by name. It is a judgment where the empty array is not, and it is taken rather than deferred.

**The non-empty tuple type is deliberately not taken.** §1.2 does reach the empty array — the tuple typechecks with `src/` unchanged — and it is declined here because it is not free: a call site building `types` from a `readonly string[]` variable stops compiling. That is a §12 question about a public type and belongs to the API deliverable. **An empty array is a supported spelling, not an unreachable one.**

**−137 B minified, and the split is worth stating because it is not the projected number.** The review projected −128 B for the two checks; that reproduced _exactly_, verified by measuring a variant that kept the destructure. The remaining **−9 B** is this pass collapsing `const { types } = next.command`, which existed only because `types.length` was a second reader. Brotli is **−23 to −39 B**. Twenty-odd bytes is inside the noise band this phase has now demonstrated three times — **the argument is §1.1 and §1.2, and it would read the same at 3 B or at 300 B.** `drag.js` is byte-identical at 121 B for the third consecutive slice, and baseline B does not move; neither reaches `kernel/kernel.js`.

**The budgets are re-based down, and this time the reason is not tidiness.** The deltas left slack at **162–179 B**, which is _past_ the +157 B this file measures for the smallest module entering a graph — a headroom wide enough to swallow the module it exists to notice. All twelve kernel-bearing rows return to 150 B; `drag.js` keeps its deliberate 29 B and baseline B its 151 B.

**The tests assert the new behavior rather than dropping the old expectations.** The three-case rejection table becomes a two-case one — `['pointerdown']` and `['', 'pointerdown']`, the second proving the surviving check is not weakened by accepting the empty string beside it — and two positive tests land: one comparing an empty-`types` harness against a no-`command` harness call-for-call, one asserting the `''` listener fires on `Event('')` and on nothing else. **They cross-validate**: the second proves the harness's command wiring works, which is what makes the first's zero mean _unreachable_ rather than _unwired_.

**F-90 was found on the way and is the reason this pass touched three contracts.** [01 §When construction itself fails](contract/01-construction-ownership.md), [02 §Discrete admission](contract/02-kernel-behavior-contract.md) and [05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md)'s D-32 row all still listed the **non-string** and **duplicate** checks — deleted two days earlier, with the code and its comment updated and no contract. **This is F-84's shape a second time**, and the second instance makes it a class: a deletion pass reads the source it edits and the test it breaks, and a contract sentence is neither. No instrument here reads a claim, and the suite could not have caught it — it asserted the surviving identities and had nothing to say about sentences describing checks that no longer ran. All three now state the landed behavior.

**The `m5.browser.test.ts` failure the review flagged stayed unrelated, and the landed tree said so more cleanly than the ablation could.** Free drag declares no `command` member, so no path that test reaches evaluates either deleted branch. On this tree the whole `m5` file passes.

### The D-117 remediation review, and its two identity repairs, 2026-08-24

**Reviewed** — [`reviews/phase-23/d117-implementation-review-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/d117-implementation-review-claude.md). Clean overall: the boundary claim was re-established by an independent normalizer over all thirteen changed files, every landed figure reproduced exactly, and `drag.js` was confirmed unmoved at 121 B. Two findings and one stale sentence came back.

**F-86 is the one real defect, and it is an identity that is false.** `sortable/placeholder-not-detached` named the fourth of four disjuncts, and the state it named as the fault — detached — is the one the accepting case requires. Repaired by naming the umbrella rather than splitting the branch: **`sortable/placeholder-not-adoptable`**. The site's own comment already said _adopted_, so the token comes off the site the way D-117 (c) requires. Predicate, classification and lifecycle path are untouched.

**It is the same question as the placeholder pair, answered the other way, one module apart.** Read as _which subject_, the site is about a placeholder; read as _which branch_, it is about four. The landing note flagged that reading as the place a second implementer would diverge and then got it wrong in the other direction on the very next site — which is the argument for recording the **reading** rather than either pair.

**F-87 is legibility.** `spec/command-type-empty` became **`spec/command-entry-empty`**, so an empty array and an empty entry no longer differ by a trailing `s`. Same length, different repairs, and the identity is the whole of what a bug report quotes.

**One stale sentence in the size doctrine — and correcting it turned out to need a stronger correction than the review could see.** `measure.ts` §`budget` claimed that a change fitting inside ~150 B is a change that added no module. The review measured the smallest module's cost to enter a graph at **149 B**, one byte under. Re-measured after this two-character remediation it is **157 B**, and pre-slice it was **154 B** — three figures, two of them straddling the headroom, across two passes in which **no module moved and no code changed**. A module's marginal cost is what Brotli charges for it given everything else in the graph, so it is not a property of the module and no headroom can be sized against it. **The struck sentence is therefore unreliable in principle rather than merely stale**, and the correction says so. **Budgets and graph assertions are unchanged**: `free drag minimal` already declares that module absent, which is the half of the declaration that was carrying the claim all along.

**Two identities grew by one character each, and the bundles did move.** Minified is **+1 to +2 B** on every composition; Brotli runs **−5 to +14 B**, which is dictionary noise at this scale and not a signal — `free drag minimal` is 5 B _smaller_ for one added character. All fourteen rows stay green at **136–155 B** of slack and no budget was touched. **`drag.js` and baseline B are byte-identical**, the first because the classification still does not reach it.

**The suite conceded the point rather than being argued with.** Twenty-seven assertions across eleven files re-pointed to identities, and the thirteen re-entry assertions did not move at all (**they moved on 2026-08-28**, when the identity they matched was withdrawn — see §The re-entry latch stops naming itself) — they match `/re-entered/u`, and `drag: seam/re-entered` still contains it. `tests/consumer.node.test.ts` keeps D-108's meaning exactly and now reads the packed identities instead of the packed sentences; `tests/kernel/vocabulary.node.test.ts` is untouched, because no tier acquired a binding. Two present-tense contract documents that quoted the old strings were corrected with them — 04 §Dev-only invariants' code block and 05's three-prefix sentence — which is F-84's lesson applied in the same pass that would otherwise have created it again.

**Relocated from its original position.** `reviews/finalization-review/` exists as an empty directory and was implicitly scoped to the vertical-sortable slice. Run there it would have reviewed a package about to grow a second behavior, a second input mode, a second axis and a revised settlement protocol — the wrong artifact, reviewed at the moment its findings had the shortest shelf life.

**It also does not belong before Phase 22.** Checkpoint E is the _architectural_ review and it deliberately precedes refinement so its findings feed the pass. The finalization review's distinct job is **product-level and final**: the complete public surface, the documentation, the migration story, and the set of open questions — reviewed on the artifact that will actually ship.

**Scope.** The complete package: both behaviors, both input modes, both axes, the full feature set, the measurements, the docs, the demo corpus. Owner review plus an independent audit, plus a closure assessment. Findings are closed, not carried — this is the last review before cutover, so "deferred" is not an available classification.

**Carried work — ledger L-11, the cancel _reason_ sentinels.** Raised at Checkpoint D while resolving D5, and **deferred here by the owner's decision** rather than declined. Every cancel reason the library itself produces is a namespaced string constant that is not exported, so a consumer that wants "stay silent when the user pressed Escape, warn when the item vanished" must hard-code an undocumented string that can change without notice. `stage: CancelStage` does not cover it — `AT_PROPOSAL`/`AT_CONSUMER` say _when_ an operation was abandoned, never _why_.

**This deliverable is declined and replaced by D-154, landed 2026-08-29** — see §Cancellation provenance below. The first bullet is **void**: the three sentinels are deleted rather than exported. The second landed as written. The third stands. What replaced them is `origin: CancelOrigin` on both canceled arms. The bullets are kept as decided, because the decision that replaces them is argued against exactly this text.

- ~~Export the three kernel sentinels — `'drag:escape'`, `'drag:pointercancel'`, `'drag:lostpointercapture'` (`src/kernel/kernel.ts`) — from **`drag.js`**, beside the `FAILURE_*` constants.~~ **This destination survives Revision 2 unchanged, and it was checked rather than assumed.** An earlier wording of D-48 made `drag.js` the kernel entrypoint, which would have sited these sentinels — like `FailureStage`, `Point` and `DOMRealm` already there — behind the kernel tier, inverting the disclosure ladder D-47 §11 exists to protect. D-48 is corrected: `drag.js` stays the shared vocabulary and a new `kernel.js` carries the authoring types, so `drag.js` is still the right home and this bullet needs no change. Ledger §7 records the near-miss.
- Export the two sortable sentinels — `'sortable:item-removed'`, `'sortable:collection-invalidated'` (`src/sortable/domain.ts`) — from **`sortable.js`**.
- `CanceledReorderResult.reason` stays typed `unknown`. `controller.cancel(reason)` accepts anything, so the union is genuinely open and narrowing it would be a worse lie than the current gap.
- **It adds five runtime cells to two frozen entrypoints, so it requires an M-3 re-measurement** and a contract 03 §The export topology this requires amendment — the same discipline Phase 17's rename followed. That is why it is a phase item and not a refinement: it changes the public surface, and Phase 22 is explicitly for changing code against evidence, not surface. **Revision 2 does not change this**: the freeze rule was never what deferred L-11, Checkpoint D's exit condition was — closing parity items rather than growing the surface — and Revision 2 grows the surface for its own reasons without reopening that. If the Revision 2 implementation phase re-measures M-3 anyway, Phase 23 may find L-11's cost already paid; that is a scheduling observation, not a reclassification.

It is deferred to _here_ rather than to Phase 22 because this is the phase that reviews the complete public surface, and it is the last phase at which the finding can still be open at all.

### The decomposition-shape review, 2026-08-24 (F-91, F-92)

**Reviewed** — [`reviews/phase-23/decomposition-shape-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/decomposition-shape-claude.md). The question raised by the owner: whether drag2 has drifted toward too many semantic seams — _do one tiny step, trap on `null`, continue_ — with the keyboard `command.admit` flow and `buildReorderProposal` as the named examples and `packages/tproc` as the contrast. **Investigative; nothing is decided and no production change is proposed.**

**The two named sites split, and they split cleanly.** `command.admit` is **not** a defect: five steps, each `null` meaning a different decline, one channel — and that is right, because the recipient is the kernel and the kernel has one branch. The ordering is normative (D-46 fixes _what did the event land on_ before _is the move feasible_, and the probe-E table records what the other order produced), so flattening it would erase a rule the contract owns and buy nothing. `buildReorderProposal` **is** a defect, and it is a symptom: its four guards all check that a value _this package built_ is well-formed against a snapshot _this package built_, which is the same reading that classified the site P2 under D-117.

**The finding is an unowned noun, not an over-split verb (F-91).** `Insertion` is constructed by literal in seven places across four modules; the expression deriving its neighbours is written four times over three differently-produced arrays, and the fourth of those — `collection.ts:186` — is not building a value but checking the first three. `homeInsertion` spells the concept a fifth way and argues the equivalence in prose. **The package decomposed the steps of insertion resolution and left the concept they all produce without a name**, so a rule small enough to inline is inlined five times and the one seam that would have paid was never drawn. The disposition the record already reached for the same shape is F-7's: _export it as a pure helper, never a seam_.

**Three of the four things a seam is usually bought with are missing here.** Of the five pure helpers the split produced — `directionOf`, `keyboardInsertion`, `buildReorderProposal`, `reconcileCollection`, `homeInsertion` — **none is imported by any test**; every one is exercised only through the composed behavior. None is recursive, none is reached from two sites, and none is large enough to need a name to be readable.

**One duplication is defensible and is left alone.** The destination view is derived three times, at three instants with three amounts of live state — pre-activation with no index, geometry-scan time while the index is being built, invalidation while it is deliberately dirty — and the maintained copy sits behind the required `resolveInsertion` slot. The construction expression has no such constraint: it takes an array and an index.

**The drift hypothesis is falsified for the layer it named and confirmed for another (F-92).** `keyboard.ts` has one commit and `collection.ts` three, both laid down at scaffold and completion; `spec.ts` has fifteen, and what the review passes deposited there is **48 terminal barriers**, 22 in that one file. Barriers and seams look alike on screen — a short `if`, an early return, one per line — and share nothing else, which is what made the wrong layer look guilty. Whether I-36's obligation belongs at forty-eight call sites or in the seam runner is a real question, recorded and **deliberately not opened**: it is a separate slice with a different risk profile.

**The candidate slice, and its falsifier.** A construction owner for `Insertion` — a pure helper, never a seam — consumed by keyboard, both axes, home and reconcile. Expected: one expression instead of four; `homeInsertion`'s prose equivalence becomes a derivation or is found not to be one; `buildReorderProposal`'s neighbour guard becomes provably redundant or narrows to the three checks that are genuinely about `(snapshot, insertion)`; and ~~D-117's P2 classification~~ of `sortable/release-no-proposal` is revisited on evidence — the premise is wrong and was wrong when the review wrote it (**F-94**, corrected 2026-08-24): D-117 (1) settles that site as **P1**. **The review proves the five spellings are _written_ as five, not that they are _one_** — proving the equivalence is the slice's first step, not its conclusion — and `reconcileCollection`'s survival test (I-14) is a decision that must not be absorbed into a construction. Size is expected to fall and is not evidence until `bench/size` says so.

**No instrument.** The one mechanically checkable fact — that no test imports the pure helpers — would need a check encoding what a seam is for, which is a semantic model and which D-112's admission criterion refuses. Recorded instead, as F-84 was.

### `Insertion` gets a construction owner, 2026-08-24 (D-119, closing F-91)

**Decided and implemented** — D-119, on [`reviews/phase-23/decomposition-shape-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/decomposition-shape-claude.md) §7. The review's own instruction is what shaped the slice: _proving the equivalence is the first step of the slice, not its conclusion._

**The proof ran before any production line moved, and it is exhaustive rather than argued.** Every collection up to six items, every dragged position, every incumbent `(before, after)` pair for `reconcileCollection`, every gap index in and out of range for `buildReorderProposal` — run against the real functions on the pre-slice tree. **All five spellings are one rule**: _the gap at index `i` of the destination view_, `d[i - 1] ?? null` and `d[i] ?? null`. `homeInsertion`'s full-list `±1` is that rule evaluated without materializing the view, because removing the item at `from` leaves every earlier element in place and shifts every later one down by one, so `d[from - 1]` **is** `items[from - 1]` and `d[from]` **is** `items[from + 1]`. The rect cache's maintained array was included rather than assumed: `createRectIndex().refresh(...)` was driven directly and its `items` compared element-for-element against `destinationOf`, which is what makes `y()`'s and `xy()`'s expression the rule verbatim rather than a lookalike.

**The proof has exactly one precondition, and the package already refuses the input that breaks it.** Element identity — what `copyUniqueItems` enforces at every snapshot mint. `indexOf` finds the first occurrence where a filtered view drops all of them, so on `[x, a, a, y]` with `a` dragged the two spellings differ by one neighbour. That is the whole of the gap between them, and it is unconstructible.

**The owner is `insertionAt`, in `src/sortable/domain.ts` beside the type it builds** — a pure helper, never a seam, which is F-7's disposition applied to the value F-91 found unowned. It takes an array and an index, so each caller passes whichever destination view it already holds: `keyboardInsertion`'s locally filtered one, the axis cache's maintained one, `destinationOf`'s. **The three derivations of the view are untouched**, which is what the review's §3.2 asked for and what the argument-shape guarantees rather than promises — a rule that needs no instant cannot prefer one of the three.

**`reconcileCollection` keeps its decision and loses only its duplication.** Its three arms keep their own survival tests verbatim and now derive the gap they used to carry across from the incumbent. The proof is what licenses that: given each arm's guard, the carried neighbours and the derived ones are the same two elements.

**`homeInsertion` is the one site that does not call the owner, and the reason is the review's own fourth falsifier.** Calling it would allocate a destination view where seeding home allocates none, and _if a candidate implementation changes the allocation profile, it has exceeded the slice._ So it keeps its evaluation and gives up its prose: the equivalence now lives in [`tests/sortable/insertion.browser.test.ts`](../tests/sortable/insertion.browser.test.ts), which is also the first test in the package to import either pure helper directly — the gap §2 of the review measured, filled at exactly the one place where construction is not enforced by construction.

**The expected consequence that did not arrive is worth more than the three that did.** The review predicted `buildReorderProposal`'s neighbour guard would become provably redundant. **It does not.** Every gap this package builds is now `insertionAt` over a view of the snapshot it names, so for those the check is vacuous once the version matches — but `InsertionGeometry.resolve` is **published at the middle tier** (D-61, D-68), and a version-matching gap can arrive from third-party axis code. **That fault leaves the tier that made it**: it does not cost the axis author their own feature, it hands the _consumer_ a `{ from, to, before, after }` to apply to their own array. The guard is therefore **re-scoped rather than deleted** — one check about author conformance beside three about the pair `(snapshot, insertion)` — and the review's fourth consequence lands on evidence instead of policy: **`sortable/release-no-proposal` is P1, and this slice makes it P1 by proof rather than by default.** ~~D-117's P2 classification is confirmed~~ — corrected 2026-08-24, **F-94**. D-117 (1) settles all four sortable release faults as **P1**, in those words; the P2 reading entered the record in the review that opened F-91 and was carried into this row unchecked, and [05](contract/05-lifecycle-invariants.md) §Diagnostics, by provenance and audience forbids the record holding two answers for one site. What the slice actually supplies is the proof rule (b)'s default was standing in for: a version-matching gap reaching this check from a third-party `InsertionGeometry.resolve` is outside the library, which is P1 on the call graph. The range check is not implied by the neighbour check either, and the proof says why: an index past the end of the view reads `null` at both ends, and a start gap of an empty view legitimately does too.

**So no validation was removed, and that is the result rather than a shortfall.** The review's §7 corollary — _a validator that re-checks a value the package itself built is the receipt for a missing constructor_ — is right about three of the four guards and wrong about the fourth, because that one is not re-checking a value this package built.

**F-93 is what the proof found on its way past.** The construction rule and the survival rules disagree about exactly one input: a `{ before: null, after: null }` gap, which is the only gap of a single-item collection. `insertionAt` builds it, `placeholderAt` reads it as trivially occupied, and `reconcileCollection`'s start-gap arm refuses it — so a one-item collection republished during its own drag cancels although nothing changed. **Recorded, not fixed**: whether that gap survives a replacement is I-14's decision, and a constructor that answered it would be this finding one level up. `homeInsertion` on a single-item snapshot is its only producer.

**Measured, and the measurement is the second useful thing here.** Minified: **−114 to −119 B, flat**, on every row carrying the sortable behavior, and **0 B** on the four free-drag rows, the kernel root, `drag.js` and baseline B. Brotli: **−24 B to +6 B** on those same rows — **two of them up**, `+ landing` by 6 B and `complete` by 1 B. One edit, one direction minified, both directions compressed. This phase has asserted three times that twenty-odd Brotli bytes is inside the noise band; this is the first slice to demonstrate it **on one tree** rather than by comparing two, and the direction disagreement is the demonstration. **The budgets do not move**: they already sit at their measurement plus **144–174 B** — the ~150 B target, read in both directions — and re-basing would raise two budgets on 6 B and 1 B of compression noise, which is the one habit a budget sized to notice a module must not acquire.

**One allocation was added and it is named.** `buildReorderProposal` builds one short-lived gap to compare against, at the release seam, which already allocates three objects and runs once per operation. No per-frame and no per-candidate path allocates anything it did not before; `y()` and `xy()` still build exactly the one object they returned.

**The feature-to-vocabulary import edge was checked, not assumed.** [03](contract/03-feature-composition.md) §Consumer-declared views, not producer projections forbids an import edge from a feature to **the behavior's runtime type** — the aggregate `Pick<SortableRuntime, …>` D-13 removed. `y.ts` and `xy.ts` already name `Insertion` and `CollectionSnapshot` from the domain vocabulary in that section's own code sketch; what is new is that the edge now carries a **value**. The section says so explicitly rather than leaving a reader to infer it, and the module graphs are unchanged — `sortable/domain.js` is in every composition that reaches an axis feature already.

**What was not done, deliberately.** F-92 is untouched and unopened: the review said bundling the forty-eight terminal barriers with this would damage both, and it is a separate slice with a different risk profile. No `Option`/`Result` wrapper, no flattening of `command.admit`, no unification of the three destination-view derivations, and no change to `reconcileCollection`'s survival rules.

### The collection uniqueness question, decided 2026-08-25 (D-120, closing F-95)

**An owner question rather than a finding, and the answer is that nothing moves.** `ItemSource` returns `readonly HTMLElement[]` and every structural mint runs `copyUniqueItems`, which copies and builds a `Set` solely to refuse duplicate element identity. Three candidates were put — keep the runtime check, publish an ordered `ReadonlySet` snapshotted straight back into an array, or make uniqueness an unchecked precondition of the array API. Record [`collection-uniqueness-ownership-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/collection-uniqueness-ownership-claude.md).

**Both alternatives fail for one reason, not two: each converts a refusal the library can still make into a corruption nobody can observe.** The `ReadonlySet` proposal is the sharper rejection because it looks like the principled answer — **a `Set` does not reject duplicates, it absorbs them**, so a consumer converting their own array loses elements silently and the type then asserts the precondition the conversion just destroyed. It also buys no allocation back, and it would end D-44's array-identity fast path, making every scroll, resize and zoom a structural mint.

**The counterfactual was executed rather than argued, per Code of Size §1.1, and it decided the question.** A duplicated **dragged** item yields a proposal that passes all four of `buildReorderProposal`'s guards — version, membership, range, neighbour identity — because within each index space every value is consistent; it is the two spaces that disagree, and nothing compares them. The consumer applies the published `{ from, to }` and their element lands in the wrong position, with no throw, no `onError` and no cancellation. A duplicated **non**-dragged item, which the cheap O(1) length test cannot see, makes `reconcileCollection` cancel a live operation against a republication of the identical, unchanged collection — I-14 violated by a publication that changed nothing.

**The cost was measured on when it runs, which is §0's own rule.** Never per frame or per sample; construction once, then only the structural branch D-44 already gates. 105 ns / 715 ns / 2.7 us / 13.4 us at n = 10 / 50 / 200 / 1000 — against a rect-index rebuild the same publication forces, which at n = 1000 is milliseconds. Roughly one percent of the work its own trigger causes.

**What the question actually surfaced is in the record, not the code** (F-95). D-77 swept this check under the Code of Size and kept it deliberately, but the rule it froze in the same cell — _a construction-time throw is permitted only for an invariant over what installers contribute_ — reaches `claim` and not this. The verdict was right and the stated rationale did not cover it, which is why F-67 and F-68 both re-examined the check and neither noticed: both asked where it should run, not why it exists. Closed by supplying the derivation — §1.1's _whose state is corrupted_, answered twice over in the library's favour — rather than by widening a rule that is correct about the thing it is about.

**Nothing is booked as work.** The one-pass spelling of `copyUniqueItems` is a measurement left to the implementer and §11 warns against hand-shaping source for it; F-93 is untouched and stays open on its own terms.

### The three contract questions the gate left open, decided 2026-08-25 (D-121 … D-123)

**The reachability-gate audit returned three rows unresolved, and all three are contract questions rather than size ones.** Record [`gate-contract-questions-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/gate-contract-questions-claude.md), on [`reachability-gate-audit-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/reachability-gate-audit-claude.md) §1.1, §3.4 and §3.9. No production code changed.

**Collection uniqueness — the surface admits duplicates today, so the term is published and D-120's refusal is reversed** (D-121). `items: ItemSource` carries **no TSDoc at all**, no shipped `.d.ts` says _unique_, _distinct_ or _duplicate_ of the collection, and `README.md` documents array **identity** while saying nothing about element identity. The term is published rather than left to obvious semantics for a reason specific to this API: the realistic route to a duplicate is composing `items()` from overlapping sources, where the consumer believes they are conforming and the belief is reasonable — and the library's own story satisfies the precondition **one indirection away** from where it would be stated, by mapping a label list it assumes is duplicate-free. What gets published is a definition rather than a restriction: `from` indexes the snapshot and `to` the destination view, and the two spaces differ by exactly one only while the dragged element occurs once.

**Three things that deletion must not sweep up.** The **copy stays** on §9 ownership grounds the gate does not touch; **D-80 (b)'s statement ordering stays** and re-anchors from F-68 to F-69 (F-98); and **D-120 §4 (b)'s rejection of the `ReadonlySet` representation stands and is strengthened** — once uniqueness is a published term, a representation that enforces it by silently absorbing duplicates is the worst available way to state it.

**Frame-part symbol keys — the check fails clause (b) as well as the gate** (D-122). Gate (a) passes, as the audit judged: a symbol key is type-legal, ordinary, and nowhere forbidden in the published TSDoc. But `scrubFrame` resets the kernel's own fields and delegates the part to `resetFramePart`, which is **behavior-author code** — so a symbol-keyed field is declared by the author, reset by the author's own function, and invisible only to the library's `Object.keys`-based assertions. No library-owned state is corrupted and no library-computed value is published malformed. The counter — the frame object is library-allocated and controller-lived — is declined: a library-allocated container no more owns the author's field than a handed-out `Map` owns its values.

**Insertion neighbour coherence — the defect is the public construction surface** (D-123). The precondition **is** meetable, which the audit understated, and the consequence is load-bearing: keep-it-private-and-keep-the-guard is not an available position, so the choice is publish-and-delete or withhold-and-delete. That reframes it as an ownership inconsistency — D-119 gave `Insertion` one construction owner, D-61 opened the tier, and the package publishes the type and the obligation while withholding the tested implementation, with `buildReorderProposal`'s neighbour test as the receipt one level up from F-91. `insertionAt` is published from `sortable/feature`, not from the consumer root, and the packaging consequence is named rather than assumed away: that entry is **`typeOnly`** in `files.json`, so this converts it into a runtime entry.

**Three findings recorded rather than folded in.** F-96 — the scrub assertion's symbol blind spot outlives the check and must be documented as partial, not widened. F-97 — the published `kernel/frames.d.ts` names `validateFramePart` as _authoritative_, so deleting any arm of it is a contract edit, and that sentence governs the audit's §3.2 and §3.3 deletions too. F-98 — D-80 (b)'s ordering survives on F-69 alone once F-68's window closes, and a comment left anchored to F-68 would read as dead.

### The gate's five clean deletions, landed 2026-08-25 (D-124)

**Implemented** — D-124, on [`reachability-gate-audit-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/reachability-gate-audit-claude.md) §§1.2, 1.3, 2, 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.8. The rows the audit classified cleanly and the owner scheduled; the three it returned unresolved are D-121 … D-123 and are **not** implemented here.

**The audit's own publication caveat is discharged before any code moved.** Its §7 made every finding conditional on generated API documentation not publishing contract text the shipped `.d.ts` does not. `just docs-api-build` was run and its output read: TypeDoc renders `SortableConfig`, `FreeDragController` and `LandingOptions` as **type aliases and drops their per-member TSDoc entirely**, so the generated site publishes strictly _less_ than the editor surface the audit read, and the collection's distinctness appears nowhere in it. **No finding changes**, and the honest note is that the weaker surface is the generated one — the `.d.ts` an integrator sees in an editor is where these preconditions live.

**Five guards go**, each because the state it refuses is unreachable through correct use of a published term: `moveTo`'s finite coordinates (published on the member's own doc comment), the landing's `duration === Infinity` (_a duration is finite_, `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1's own paradigm), `placeholder-not-adoptable` (four arms restating one published sentence and one type), the frame part's kernel-key collision (`FramePartOf` makes it uninhabitable) and its `__proto__` arm (`defineProperty`-only), and `home-not-finite`'s throw.

**The copy at `home` stays and is the one place a mechanical sweep would have gone wrong.** `const { x } = home; const { y } = home;` is not defensiveness: the library reads a consumer-owned point across a seam boundary and pins geometry with it, and a getter-backed `Point` is a legitimate shape rather than misuse — §1.1's explicit carve-out for taking ownership. A sweep for `Number.isFinite` takes it out with the throw.

**`moveTo` and the landing duration are one decision because they are one path, and the audit's trace is executed here rather than read.** `moveTo` → `offsetX`/`offsetY` → `rendered` → `LandingContext.from` → `distance: Math.hypot(target − from)`, handed to the `duration` function D-67 added the context for. A **conforming** author writing `({ distance }) => distance / SPEED` receives a **library-minted** non-finite value. Deleting either check alone is safe — the other catches the result and it classifies. Deleting both turns `moveTo(Infinity)` into a **held settlement gate with no terminal**, which this architecture calls its one unclassifiable failure. **That is not a reason to keep either**, and §1.1 says why: what the library goes on to compute from input the contract never admitted is part of the undefined behaviour. It is the reason for one rationale and one test — `tests/free-drag/validation.browser.test.ts` §_a non-finite moveTo() reaching the landing distance_, which pins the minted `distance`, the compounding `Infinity` case, and the benign `NaN` one the deleted `=== Infinity` predicate never fired for.

**Every deleted rejection becomes an asserted outcome**, the form this package already uses for the four frame checks removed on 2026-08-22 — a colliding key overwrites `phase`, a `__proto__` data property nulls the frame's prototype, an adopted item is mechanized and then lost one seam later as `sortable/insertion-placeholder-lost`, a non-finite `moveTo` poisons the derived geometry and reports nothing, a non-finite `home` composes into the target unattributed. **A returning guard argues with a test rather than with a silence.**

**One documented guarantee is lost, and it is recorded rather than buried.** Resolution and the deleted duration test both preceded the reduced-motion collapse, deliberately, so a consumer diagnosing a bug did not get a different answer because of the reader's OS setting. With no test left the collapse meets the value first: under `reduce` an unbounded duration lands normally, without it the gate hangs. That row is now the strongest argument available to anyone who wants the guard back, which is why it is pinned rather than deleted.

**Where the shipped TSDoc promised a rejection it now states the boundary** — `moveTo`, `LandingOptions.duration`, `PlaceholderFactory` and `ResolveHome` — in the form `box` already used: _nothing detects a violation_, and what the violation does instead. That is the audit's §5 cross-cutting finding acted on rather than noted.

**Measured by composition, and the shape of the table is the evidence.** Brotli **−76 to −216 B** on every row reaching a deleted site, minified −267 to −653 B, **0 B on both control rows**. The kernel root moves 76 B on the frame checks alone because it reaches no behavior; the free-drag rows move most where `landing()` is composed; `both behaviors` moves −216 B because it is the only row carrying every site. `drag.js` is byte-identical at **121 B for the fifth consecutive slice**. **Twelve budgets re-base** to landed + 150 B: slack had reached 226–387 B and the movement is far outside the ±25 B band this phase has demonstrated three times — the same rule D-119 declined to apply to a ±24 B slice, read in the direction it is usually read.

**F-99 is what the slice found on its way past.** `DOMRealm.isElement` lost its only production caller with `placeholder-not-adoptable`'s fourth arm, and `DOMRealm` is published — so removing the member is a public-surface question under §12 and §4, not a size cleanup. Recorded because a member whose last caller was deleted is indistinguishable from one that was always for consumers.

### D-124's landing, reviewed against D-121 … D-123, 2026-08-25 (F-100)

**The two slices were authored in parallel and do not collide.** D-124 deleted five guards; D-121 … D-123 published three contract terms. The one overlap is `validateFramePart`, and it was handled correctly in both directions: D-124 removed the kernel-key and `__proto__` arms, kept the symbol arm, and recorded in the source why — _"D-122 has since decided to close it that way — publish the string-key term and drop this arm — and that is a separate landing; until it lands, this check is the only one here with a live case."_ The uniqueness refusal and D-80 (b)'s ordering comment are untouched, which is what D-121 and F-98 require while the `items` term is unpublished.

**F-97 is partially discharged, and the discharged half is the model for the rest.** The frame arms revised the `FramePartOf` sentence **in the same change** rather than after it — struck in place, reversed to _this type is the contract_, and followed by what the misuse now buys. It stays open for `dispatch/tag-out-of-range` and `spec/command-type-pointerdown`, whose shipped sentences still publish the precondition and promise the guard. **The second of those carries a tension worth naming before anyone acts on it**: D-118 decided that check stays four days earlier as _the only one here the library owns_, which is a clause-(b) argument the gate now reaches only after (a) — a re-put of D-118, not a defect in it.

**One real gap, and it points back at D-122** (F-100). [`kernel/frames.ts:125`](../src/kernel/frames.ts) states the authoring contract as a _string-keyed_ data record and claims it _is published on `FramePartOf`_. It is not — neither the source TSDoc nor the emitted `kernel/frames.d.ts` carries a key-kind term, and the only place the term is written is a comment on an **internal** function. The hazard is specific: an implementer opening D-122's slice reads that line, believes the publication half is done, and lands the deletion alone — leaving the term published nowhere and the check that enforced it gone. **F-94's shape rather than a slip**: a claim about a publication, written beside the thing it describes, never read back against the surface it names.

### The middle-tier boundary, decided 2026-08-25 (D-125, D-126)

**Two questions taken together because they have one answer.** Record [`middle-tier-boundary-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/middle-tier-boundary-claude.md). The rule both fall out of: **publish what the protocol's producers need in order to produce, and not what its implementers happen to use.**

**`insertionAt` is published as `(destination, index, snapshot)`** (D-125). All seven internal call sites already hold the snapshot they take `version` from, and so does a third-party axis — `resolve(frame, runtime)` is handed `runtime.snapshot` and `InsertionRuntimeView` is already published. Same arity, one token per site, no new dependency and no runtime cost; what it buys is that the stray-number spelling stops existing rather than being caught later. **Deriving the destination view inside is refused on §0 grounds**: `y()` and `xy()` call this per resolution and pass the rect cache's maintained list precisely so nothing is allocated on a pointer-move path.

**`homeInsertion` is not published, and the reason is structural.** Exactly one contribution slot _produces_ an `Insertion` — `AxisContribution.insertion` — so the sole third-party producer is the axis — and the home gap is already spelled in that protocol as `null` (I-15), which is cheaper than building it and is the documented meaning. What `homeInsertion` spells is the rule over the undeleted list, an allocation trade D-119 exempted deliberately rather than a contract term to freeze.

**Free drag's direct factory collapses; the sortable's stays as a test seam** (D-126). Neither reaches the published artifact — both are tree-shaken, measured in the emitted JS and `.d.ts`. `createFreeDragBehavior` has **no caller anywhere**, and every free-drag test constructs through the public entry, so no lower seam has anything to reach. The sortable's is **not** duplication: both factories delegate to the same private `install()`, so the browser suite exercises the production wiring rather than a parallel one that could drift — and the slots it takes are unreachable through the public config, since `axis` is required by D-77 and `EMPTY_SLOTS` carries a stub. The two behaviors diverge here without breaking F-67's one-rule requirement: the rule is the same — a test seam exists where a test drives it — and only the facts differ.

**Neither could have become public surface anyway.** Both take an already-assembled slot record, and `feature.ts` is explicit that `SortableSlots` is where the published closure stops (D-61) — so _genuine public/internal seam_ was never among the available answers.

**One finding recorded** (F-101): two of the sortable seam's three call sites do not exercise it — a type test that never calls it, and the duplicate-element control D-121 retires — so after D-121 it rests on `createHarness` alone. Still sufficient, and a thinner reason than the call count suggests.

### The gate's contract consequences, landed 2026-08-25 (D-121 … D-123, D-125 … D-127)

**One slice, because the deletions are consequences of the publications and not a second sweep.** Three contract terms are published — collection distinctness on `items`, string keys on `FramePartOf`, `insertionAt` from `sortable/feature.js` — and what each publication makes unowned is then deleted. Landing them apart would have left a term published with its check still standing, or worse, a check deleted with its term published nowhere, which is exactly the hazard F-100 recorded.

**The collection's uniqueness refusal goes, and the copy stays** (D-121). `ItemSource` publishes distinctness as what makes `{ from, to }` well-formed rather than as a restriction, with what a violation does instead; `copyUniqueItems` is **gone entirely**: with the `Set` and the `throw` removed it was a one-expression wrapper, and renaming it to `copyItems` only moved the problem — a `[...items]` spread does not earn a function any more than `destinationOf`'s `.filter()` did (D-127 (a)). The copy stays at all three mint boundaries, spelled inline, with the §9 ownership reason at the two sites that state an ordering. **D-80 (b)'s statement ordering survives on F-69 alone** (F-98) — `merged.items()` is consumer code and may throw with installer hooks recorded and unrun — and the comment says so rather than being deleted with F-68's window. **The interesting half is what happened to the tests.** Two composition rows went, because the throw they arranged no longer exists. Two others were **re-pointed**: the version-accounting row and the post-`destroy()` row each used a duplicate as their _invalid input_, and the property each pins — a pull that produces no collection consumes no version; an inert controller does not reach the consumer's own code — is live and had no remaining trigger in the harness. They now use a **throwing `items()`**, the only one left, which had no coverage at all. A property whose only fixture was a retired refusal is a property about to be lost quietly.

**Frame parts are published string-keyed, and the validator goes entirely** (D-122). The term lands on `FramePartOf`'s shipped TSDoc first — F-100's requirement, and the reason that finding existed — stated as what it protects: symbol keys are copied between frames and walked by none of the three `Object.keys` instruments, so an author using one is outside the reach of the checks that would catch a retained node. **With one arm left, the arm was the function**: `validateFramePart` is deleted and `composeFrame` calls the factory straight into `Object.assign`. `assertFrameScrubbed` is documented as **partial** and deliberately not widened to `Reflect.ownKeys` (F-96) — widening it would police input the contract excludes, twice per drag, on every conforming author.

**`insertionAt` is published, and the two proposal tests go with the publication** (D-123, D-125). `sortable/feature` becomes a **runtime** entry: a new emitted module, a `default` condition in the export map, and the sortable middle tier's first runtime export. It is the construction rule for the value the tier's only _producing_ slot returns, so the package stops publishing an obligation whose implementation it withheld. **`buildReorderProposal` lost its destination view with the tests** — that array existed only to re-derive the neighbours and length they compared against — so the release path allocates nothing and carries the author's own `before`/`after` onward. The version and membership tests stay: those are about the pair `(snapshot, insertion)`, and no author can satisfy a snapshot that is gone.

**`createFreeDragBehavior` collapses; `createSortableBehavior` stays and says what it protects** (D-126). F-101's prediction held on contact: the duplicate-element control retired with D-121, the two declaration-test occurrences never called the factory, and **no ceremony was invented to keep the count up** — `createHarness` is the one real caller, which is what D-126 §2.3 always rested on.

**The D-124 landing review's methodological finding is applied** (`bb1d9022`). Assertions that froze the exact downstream shape of undefined behaviour are gone: two `Object.assign` rows that would pass with the library absent, a row pinning Chrome's `animate()` domain that `.plan/measurements/animate-duration-domain.md` already owns, three _no terminal at all_ rows that made the negation of a tier-B invariant a regression contract, and two milder rows freezing the wreckage an adopted item leaves. **What stays is what the pattern was for**: the acceptance tripwires a returning guard must argue with, the executed `moveTo` → offsets → `LandingContext.from` → `distance` coupling, and the rows where a library invariant _survives_ misuse. The counterfactual evidence is in D-121 … D-124 and `COVERAGE.md`; undefined behaviour does not need a regression contract. Two changelog clauses left the published TSDoc with it.

**Four owner source-shape findings land with it** (D-127), and one is rejected by its own measurement. `destinationOf` is inlined — a `.filter()` wrapper does not earn a semantic boundary. The **default-placeholder path stops taking rollback snapshots it discards**: the snapshot moved inside the `undo?.push`, so the library's own `<div>` no longer pays four `getAttribute` reads and four closures per activation for a ledger that is `null`; `restoreAttribute`'s semantics, `removeNamedItem` guard and reentrant ordering are byte-identical. The `layout-animation.ts` cleanup had already landed in `7b4f49b0`, so nothing was done. And **chained assignment in `resetSortableFramePart` was measured and rejected**: −25 B minified, **+1 to +5 B brotli on every sortable row**, because the repeated statements compress better than the shape replacing them.

**Measured by composition, and the split between the behaviors is the evidence.** Every free-drag row moves the same −32 to −34 B and the kernel root −41 B: that is `validateFramePart` and nothing else, because free drag reaches nothing else here. The sortable rows move −99 to −117 B — the same deletion plus the collection and proposal work — and `both behaviors` moves −114 B rather than the sum, because the kernel half is shared and counted once. **Module counts hold exactly on all fourteen rows**: publishing `insertionAt` adds an emitted module to the package and to no composition's graph. `drag.js` is byte-identical at **121 B for the sixth consecutive slice**, baseline B for the fifth. **Twelve budgets re-base** to landed + 150 B, under the same rule and in the same direction as D-124's.

### The owner's source-shape pass, landed 2026-08-25 (D-128)

**Eleven runtime declarations deleted, against two audits that priced them and did not agree with the outcome.** [`source-shape-audit-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/source-shape-audit-claude.md) tabled candidates; [`source-shape-audit-exhaustive-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/source-shape-audit-exhaustive-claude.md) then gave all 182 runtime declarations a verdict, after the first was found to be a candidate list presented as an audit — its own proof being two structurally symmetric pairs where one member was tabled and the twin was not. **The owner's dispositions override five KEEP verdicts**, and that is the decision rather than a discrepancy.

**The frame lifecycle collapses into the language it was wrapping.** `composeFrame` was `Object.assign(a, b)`, `beginFrame` was `Object.assign(draft, current)`, `scrubFrame` was two calls; each is written at its one call site now, where the surrounding comment already carried the rule the name was standing in for. `createKernelFrame` and `resetKernelFields` were a create/reset **mirror pair whose invariant was that their two field lists agree** — which two functions cannot hold — and they become one `DEFAULT_FRAME` literal behind `frame(existing?)`: allocate when called with nothing, reset when called with a frame. That is the group's one surviving helper, and it survives because the shared literal _is_ the invariant.

**The frame-assertion family goes entirely, and that discharges O-12.** `assertFrameShapesMatch`, `assertFrameScrubbed`, `assert`, `sameKeys`, `captureFrameKeys` and `validateFrameDescriptors` are gone, along with `armedKeys`. That obligation booked exactly this machinery as an owner's call, refused to treat it as a size edit because the four are I-27's and I-28's only mechanisms, and priced D-117's ablation D at −240…−266 B **as a rank**. The rank was close: the landed pass measures −226…−252 B. **I-27 and I-28 keep tier C and lose their mechanism** — tier C always meant _stated, not enforceable_ — and **D-108 is narrowed rather than retracted**: it decided _gated or not_ for the checks that exist, and the two seam reports that remain still ship un-gated and are still read off the packed tarball, where the row now also asserts that `kernel/frames.js` ships no diagnostic at all.

**`seamFailed` and `seamDiscarded` go as a pair**, on the exhaustive audit's own §1.1 reasoning: one had no production reader and the other had one, so deleting either alone would leave `SeamOutcome` with one arm named and the other open-coded — a worse reading of the enum than keeping both or inlining both. The D-23 rule `seamFailed`'s comment carried is relocated to `SeamOutcome`, beside the early returns in `runSeam` that actually enforce it.

**What the deletions cost in tests is the part worth reading.** Twenty-two rows went, and nearly all of them asserted `Object.assign` or `Object.keys` through a wrapper — re-spelling those against the language would be testing the language, which is the D-124 landing review's finding arriving from a different direction. **Three properties were re-pointed to the production site rather than deleted**: an unvalidated frame part and a non-deterministic factory are now asserted at `arm()`, where the check used to run; and the `composed > 1` unwind arm — whose only trigger was the shape assertion throwing — is driven by a root whose `addEventListener` refuses, which is the failure that can still happen with both frames standing.

**Measured flat, which is the evidence.** Every composition moves −226 to −252 B and the **kernel root moves −233 B on its own**: this is kernel code, so every row pays for it and none pays more than the kernel does — the exact inverse of the previous slice's table, where the two behaviors split because the deletions were behavior code. Module counts hold on all fourteen rows; `drag.js` is byte-identical at **121 B for the seventh consecutive slice**. Twelve budgets re-base.

**One group is deliberately not resolved and is not in this commit** (F-102). The same working tree comments out `POINTER_OWNERS` and `COMMAND_OWNERS` and rewrites `pathOwnsInteraction` to test `[data-drag-ignore]` alone. That is not a micro-abstraction cleanup: the two tables are documented as deliberately different — the keyboard one _narrower and not a subset_ — the rewrite drops `isContentEditable`, and finishing it means choosing which of three policies survives, which is D-46's product surface.
