Confirmed by compiled probe: every hoisted unique installer is assignable to its behavior's plugin alias, so D-146's _two writers are unrepresentable_ was false at the published surface, and an axis in `plugins` leaks its rect index because only the `axis` position registers `insertion.retire`.

**D-150** answered it by having each multi-writer group name its refusals — four `?: never` clauses and a declaration instrument. The owner reopened it the same day: a group should not carry negative knowledge of its siblings to defend an invariant that is not a property of the group, and the check belongs at the composition boundary where the whole composition is in view.

**D-151** is that check, and the reopening paid for itself twice over — [`composition-check-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/composition-check-claude.md).

- **The invariant was misstated, by everyone including the reviews** (F-143). It is not cardinality. The merge already resolves every atomic key to one installer and every unique group _requires_ its unique member, so the count is one before any check runs and an accumulated fold would count to one and stop. The violation is **ownership**: one writer at a position the assembler never reads. The positional statement implies the cardinality one and needs no merge model at all, because a misplaced installer is wrong whether or not it survives.
- **The unique-slot set is derived rather than listed** — a key a sibling group declares and the plugin group does not — so a future capability group joins it by being declared, and the maintenance obligation D-150 defended with an instrument disappears instead of being policed.
- **Two type-level traps were found by compiling, not by reasoning** (F-144, F-145). `keyof` of a union is the intersection of its keys, so the natural spelling of the check can never fail; and without `const` type parameters, subtype reduction collapses `[goodPlugin, axisInstaller]` before the check sees it. The first prototype failed on exactly the second and on nothing else.
- **One case is given up by owner direction**: explicit widening to the plugin alias. It is the one thing D-150 caught that this does not, it is bounded by the entry being the only install path, and it is a documentation obligation on the slot.

### The remediation pass landed, 2026-08-28 (D-147, D-148, D-149, D-151; F-132…F-136 closed)

All four decisions implemented as one slice, because each one's subject sits in a file another one rewrites.

- **D-147 and D-148 are what they said.** Two `reverse()` calls out, four backwards walks in; `AxisSource` deleted, `slots.axis` narrowed to `DragAxis`, `action.effect`'s `TAG_POLICY` branch and the I-36 barrier deleted with the site. COVERAGE row **L-3** and its two controls are struck rather than ported.
- **D-149's rewrite obligation was dischargeable in full.** Neither of the two states the record flagged as watch-items needed a finding. `PresentationView.insertion` outside its bracket is read from the object the production path hands a declared slot — its contracted observer — and every failure exit is reachable from a slot: a throwing `measureInsertion`, a throwing `invalidateInsertion` (from the _second_ call, since activation invalidates too), a throwing hook, a cross-container anchor. The frame task's stale-attempt path is driven by a real drag, and the attempt number is the one the production task dispatched rather than one the test chose. What the five sites keep is a **capture**, not a mutation: the install's own `spec`, a `dispatch` decorator over the host, and the per-operation view as a slot receives it.
- **One thing the rewrite taught, and it confirms the record's own warning.** The re-anchor barrier row is **not discriminating** with an honest placeholder: teardown detaches it, so `item.parentElement === placeholder.parentElement` short-circuits one conjunct before the latch is read. It needs a consumer element that proxies `parentElement` — which is exactly what the old comment said and what the stub-host version was quietly relying on the absence of. Falsified both ways before keeping.
- **D-151 landed at the twelve cases the prototype predicted**, at the real signatures and through the packed declarations. One deviation: the per-behavior `SortableUnique`/`FreeDragUnique` aliases are **inlined**, because naming them in a public signature obliges `tests/docs.node.test.ts` to see them exported (F-51's closure rule, not F-146 — the citation was wrong when written, F-159). The ordinary tier gains one erased name per behavior instead of two.
- **Both `install()` functions are inlined**, on owner direction and after the fact. Each was a name for the handshake's two constructor calls, and D-149 is what made that visible: with the runtime aggregate gone, `install` had nothing left to assemble. Free drag had one caller. The sortable has two, so the pair is written twice — the cost is stated in the module header, and what D-126's seam protects does not depend on the indirection, because both factories reach the same two constructors with the same arguments. `BehaviorInstall` is untouched: it is the published return type of `BehaviorFactory` (D-68) and would be part of the kernel tier's closure whether or not anything internal named it.
- **The fixtures that had to move were misuse.** Two packed-consumer fixtures installed a `ConstraintInstaller` through `plugins` — the very defect — and now fill `bounds`. Both packed fixtures gained a refusal row, falsified by swapping the offender for a legitimate plugin and watching the directive go unused.

### One classified fault had three transports, decided 2026-08-28 (D-152, F-152…F-154)

Owner review of `free-drag/spec.ts`'s release invariant asked two questions that turned out to separate — [`rejection-transport-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/rejection-transport-claude.md).

- **`SeamRejection` is residue and goes.** All six sites return exactly the stage their seam is already run with, and a `throw` at the same branch reaches the same `context.fail` → `failOperation` → one checkpoint → one `DraggableError` — a fact `requestFailure` already states in its own comment (D-49). `host.fail` remains for the case that needs a different stage. The union was on two seams out of six, so it expressed _these two seams may fail in a second way_ rather than _a seam may fail_ (F-153).
- **The `drag: <area>/<condition>` token is not a second taxonomy, and D-117 is the reason.** D-117 reached the same reading first — _the prose was doing classification work the coarse vocabulary declines to do_ — and answered it by stripping prose to identities, not by deleting them. D-132 replaced that premise's subject and did not weaken it: `FAILURE_RELEASE` separates the release family exactly as badly as `interaction` did. **Neither row cited the other, which is the whole of why the question came back** (F-152), and the citation is now written into both.
- **Amended the same day on owner challenge** — the identity rule is keyed to **whether anything else says what happened**, not to whether the `Error` was synthesised. The three-way ownership model behind the challenge is right and has a missing cell that holds most of the sites: a **P1 fault the library detects itself, with nothing caught**. And the premise did not survive the record: `sortable/release-no-proposal` is P1 **on the call graph** (D-119 §6.3), three siblings are P1 by default, and **F-94** is a tier-B row filed because the same P2-by-assertion chain ran through the same family once already. D-152's original appeal to D-117 was the error — D-117 answers _how much text_, having assumed the message exists (F-155, F-156).
- **Two sentences the record owed.** A behavior raises a **cause** and mints nothing; the kernel mints the `DraggableError`, and `handleFailed` is the only place one is built **for an operation** — true today, stated nowhere, and load-bearing because a stale checkpoint is demoted and a cancel latch outranks it. The qualifier is not decoration (F-160): `panic` and a throwing `admit` mint two more, both outside the operation channel, so _nowhere else_ was false as written while the rule it stands for was not. And the consumer-facing negative: `stage` is the classification and the whole of it, `message` and `cause` are diagnostics, nothing may branch on either (F-154).

### One classified fault, two transports, landed 2026-08-28 (D-152; F-146…F-156 closed)

**`SeamRejection` is deleted.** A non-discardable seam now fails the way the other four already did — by throwing — and the deletion is a deletion rather than a move: `isRejection`, the two `requestFailure` adapters, the union arm on both published `BehaviorSpec` members and the type's export from `kernel.js` all go with it.

- **The transport carried nothing the kernel lacked, and the implementation confirms it site by site.** Each of the eight return sites named exactly the stage its seam was already running at, so every one becomes a bare `throw` and the stage comes from the open phase. `runPhase` catches, calls `context.fail(stage, raised)` and returns `FAILED`; the deleted path called `requestFailure`, which set `failureRequested` and called the same `context.fail` — after which `runPhase` returned `FAILED` regardless of the adapter's `null`. **Same function, same argument, same `SEAM_PREPARE_FAILED`.**
- **The census undercounted by two, and the type is what found them.** The record's table enumerated six `rejection(...)` calls; the two `SETTLED_REJECTED` arms build the same shape as a bare object literal, so a search for the helper missed them. They are the amendment's _caught-cause_ row and became `throw input.error` — the consumer's own rejection value, re-raised verbatim, with no identity added.
- **The identities ship unchanged**, including `free-drag/release-no-visual`, on the amended rule: where nothing was caught, the identity is the only description of the fault that exists. The published negative that makes them safe to ship is now on `DraggableError`'s own JSDoc — _`stage` is the classification and the whole of it_ — and therefore in `kernel/errors.d.ts`.
- **One mint site, stated where it happens.** `handleFailed` is the only place an operation's `DraggableError` is built, and the comment there now says why: the stage is not final at the raise site, because a stale checkpoint demotes to a `DraggableWarning` and a held cancel latch outranks the checkpoint outright.

**Measured, from the implementation state** (F-150). Composition rows fall **−207 to −209 B** minified and −57 to −82 B brotli — `free drag complete` is the −209 and was outside the range first quoted (F-161) — with `both behaviors` at −265 B. **`kernel.js` moves for the first time in this series**: −152 B minified, −45 B brotli, because the probe and both adapters were kernel code.

**The premium figure this paragraph first carried is withdrawn** (F-157). ~~131 B → 113 B, 1.07% of `complete`~~, and ~~baseline A falls −209 B with the rows~~: baseline A had been importing a module D-149 deleted, and built only because the previously-emitted `sortable/runtime.js` survived on disk. Repaired and re-measured at each commit, the premium is **206 B → 208 B, 1.96%**, and the series since D-146 is flat at 221 → 222 → 206 → 208 rather than descending. Baseline A falls **−207 B / −69 B** here — slightly _more_ than composed `complete`'s −67 B brotli — which is what a change entirely inside the kernel should do. The instrument is repaired with it: `just build` cleans first, and `tests/bench/size.node.test.ts` refuses a generated file older than its own build. [`bundle-structure.md`](bundle-structure.md) §The denominator was broken carries the table.

### The measurement instrument was measuring a fixture, landed 2026-08-28 (F-157…F-163 closed)

**Review of the D-152 pass confirmed the transport deletion in full and found the size harness broken** — [`d152-transport-deletion-review-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/d152-transport-deletion-review-claude.md). All eight sites, the precedence and demotion paths, the deleted declarations and the packed surface reproduced independently; nothing about D-152 is reopened here.

- **F-157 is the substance, and it is older than the pass that reported it.** Baseline A imported `createSortableRuntime` and called the one-argument `createSortableSpec`, both deleted at `cdc83990`. It kept building because the bundler emits and overwrites but never removes: once the aggregate left `sortable/runtime.ts` no chunk was emitted for that module, and the file already on disk stayed importable. **On a clean checkout `just size` did not run at all.** Three passes measured against it and one review reproduced its arithmetic without asking whether the fixture still compiled against the library.
- **The repair is at the module, because the drift is not at the call.** `just build` cleans first, so an orphaned output cannot exist; and `tests/bench/size.node.test.ts` refuses any generated file older than its own build, over `files.json`'s own clean pathspecs. Scoring by **age** rather than by name is what makes it independent of every graph declaration — and it has to be, because the two baselines declare no topology at all. The fixture's own stated guard compares `buildSlots`'s slot keys against `assemble()` and never calls `mount()`, which is the half that drifted; a fixture that would throw on its first line still bundles and still reports a number.
- **The corrected numbers say something better than the wrong ones did.** The premium is **208 B, 1.96%**, and the series since D-146 is 221 → 222 → 206 → 208 — **flat**, not descending. Every apparent fall was the denominator shrinking. 03 §What isolation cannot shake asked whether composition overhead grows with the feature count; _stable_ is the answer it wanted, and it was true all along.
- **F-158 is the class F-148 named, one document deeper.** Eleven `SeamRejection` references in contracts 02, 05 and 06, of which two were false rather than stale: _classifies at the named stage_ describes a transport that carried a stage, and the seam owns the stage now. Names are cheap to grep and semantics are not, which is why the sweep is the finding rather than the count.
- **F-162 is F-151's asymmetry recurring in the pass that closed F-151.** Free drag's `SETTLED_REJECTED` re-raise had no witness; sortable's did. The stage assertion alone does not discriminate — `FAILURE_RESOLUTION` is the seam's own — so the new row pins the cause **by identity**, and substituting a fresh `Error` at the raise site fails it and nothing else.
- **F-160 narrows a sentence rather than a rule.** `handleFailed` is the only mint site **for an operation**; `panic` and a throwing `admit` mint two more outside the operation channel. The in-source comment carried the qualifier and the record dropped it in transcription.

### The error classes stop labelling themselves, landed 2026-08-28

Owner change. `DraggableWarning` declares **no constructor at all** — `extends Error {}` — and `this.name = …` leaves both classes; seventeen call sites move to the native `new DraggableWarning(reason, { cause })`.

- **`instanceof` was already the whole identity** (D-130 §2.2), so a `name` of its own was a second discriminator saying what the first says exactly — and a weaker one, because a string is copyable and a prototype is not. The published JSDoc now states the consequence rather than leaving it to be discovered: a logged fault heads `Error: drag: …`, and the `drag: <area>/<condition>` identity D-152 defends is on that same line regardless.
- **Pinned, because it reads like housekeeping.** `tests/kernel/errors.node.test.ts` asserts `name` is `'Error'` on both classes; falsified by re-adding the label. The row exists so the trade is re-taken deliberately if anyone puts it back.
- **`drag.js` moves — the row built to notice.** 344 → 252 B minified, 159 → **142 B** brotli. The vocabulary root is two modules and one is `errors.ts`.
- **Minified rises while brotli falls**, on every composition row: +12 to +28 B against −4 to −13 B. `{ cause: error }` is more characters than a positional argument and seventeen near-identical literals compress better than the shapes they replaced. Brotli is the reported figure, so it is a saving — the minified column alone would have called it a cost, which is worth recording as the one case in this package where the two columns disagree in sign.
- **The premium is 211 B, 1.99%**, up 3 B: baseline A composes the same `errors.ts` by hand and takes the same saving.

### The re-entry latch stops naming itself, landed 2026-08-28

Owner review. `refuseReentry` built `new Error('drag: seam/re-entered')` and threw it; the latch now sets a boolean and throws a **description-less module-private symbol**.

- **The invariant machinery is untouched, and that was the constraint.** Nesting is still refused before `begin()` mutates anything; the refusal still _latches_, so behavior code that catches the throw panics anyway; the escape still bypasses `runPhase`'s classification to reach `drain`'s panic path. What changed is only the value that unwinds — and the latch, not the value, was always the decision, which is why the value could go.
- **F-85 is what licenses it, and it was already settled.** The site is P2 on the call graph: every phase-opening call is inside a queue handler, so no behavior-facing entry can open a nested phase. A condition no consumer can reach has nobody to name it to.
- **The consumer-facing fault is unchanged and slightly truer.** `panic` mints `DraggableError(null, thrown)`; a non-`Error` cause has no message for the constructor to adopt, so it mints `drag: controller destroyed` — an identity, so 05's floor holds, and the one a consumer can actually act on.
- **It defeats a premise D-152 recorded as decisive.** _You cannot throw nothing — a blank sentinel `Error` gives `cause.message === ''`, which the constructor adopts._ True of an `Error`, and that is the whole of it: throw a non-`Error` and the existing fallthrough does the right thing with no rule undone. The decline stands for the eight sites it was taken over; the mechanism argument does not.
- **Thirteen assertions moved**, from `/re-entered/u` to _something unwound and it was not an ordinary failure_ — a symbol with no description, which behavior code cannot raise by accident. A bare `.toThrow()` was refused: it passes for exactly the escape the latch exists to prevent.
- **The bytes go the other way, and it is the same effect this package measured once before.** Minified falls 27–30 B on every row; **brotli rises up to 10 B** on the sortable rows. The withdrawn string shared `drag: ` and its word fragments with forty sibling identities, so it was a _dictionary contributor_ — D-117's measurement found the parts understating the whole by 2–9 % for that reason, and this is that effect run backwards. `kernel.js` falls both ways, −30 B / −7 B. The change was not made for bytes and does not earn any.

### The re-entry guard reassessed under the reachability gate, decided 2026-08-28

Owner challenge, after `CODE_OF_SIZE.md` §1.1 made reachability a decisive gate. **Should the guard exist at all, given F-85 proved the state unreachable?** Decision D-153: it stays, the sentinel becomes `null`, and the ground is new. Record [`reviews/phase-23/reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/reentry-guard-reachability-claude.md).

- **The question was open and I said otherwise.** F-85 resolves a **P-class**, D-117 (b) governs a **payload**, and §1.1 states the separation in its own text — _this section decides whether a check exists; §1.3 decides whether its message ships_. Neither row asked whether the check should exist, and both were being cited as though they had. **F-155's move, four days later, in a ledger row instead of in prose** (F-168).
- **The reachability claim holds, re-derived rather than carried forward.** Eleven phase-opening sites at `fac8276e`, not F-85's nine — the proof had already drifted — every one in `handle`'s call tree, `handle` passed to `drain` at two sites, `drain` returning while `queue.running`. A behavior calling the driver is impossible by scope; the real vector is the kernel opening a phase from inside foreign code, and every entry that could provoke one enqueues.
- **§1.1's gate does not reach it, and that is not an argument for keeping it.** The section governs what the library owes a party who left the contract, and this state has no such party; read literally it deletes every internal assertion in the package, D-117's eight P2 sites included. The document limits itself twice — §1.1 names the one outcome it reopens and this is not it, and §0 cites this latch by name at **~1 ns, _a cost that does not exist_**.
- **What keeps it is that it is the instrument, not the machinery.** Four other pieces of kernel state are written against non-reentrancy; the claim is a call-graph property no test holds; the guard fails loudly at the first edit that breaks it. **New ground, stated so it can be rejected** — and priced for that: **20–50 B Brotli**, both control rows unmoved.
- **The counterfactual was run, and the record's account of it was wrong** (F-169). Not _publishes a half-built frame_: the swaps cancel, so nested from `prepare` **neither** transaction lands and nested from `effect` the outer's frame is replaced wholesale — both returning `SEAM_COMMITTED` with **no diagnostic on either path**. §1.1's instruction to verify before _defending_ a check, not only before deleting one, is what found it.
- **`throw null` closes F-164 and F-166 together**, and is smaller than the symbol it replaces. No second sentinel to collapse into `FAILED`; no `TypeError` when a consumer's `onError` interpolates `cause`. The general form: an identity withdrawal picks a replacement, and the replacement lands on a public field even when the identity did not.

### The re-entry sentinel becomes `null`, landed 2026-08-28 (D-153; F-164…F-169)

The guard is **not** reopened: D-153 settles that it stays, as the enforcement point four other pieces of kernel state are written against and as the only pin on a reachability claim derived by hand three times. What lands is the sentinel and three corrections to the record.

- **`throw null` at both escape points**, the `REENTERED` declaration deleted, the boolean latch and the refusal/unwind structure untouched. Every property the symbol was picked for survives: no message to leak, nothing for the constructor to adopt, `drag: controller destroyed` minted, and the latch — not the value — remaining the whole decision.
- **F-166 is what makes `null` better rather than merely smaller.** The value lands on `DraggableError.cause`, a public field; a symbol there throws `TypeError` on `` `${err.cause}` ``, and `notify`'s terminus swallows that throw, so a consumer whose `onError` logs with a template literal lost the report entirely — on the one fault meaning _the controller is gone_. `null` interpolates to `'null'`, and is the truer value besides: on this path there is no cause.
- **F-164 dissolves rather than being asserted.** With no second module-private symbol there is nothing for a future edit to collapse into `FAILED`, and `FAILED`'s own JSDoc claim is again a claim about one symbol.
- **The tests moved with it, and the sentinel that stayed is the load-bearing one.** `expectReentryPanic` pins `null` exactly; that is only an assertion because `escapeOf` reports a completed run as `NOTHING_THROWN` rather than as a falsy value of its own — otherwise the row would pass vacuously against a refusal that stopped refusing.
- **F-167 corrects a reading, not the runtime.** `openStage` marks the **foreign-code window**: it is cleared before `runPhase` classifies, so the guard is not a general nesting interlock and the record's _a phase never opens while a phase is open_ claimed coverage it never had. Nothing opens a phase past the clear — `context.fail` enqueues — so widening the guard would be machinery for a state nothing produces, which §1.1 refuses.
- **F-169 replaces a described counterfactual with a measured one**, in `seams.ts`, contract 02 and F-85's row alike. A nested `begin()` does **not** publish a half-built frame: the swaps cancel, so nested from `prepare` neither transaction lands, and nested from `effect` the outer's committed frame is replaced wholesale while the staged command belongs to the outer. Both return `SEAM_COMMITTED` with no diagnostic — the defence was right and its stated reason was not, which is the harder half of §1.1's rule.
- **Two follow-ups from the narrow review, closed the same day.** **F-170**: the latch's authority was pinned only in the direction that panics, and `if (reentry || raised === null)` passed the whole suite — reachable from a supported call, because both behaviors re-raise a consumer's rejection verbatim and `Promise.reject(null)` hands the driver its own escape value. One sortable row now holds the negative through the public flow. **This is F-164's shape with the polarity reversed**: dissolving the symbol removed the collapse target and introduced a collision with a value a consumer can supply, and it is the stronger risk, since `Symbol()` was unobtainable and `null` is one `Promise.reject` away. **F-171**: the F-167 correction itself claimed two `context.notify` arms sit inside the foreign-code window when one does, and reached the right conclusion by half a route — consumer code _does_ run past the clear, and `queue.running` rather than `context.fail` is what covers it.
- **F-165's range convention is applied to this pass's own figures**, which is the only way that finding closes rather than recurring: sortable −17 to −9, free-drag **−9 to +9**, and the positive row is named.

### Cancellation provenance, and L-11 closes against its own deliverable, 2026-08-28 (D-154, F-172, F-173)

**Landed 2026-08-29** — [`reviews/phase-23/cancellation-provenance-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/cancellation-provenance-claude.md). The owner's question: are the three kernel-minted cancellation reasons part of the consumer-facing semantic result, or internal detail leaking through a generic channel? **This is ledger L-11**, and the answer **declines and replaces the deliverable written for it above**.

**The standing deliverable answers L-2's question rather than L-11's.** L-2 asks whether a name is reachable and unexported; its remedy is always _export it_. It never asks whether the channel is the right place for the information, and the three bullets above export five strings without asking.

**`reason` has five producers, and the fifth decides it.** Neither behavior has a `failed` arm: `SETTLED_FAILED` maps to `{ type: 'canceled', reason: input.error }`, so the slot holds a consumer value, a behavior value, a kernel value, nothing, and **an error thrown by code the library does not own** — indistinguishably. `reason === CANCEL_ESCAPE` is unsound by construction. **Independently**, provenance on a consumer-writable channel is a claim rather than a fact: `cancel('drag:escape')` already forges it, and exporting the sentinel institutionalises it. **And the three strings are not three causes** — `pointercancel` and `lostpointercapture` are two DOM spellings of one fact, while `Escape` differs in kind, which the ingress structure corroborates: a command operation arms no pointer input, so two of the three are unreachable on the keyboard ingress.

**What lands: `origin: CancelOrigin`** — `SUPPLIED`, `ABORTED`, `INTERRUPTED`, `FAILED` — on the canceled arm of both behaviors, published on `CancelStage`'s existing route and **not** on `drag.js`. Three orthogonal fields: `origin` is who decided, `stage` is when, `reason` is what the decider had to say and stays `unknown`. The three `drag:` strings are deleted; the sortable's two survive and are exported, which is the second bullet above intact. **SC-1 fires** and its trigger text — _five runtime cells onto two frozen entrypoints_ — describes the declined deliverable and is rewritten with this one. No byte claim is made until `bench/size` says so.

**F-172** (closed): 07 §The results listed _a destroyed controller_ as a producer of the canceled arm; `destroy()` publishes no terminal at all, and the suite asserts the absence twice. **F-173** (open, tier B): the fifth producer appears in none of the three documents that enumerate what `reason` carries, so the deliverable they prescribe is unsound because of what they omit — **an open channel's producer set is a contract fact and needs an owner**.

**What the implementation added to the decision, and what it cost.** The kernel's `cancel` splits into an internal `cancelWith(reason, origin)` and the `KernelHost.cancel` face that is always `CANCEL_SUPPLIED`; `pointercancel` and `lostpointercapture` share one `case`, which is the decision's _two spellings of one fact_ written as code rather than asserted about it; and `CANCEL_FAILED` is minted by each behavior's own D-66 fallback, the one origin a behavior writes. **SC-1's third trigger fired and the re-measurement it obliges found no re-base owed** — every row under budget, with slack running 63–630 B across the fourteen and 456–630 B on the eleven that carry a behavior (F-175). **The byte direction the decision declined to predict is up**: +22 to +23 B minified on every behavior row and +4 to +19 B Brotli, against −4 B minified on `kernel.js` and 0 B on both rows that carry no behavior. A field on a result costs a property at every site that builds one; three literals at three call sites do not pay for it. Figures in [`measurements/budget-rebases.md`](measurements/budget-rebases.md) §SC-1 fires.

---

---

**The remediation pass, landed 2026-08-22 (D-116 and seven review findings).** The slice was reviewed against its own fail-open rule and eight findings came back, none blocking. **MNT-03 reached the design and is D-116**, above. The other seven are implementation. **MNT-01** — D-113's guard read the emitted tree and asserted nothing about having found one, so it passed with all 33 declarations moved aside, which is the ordinary state of a fresh clone; it now carries three floors, and the third asserts its one exemption is exercised rather than merely available. **MNT-02** — the same guard caught one of the four shapes its own defect can take: single-line blocks, 71 of 200, were never classified at all; the `~~` exemption fired on any strike-through anywhere in a block and so exempted nine live ones, including `SortableInstaller`'s own; and the module-header exemption fired on position. The first two are fixed by classifying the block and by reading its **subject** — a deletion note opens with the struck name of what is gone. The third is **deleted rather than tightened**: a block first in a region is textually indistinguishable from an orphan injected there, so the two module headers became `//` comments in source, which is D-113's own repair to `kernel/spec.ts` applied to the same shape. All four forms were injected and all four now fail. **MNT-04** — a citation naming its document in backticks was swallowed by the history census's any-backticked-token alternative, so ten checkable citations were never resolved; the backticked form now resolves like the linked one, against the citing file's directory, the package root and the record, and a document that resolves nowhere is a failure rather than a demotion. It surfaced one real dangler, `§Corrections item 3`, now delimited as `§Corrections to the record, item 3`. **MNT-05** — Check D-56 was withdrawn in the register and still stated as owed at the two `plan.md` sites a reader arrives at first, which is F-81's mechanism on the register's own headline disposition; both now carry the withdrawal and point at O-8. **MNT-06** — the register was absent from `.plan/README.md`'s index of its own directory, which is where a human owner starts. **MNT-07** — the pass's three instruments had no rows in `tests/COVERAGE.md`, whose checker runs one direction only and therefore could not report them. **F-83 and MNT-08** — three of the four new decision rows carried a fourth cell against a three-column header, so their already-written _closes …_ clauses did not render, and F-82's row was truncated mid-sentence at the same boundary; both are repaired and none of the wording is re-decided.

**The closure pass, landed 2026-08-22.** The remediation was reviewed in turn and left five concrete residuals, none of them needing a decision. **C-01** — SC-1's re-derived citation, the one D-116 (c) exists to repair, sat inside a code span at the site D-116 (b) added to scope precisely so it would be checked, so the specimen rule exempted it; it is written as a citation now, and the specimen rule is untouched for the quotations that are quotations. **C-02** — D-116 (d) called its own gap prospective and a fourth lead-in was already spelled, in the row above D-106: D-105's _reopening conditions_ are registered as **SC-4** and the row points at it, the backstop's vocabulary carries the fourth form, and the standing set is four rather than three of four. **D-116 (a)** read against its own headline — _cites the id instead of stating the condition twice_ would have deleted the historical wording every landed site keeps — and is corrected to what landed: the row names or summarizes the condition as it was decided, and the live authoritative form is the register's. **The MNT-04 residue** — `NAMED` matched `.md` alone while the rule as written said _a document named in backticks_, so nine citations naming a `.ts` target stayed swallowed; a named source file is now indexed the way its citations name it — a `describe` title, a ruled-off comment lead-in, a top-level declaration — and all nine resolve. **C-03** — F-83's class was wider than its own survey: eighteen further rows dropped a whole cell and two carried unescaped pipes inside code spans. All twenty are repaired and the tables are verified by parsing.

### Two DevTools traces read structurally, 2026-08-29 (F-180, F-181, F-182; opens Q-L1)

**Measured, nothing decided** — [`reviews/phase-23/trace-execution-topology-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/trace-execution-topology-claude.md). Two enhanced traces of one manual pointer drag each, `sortable + y() + landing() + layoutAnimation()` against `sortable + y()`, with source maps and source contents embedded. Read from the traces first; the plan narrative was consulted only to compare against the owner's hypotheses. **Three classes of claim are kept apart** — execution topology (strong), runtime milliseconds (manual gestures, never load-bearing), and bundle attribution (a real measurement of these bundles, minified, not the Brotli budget).

**The topology, reconstructed.** Per pointer sample: `onPointer → dispatchKernel → drain → handle → handleMove → begin → runLeaf → runPhase → runMoved → moved → {write, schedule}` — so **the seam phase machinery and the frame transaction are both on the per-sample path**, and the frame is a fixed 14 fields that **does not widen with composition**. Coalescing holds: 70 moves → 36 rAF, 81 → 44. Per spatial frame the generic path runs in full and **most frames stop at `placeholderAt`'s early return**, after one `getBoundingClientRect` on the placeholder in the axis resolve.

**The sharpest structural difference is the terminal.** Without `landing()`, release-to-retirement is **2.1 ms** inside the `pointerup` task; with it, `armSettlement` installs a runner and the kernel holds the operation open **~200 ms**. Landing does not add a step — it converts a synchronous terminal into an asynchronous gate.

**The bundle delta is exact.** 31,850 B against 29,793 B minified; the three optional modules are **2,052 B of a 2,057 B delta**, and **every other module moves by ≤ 10 B** — while the `minimal` fixture still _statically imports_ the two it does not use. So the implementations tree-shake perfectly, and that is precisely the evidence that **none of the generic machinery supporting them is conditional**.

**The centre of gravity, and it is not where the hypotheses put it.** ~**700–825 B** of `kernel.ts` is landing-hold capability unreachable without `landing()`, against **678 B** of landing implementation that tree-shakes: **the optional half of the capability is smaller than the mandatory half.** The displacement bracket's residue is ~387 B and is the _smallest_ of the extensibility residues, not the largest. And `armSettlement`'s anchor measurement **looks** landing-specific and is not — `minimal`'s own join consumes the pin.

**Q-L1 is named and left open**: should the settlement's landing-hold be a kernel capability the behavior installs, rather than a fixed part of the settlement protocol? The note states both sides — the byte asymmetry against `holdForLanding` being the kernel SPI by design, the gate being a lifecycle property, and a variable attempt field set reaching D-10 — and **refuses to answer it on a minified line-range figure**. `bench/size` proof is owed first.

**F-180**: the per-module attribution is a new instrument reaching `bundle-structure.md`'s subject from another direction, never compared. **F-181**: `verified-refresh.ts` is 1,234 B and both its fast path and its full-rebuild fallback fire in one ordinary drag, with the avoidance rate unmeasured. **F-182**: `layoutAnimation()` moves `Layout` per frame from 18/36 to 44/44, so **features are additive in bytes and are not additive in forced layout**.

### Q-L1 gets a measurement contract, 2026-08-29 (F-183)

**Contract only — no probe, no decision** — [`measurements/ql1-landing-hold.md`](measurements/ql1-landing-hold.md). Q-L1 asks whether the settlement's landing-hold should be a kernel capability the behavior installs. It cannot be answered from what exists: the only figure in hand is ~697–825 B of **minified source-map attribution by hand-read line range** in a Vite page build, and the budgets are Brotli from `bench/size` on the package's own build.

**The counterfactual is fixed before the numbers exist.** Seven invariants no arm may weaken — one terminal, release-once in order, the pin unconditional, gate failure classified through both channels, destroy-on-every-exit, `destroy()` as a synchronous barrier, and _the behavior requests, the kernel arms_. **A counterfactual that weakens one is measuring a different settlement protocol and its delta is void**, not approximate.

**Three arms, and the order is the method.** **A** removes the capability outright — unshippable, and the only thing that says what any conditional design could ever save. **B** is the candidate family, measured at its **most favourable honest member**, so a negative bounds the family without enumerating it and the probe cannot be built to flatter a design. **C** degeneralizes in place — `holds: number` has one increment site and a population of one — and **C is the arm that can kill the question**: if it captures most of the ceiling, the bytes were generality rather than topology and the fix has no contract cost.

**The trace's §5.4 refinement is the load-bearing constraint.** `armSettlement`'s anchor half and `joinSettlement`'s pin are **shared** — `minimal` reaches the pin and consumes it — so they are untouchable in every arm, along with `seams.ts`, the frame protocol and `bench/size`'s own row set. An arm that moves `baseline B` or `drag.js` is void.

**The conversion factor is derived, not assumed**: 440 B Brotli against 1,374 B minified for `layoutAnimation` (32 %), 270 B against 678 B for landing (40 %, replicated exactly on both behaviors). Predicted band **~225–330 B**, straddling the threshold — with the hazard stated in advance that interleaved kernel code may compress better than a wholly-added module, so the prediction is nearer an upper bound than a midpoint.

**Thresholds fixed in advance.** Killed if A's ceiling on `minimal` is < ~150 B, or if C captures ≥ ~70 % of it. Worth settling only if the ceiling is ≥ ~300 B, replicated on free drag within ±25 %, localized to `kernel.js`, and B recovers ≥ ~60 % while costing ≤ ~100 B on `minimal + landing`. **Everything between is record-and-decline**, D-106's disposition, reached explicitly rather than by fatigue.

**Bookkeeping in the same pass.** The trace note's three findings were renumbered **F-174…F-176 → F-180…F-182**: the D-154 implementation review had already taken F-174…F-179, and those six live in [`d154-cancel-origin-review-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/d154-cancel-origin-review-claude.md) **without ledger rows**, which is why the collision was invisible at allocation time. The unledgered set is the cause and it is still unledgered.

### Does landing belong to settlement? Q-L1 is parked behind Q-L2, 2026-08-29 (F-184, F-185, F-186)

**Analysis only** — [`reviews/phase-23/landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/landing-ownership-boundary-claude.md). The owner asked, before Q-L1's counterfactual, whether landing animation should be part of settlement semantics at all, and asked that if the hold carries a contract beyond visual polish the contract be named precisely. **It does carry one, it is not polish, and the gate is not what satisfies it.**

**The obligation is real and the library made it itself.** `acquireLift` puts the consumer's element into `position: fixed` **and the top layer**, with a placeholder holding its flow slot. Having removed an element from flow, the library must put it back without a discontinuity — a jump at drop is a defect the lift manufactured.

**The decisive fact is that the pin already discharges it, synchronously, with no landing composed.** `armSettlement` measures the anchor before the landing branch and unconditionally; `joinSettlement` writes it to the lift whether or not a runner was installed. `sortable-minimal.json` shows exactly that — `joinSettlement → runLeaf → runPhase → kernel.ts:1706 → presentation.write` — in a graph with no `landing()` in it. **The pin removes the discontinuity; the landing only makes the pin take 200 ms instead of 0.** Five candidate contracts were tested and none needs the gate: two are discharged by the pin and the presentation lifetime, one holds in `minimal`, and two — the landing failure class and I-7 — are **consequences of the window offered as reasons for it** (F-184).

**What the gate costs, as fact.** The controller is inert for the landing's duration: `openIngress` refuses while an operation is live and `RETIRE` waits on the animation — **206 ms measured, against `minimal`'s 2.1 ms** — and no document states this as a guarantee (F-185). The gate is also a two-holder generalization with a population of one since D-41 deleted readiness, and it is **already conditional on semantic state**, since `RECOVERY_IMMEDIATE` skips it with `landing()` composed.

**The two features split differently, and the difference inverts the design.** `layoutAnimation`'s measure/mutate/measure bracket cannot be CSS, and its cancel-before-measure discipline is a **correctness** requirement — the library reads the geometry those animations perturb — yet it owns no lifecycle. `landing`'s animation perturbs nothing the library ever reads again, and it owns the operation's lifecycle (F-186). **The feature that must control its animation owns nothing; the feature that need not owns everything.**

**Q-L2 is named and not settled**: should the settlement gate be replaced by a **presentation lifetime that outlives the operation** — semantic completion ending at the pin, presentation ending when the interpolation does, interpolation delegable? The note states the honest costs rather than gesturing at them, led by the strongest objection — **something must own a transform on an element after the operation ends** — and records that free drag needs its own answer and does not inherit this one.

**Q-L1 is parked behind it, and its contract is not wasted.** Arm A is a closer counterfactual to Q-L2 than to the question it was written for; arm B may be optimizing a thing that should not exist, and building it first is the waste the parking avoids. That Q-L1 took the hold's existence as its premise is the third instance of the pattern **F-168** named.

### Q-L2 settled: the relinquished tail, 2026-08-29 (D-155; F-187, F-188)

**Decided, not implemented** — [`reviews/phase-23/ql2-presentation-tail-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/ql2-presentation-tail-claude.md).

**The binary hid two objects.** An **exclusive lease on consumer-owned state** — the element `fixed` in the top layer under a style lease, a library placeholder in the consumer's list — is a claim only the library can end, and **keeping the operation alive is the necessary mechanism for holding one past the terminal**. An **interpolation** claims nothing and needs no operation. Both halves of the owner's question are right about different things.

**T1 is rejected and it is what the obvious reading becomes.** Deferring the lease past the terminal fires `onEnd` — the ordinary place a framework re-renders its list — while a library placeholder is still in that list. It keeps every hazard the gate protects against and removes the protection. **T2 is accepted**: release presentation completely at the pin, then interpolate as a **WAAPI effect on the element in flow**. It is sound because **it owns nothing that needs releasing** — no fill, nothing in `style`, self-reverting, one call to cancel, dies with the element. No lease; only a cancel handle.

**Six of the eight cases improve.** Removal, reparenting and replacement need no handling at all, where today a removal mid-landing leaves a lease on a detached node — I-7's own case. `destroy()` cancels and leaves I-7 without a subject. The one new obligation is a **cancel at `acquireLift`**, because an animation outranks inline styles in the cascade. **The release-before-terminal ordering is unchanged, and it is the invariant that decides T1 against T2.**

**Three gains that are not size**: an interrupted interpolation is not a failure, so `FAILURE_LANDING_INTERRUPTED` and `FAILURE_LANDING_CREATE` lose their producers and a cosmetic fault can no longer reach a semantic result; the pin and the animation cannot disagree **by construction**; `destroy()` settles sooner.

**One statement is owed the consumer**, since a WAAPI effect outranks a consumer `transform` write: _the terminal means the semantic transaction is complete and the DOM is the consumer's again; it does not mean every pixel has stopped moving._

**`landing()` survives as a policy object** — duration, easing, reduced-motion collapse — and stops being a lifecycle participant. A CSS-transition variant is **declined on ownership, not rendering**: it leaves residue something must remove, which is the obligation T2 has none of.

**The price, stated rather than buried**: the tail animates **in flow**, so it can be occluded and scrolls with the list. Staying in the top layer means holding the popover, which is T1. **If that is rejected the gate is right — and the recorded reason becomes _the top layer is a lease and a lease needs an operation_**, which is better than the reason recorded today.

**F-187**: an accepted free drag holds the controller inert 200 ms to animate **zero distance** — `anchorTarget` returns the current position, `DEFAULT_DURATION` is flat, nothing short-circuits a zero delta. Dissolved by D-155 as a side effect, recorded separately because it is a defect under any decision that keeps the gate. **F-188**: the sound form was reachable only by naming the unsound one — the check is _what would this hold after the terminal?_, never _how long would this last?_

**Q-L1's contract is repurposed, not resumed.** Arm A becomes **A′**, D-155's removal floor; **A″** adds the tail back so the net is a measured number rather than a subtraction. Arms B and C are withdrawn. **The measurement is not a gate on D-155** — the contract is settled on the lifecycle argument and the numbers inform sequencing.

### D-155's tail narrows to additive `translate`, 2026-08-29 (F-189, F-190)

**Amendment, decision untouched** — [`reviews/phase-23/ql2-tail-additive-translate-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/ql2-tail-additive-translate-claude.md). The owner asked whether the tail could use the same additive displacement `layoutAnimation()` uses, instead of claiming `transform`. **It can, and the package already argued the case for the other feature.**

**Every clause of `layoutAnimation`'s own comment transfers.** `transform` _replaces_ an authored `rotate()` and overrides a consumer's running transform animation; additive `transform` concatenates, so the offset lands **inside** the element's own `scale()` while the delta was measured in viewport space; `translate` applies before `transform` in the used-value chain, so it sits **outside** it and needs no correction, and `composite: 'add'` composes with an authored `translate` rather than clobbering it. **A third hazard is the tail's alone**: `LIFT_FAITHFUL` writes `transformOrigin: 0 0`, and a translate is **origin-independent** where an inverse `transform` would need an origin correction derived from a restored value.

**No geometry is reopened.** The delta is `from − target` — both origin-relative viewport deltas the kernel already holds at the join and already hands to `LandingContext` — and `targetX === null` simply installs no tail. **The trap is `session.compose`** (F-189): its projection is `inheritedSpace` for `LIFT_IN_PLACE` and **`null` for both lifted modes**, correctly, because a `fixed` element's local space _is_ viewport space — while the **released** element is in flow in every mode. Reusing it would be right in one mode and silently wrong in two, by an ancestor scale, so it would vanish on an untransformed page. The conversion is `InheritedSpace` applied directly, which is what `compose`'s in-place branch and free drag's `buildGeometry`/`buildRequest` already compute and publish as `localDeltaX`/`localDeltaY`. **One addition owed**: `inheritedSpace` is published to the activation scope and retained nowhere, so the join must keep it.

**The owed contract statement narrows correspondingly**, and the library no longer overrides anything the consumer wrote: for a bounded interval after the terminal it may contribute an **additive `translate`** that claims no inline style, does not touch `transform`, composes with an authored `translate` or a consumer animation on the same property, ends at a zero contribution, and is abandoned on removal, replacement or the next drag.

**The reparenting row is corrected** (F-190). D-155 called it _harmless_, which merged two claims. **Resource safety is unconditional** and ancestry-independent — no lease survives the terminal. **Visual continuity is best-effort** and is forfeited by an ancestry change, since the value is local and derived through the `inheritedSpace` captured at activation. The failure is bounded and self-correcting: the tail ends at a zero contribution, so **however wrong the path, the element ends exactly where flow puts it.**

**Two things flagged for implementation rather than assumed**: whether `inheritedSpaceOf` folds ancestor zoom — whatever it folds is already what this package publishes as _local_, so the tail inherits an existing definition and a zoom fixture should pin it either way; and whether the **pin is still load-bearing when a tail is installed**, since pin and release now share one task with no paint between and the style restore undoes the write. The pin is certainly not redundant on the no-tail path, and per the owner's instruction it is **not reopened**.

### 2026-08-29 — Ordinary sortable displacement reassessed under a stable-slot premise (D-156)

An owner-level premise change: the supported layout model for `x()`, `y()` and `xy()` is a **permutation of stable, interchangeable geometry**, not arbitrary CSS layout. Asked whether the current `measure -> release -> mutate -> rebuild -> measure -> FLIP` model is more general than those three rules require. Record [`sortable-slot-geometry-claude.md`](reviews/phase-23/sortable-slot-geometry-claude.md); decision **D-156**, findings **F-191** through **F-195**.

The predictive half is stronger than proposed: **every target position an ordinary move produces is already in the packed cache, one index away**, because `RectIndex` is indexed by destination slot and the destination view's relative order is fixed for the whole operation. Deltas are differences of two cached rects, available in both coordinates before the DOM write; the post-move cache is a rotation. The lagging-placeholder half is **rejected** — it makes the library hold a `translate` on every displaced row for the whole drag, which is D-155's argument run backwards, and what it buys is bought anyway.

Two supporting facts that were not known before the pass: `layoutAnimation()` composed with `xy()` already animates only the vertical component (F-191), so no correct general behaviour is being given up; and the committed semantic result is always measured at release (F-193), which is why a predictive cache is a presentation risk rather than a correctness one. The largest result is runtime rather than size — one derivable forced layout per spatial frame (F-195).

No production code touched.

---

## Phase 24 — Self-containment

**The bar, stated concretely.** As genuinely complete and self-contained as `@ydinjs/box-quad`: one coherent surface, no probe framing, no open questions carried in the docs, tests that pin the declared API and not just its behavior, a size budget that fails CI, and nothing a reader has to consult a plan document to understand.

**Deliverables.**

- **`packages/drag2/DESIGN.md`** — the architecture that ships, in the package, the way `packages/drag/DESIGN.md` does for the shipped one. The contract set stays where it is: it is the normative source and the record of how the decisions were reached, and it is not a substitute for a document a maintainer can read start to finish.
- README, typedoc entries and `files.json` final and accurate.
- The demo corpus complete — every ledger-retained capability demonstrable — and, given that the stories are the only consumer surface this package has, treated as a tested artifact rather than as documentation.
- The ledger closed: every row _retain_-and-done, _redesign_-and-done, or _drop_-with-justification. No row in flight.
- Open questions: none carried. Q-4 is closed by Phase 16; anything else still open at this point is either answered or converted into a documented, deliberate limitation.

---

## Phase 25 — Cutover

**Not started until Phase 24 exits.**

- Rename `@ydinjs/drag2` → `@ydinjs/drag`, or fold the tree in and delete the old package. Drop `private: true`.
- Move the story corpus; delete the shipped package's stories and tests, or retain specific ones as an oracle by explicit decision.
- Root docs, `.agents/docs/architecture.md`, and this directory's `README.md`.
- **There is no consumer migration.** Nothing in the workspace imports `@ydinjs/drag`. If that changes before cutover, the new consumer becomes part of Phase 24's demo corpus.

Probe E: eight of ten cases release-blocking, **six with no drag activating**. D-46 withdraws the unconditional admission call and **D-54 relocates it to activation**, adding the trailing-`click` suppressor and the pre-threshold selection clear that moving it later makes necessary; C-03 relocated a defect without inspecting it |
| Checkpoint D (C2-01 → C5-03), 05 §F-47 | The terminal barrier discharged by provisioning + a five-act floor + a register of named ceilings, swept across 62 stretches | **D-37**: the quantifier ranged over a set L-12 itself says "does not terminate as a list". The domain becomes the finite declared-slot / admission / publication set. Every landed reading stays; the ceiling register and the stretch table stop being normative |
| contract 01 §Teardown | "Physical release completes before `destroy()` returns" | **D-36**: logical closure is immediate and terminal, physical teardown defers to the outermost transaction boundary, `destroy()` returns `Promise<void>`. The seven steps and their wrapping are unchanged |
| ledger §2.1, plan Phase 14 | "The **one** breaking public change" — `ResolutionOptions` plus `controller.ready(request)` | **D-41** deletes both. The breaking change was made and unmade inside one Part; what a consumer writes instead is an `await` inside `onReorder` |
| contract 06, I-17 | "`activation.rollback` is unnecessary / vacuous for the sortable behavior" | **D-39**: `prepare` completes but is never _adopted_, so the `effect`-registered disposer never becomes responsible. Deferred teardown changes when teardown happens, not whether adoption occurred |
| contract 03 §`placeholder()` | The placeholder is "sized from the visual's offset box" | **D-43**: that is the visual's box, not the footprint it removed. Two windows, `boxPre − boxPost`. api-1 measured 62 − 32 = 30 against a visual of 60; C1 reproduced a list running 30 px too tall for a whole drag |
| contract 03 §Closed for real, D-30 | Third-party feature authoring closed by an opaque brand | **D-45**: the coherent third state D-30 could not find is a public stable _schema_ whose _installer values_ are opaque. Config authoring opens to ordinary object literals; installer authoring still needs the kernel entrypoint. **D-55** then removes the behavior brand for the same species of reason — D-48 leaves it with no producer |
| Checkpoint D parity D2, ledger L-10 | Insertion candidates measured as `visual` | **D-58**: measured as `box`. D2's _reasoning_ selects it — the incumbent placeholder and its challengers must be measured on one rect — and D2 only chose `visual` because no `box` existed. `box === visual` by default, so the common case is unchanged |
| contract 03, D-45 | `callbacks()`, `handle()`, `visual()`, `placeholder()` as fragment factories on their own subpaths | **D-56**: plain config keys; three subpaths deleted. Under D-45 they were identity wrappers returning a public config slot, and a subpath carrying no runtime machinery measures nothing — which was the stated reason those subpaths existed |
| plan Phase 9, an intermediate D-48 wording | `drag.js` becomes the kernel entrypoint | **D-48, corrected before implementation**: `drag.js` stays the shared vocabulary, a new `kernel.js` carries `draggable()` and the authoring types. Caught from the ledger, by asking whether a shipped consumer could still write its own `onError` handler without reaching somewhere new |

# Reference

## Dependency graph

**Part I — the vertical-sortable slice** (complete):

```text
0 ─▶ 1 ─▶ 2 ─▶ 3 ─▶ 4 ─▶ 5 ─▶ 6 ─▶ ⓐ ─▶ 7 ─▶ 8a ─▶ ⓑ ─▶ 8b ─▶ 9 ─▶ 10 ─▶ 11
               │                  │                 │
               └ frozen SPI gate  └ Checkpoint A     └ Checkpoint B
                                                       + Q-7 / M-4 gate (in 8b)
```

Phases 1 and 2 are independent of each other and can run in parallel. Everything from 3 onward is sequential: the driver is the substrate for the lifecycle, the lifecycle for the behavior, the behavior for the features.

The two checkpoints sit at the points where the next phase multiplies the cost of a change. **A** reviews the kernel machine while only one behavior depends on it; **B** reviews the composition model while only two features depend on it, and before any WAAPI or FLIP code exists.

**Part II — from slice to package**:

```text
                    ┌ 13a keyboard ┐
11 ─▶ 12 ledger ─▶ ─┼ 13b gate     ┼─▶ 14 revise ─▶ ⓒ ─▶ 15 ─▶ 16 ─▶ 17 ─▶ ⓓ
                    └ 13c 2nd beh. ┘

      ┌ A  bracket ┐
ⓓ ─▶ ─┼ C1 commit  ┼─▶ Revision 2 ─▶ R impl ─▶ 18 ─▶ 19 ─▶ 20 ─▶ ⓔ ─▶ 21 measure
      ┼ E  input   ┤
      └ api-1 box  ┘

21 ─▶ 22 refine ─▶ 23 final review ─▶ 24 ─▶ 25 cutover

ⓒ contract re-freeze   ⓓ sortable complete   ⓔ cross-behavior evidence
```

The three Phase 13 probes are independent and can run in parallel; **all three gate Phase 14**, which is the point — the contract reopens once, not three times.

**The four Revision 2 probes have the same shape and the same rule.** They are independent, they ran in parallel, and all four gate the revision — which reconciles once, against everything, and is implemented once, at Phase R. The difference is what they gate: Phase 13 gated an **SPI** change and was compelled to by 00's admission rule; Revision 2 gates a **public-surface** change and batched voluntarily, because the re-verification cost argument holds whatever the subject. Phase R sits before 18 for one reason: free drag inherits the controller shape, the config model, the teardown model and the liveness domain from it.

Everything from 14 onward is sequential, with three review points chosen for the same reason A and B were. **C** reviews the contract revision before anything is built on it. **D** closes the sortable while the kernel still has one behavior, so a sortable-shaped leak into the kernel is still cheap to remove. **E** is the first moment the behavior-agnosticism claim is checkable at all, and it precedes refinement so that refinement is not spent on an architecture that has not been verified.

## Risks carried into implementation

| Risk | Contract ref | Handling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Duplicate full-list layout reads dominate the frame | Q-7 | Blocking gate in Phase 8b, after Checkpoint B has frozen the composition model |
| Closure-per-controller cost | F-4 / M-2 | Measured; the swap is mechanical and semantics-free |
| Generic frame copy on the move path | F-24 / M-1 | Measured; a specialized path needs an equivalence check |
| `resetFramePart` exhaustiveness | F-11, I-28 | `__DEV__` heuristic only; known and unsolved in both probes |
| Keyboard sorting needs a lifecycle transition | Q-4, 02 §`ActionTransition` | **Scheduled**: probed in 13a, revised in 14, implemented in 16 |
| Consumer breaks I-25 (unmounts the dragged item) | Q-12 | **Closed in Phase 10: the guarded fallback is sufficient.** Judged by `tests/sortable/react.browser.test.ts`; written up in `tests/COVERAGE.md` §Q-12 and 05's resolved table |

## Risks carried into Part II

| Risk | Handling |
| --- | --- |
| The kernel is behavior-agnostic only on paper | 13c probes it as a type fixture; Phases 18–20 build the second behavior; **Checkpoint E** is where the claim is settled, and it gates refinement and cutover |
| Reopening the frozen SPI more than once | All three pressure points are probed before any revision (13), revised together (14), re-frozen at **C**. **Held through Revision 2**: D-36…D-60 change the consumer surface and the liveness proof domain, and cross into the SPI **twice**, both with named evidence — **D-53**, one added `KernelHost` liveness reader forced by D-38 and evidenced by probe A; and **D-59**, a widened `admit` return evidenced by api-1 through D-43's two-window chain. That is the rule working, not an exception to it. D-41 touches a Phase-14 SPI revision and is a **deletion**. **The count itself is a carried risk**: it was asserted as zero, then as one, and corrected to two by reviewers each time — see §Revision 2 |
| The settlement protocol redesign loses what the current shape buys | 13b prices any candidate against gate independence and the render/landing overlap before one is chosen. **Re-priced at Revision 2**: D-41 deletes readiness outright, so gate _independence_ has nothing left to be independent of, and the render/landing overlap is replaced by a serial order in which the render happens before the authoritative measurement rather than beside the landing. The property probe 1 actually needed — no fake asynchronous work when landing is absent — is a default-open rule and survives untouched |
| **The consumer surface is reviewed only as a consequence of the construction model** | Revealed by Revision 2 and recorded so it is not repeated: three review rounds and four probes on the consumer surface found a release-blocking input policy, a silent destructive-commit failure, a placeholder 30 px wrong for whole drags and an undischargeable liveness ceiling — none of which eight Part I / Phase 14 review passes had surfaced, because they were reading the surface as an output of the SPI rather than as the thing a user writes. Phase 22's API deliverable and Phase 23's finalization review are where the habit has to hold |
| **Revision 2 lands unimplemented** | The whole of D-36…D-67 is contract-only until **Phase R**, exactly as Phase 14 was until Phase 15. `src/` implements the pre-Revision-2 consumer surface, so a green suite is a baseline and not evidence for any of it; Phase R gates Phase 18 for that reason |
| A new subpath silently invalidates the size measurement | The 2-D axis (17) and free drag (18) each decide a topology change; whichever they land triggers the M-3 reopening rule 05 already states, and Phase 21 re-declares the whole graph half. **Revision 2 moves four entries at once** — D-56 removes three, D-48 adds `kernel.js`, 9 → 7 — so Phase R triggers the reopening rule on its own and Phase 21 inherits a graph that has already changed under it |
| M-1's 12→16 field cliff, at a second part shape | Recorded as a watch item in 19 and measured in 21 rather than assumed to inherit the sortable's number |
| No external consumer validates either package | Acknowledged rather than mitigated: the demo corpus is treated as a deliverable in 17, 20 and 24, and Phase 11's lift-mode regression is the standing evidence that a demo catches what the suite does not |
| A capability is dropped by omission rather than by decision | The Phase 12 ledger is the authority, and Phase 24 does not exit with a row in flight |
